Balls / knowledge report

Overview

What the project is

  • C++20 colour brick-breaker that keeps as many balls on screen as the machine carries at 60 FPS: thousands to hundreds of millions.
  • Rules: one engine-free library, balls_core.
  • Physics: plugins loaded at run time. CPU: scalar, AVX2, AVX-512. GPU: CUDA, Vulkan compute, ROCm. Composers split balls across devices.
  • Front ends: SDL3 (Windows, Linux, Android), SDL on WebAssembly + WebGPU, Unreal 5.8, Unity (own Burst backend).

How it fits together

  • Layers. Front end → core → plugin interface. source/core includes no SDL, Unreal or Unity header.
  • Written once. Menus, layout, editor tools, control API and toasts are core models; front ends only draw them.
  • Hardware at run time. One build carries every backend; a missing driver is a reason string in a menu, never a crash.
  • A frame. UI thread reads the latest SessionView and posts commands. Physics thread (SessionRunner) runs GameSession::update: spawn, tick (sync board, step, resolve hits, remove balls), publish.
  • Plugins. One interface, PhysicsBackend.h, loaded via DynamicLibrary. BallStepCore.h is shared by scalar and CUDA; SIMD and shaders mirror it, so every backend clears a board the same way.
  • Scale. 8 bytes per CPU ball; AVX2 steps 8 balls per instruction in 2,048-ball chunks; GPUs one ball per thread; past one ball per pixel, rendering draws a visibility grid.
  • Proof. balls_tests gates every backend against ReferenceBackend (double precision, no shared code; 0.02 cells mean, 0.06 worst, 600 ticks). balls_bench --accuracy compares whole runs. The HTTP control API (/play, /state, /screenshot) drives every front end.

Numbers

  • Lines: core 15,078 · plugins 9,991 · SDL 11,063 · Unreal 8,125 · Unity C# 13,305 · Unity C ABI 3,051 · tests 3,953 · tools 2,934.
  • ~780 commits on main, ~150 merges of short feature/, fix/, build/ branches.
  • Plugin interface v5; Unity C ABI v7.
  • CPU ball (CompactBallStore.h): x, y as 16-bit field fractions; two 13-bit direction components, colour (3 bits), bounce count (2 bits).
  • Step chunk: AVX2 2,048 balls, Zen4 16,384. Frame budget: free flight 85% of 60 FPS, stepping 50%.

Performance work

Brick hits on the GPU without compare-and-swap storms

Subtract-then-refund atomics and warp aggregation prevent serialized retries when thousands of balls hit the same brick.

Source: commit 81a04aaa3, source/backends/cuda/BallKernels.cu

source/backends/cuda/BallKernels.cu lines 148-177
// A ball takes a hit by subtracting first and refunding when the brick had none left, which gives out exactly the
// brick's hits without a compare-and-swap loop. When a launch wave reaches a row of bricks, hundreds of thousands of
// balls hit the same few in one tick; their CAS retries serialized into 150 ms steps. Lanes of a warp hitting the same
// brick also share one atomic where the GPU can group them.
// Avoids CAS retry storms taking 150 ms (CUDA) and 57 ms (Vulkan) on a 16x16 board (commit 81a04aaa3).
#if defined(BALLS_RANK_WITH_ATOMICS)
__device__ __forceinline__ bool consumeBrickHit(int* brickHitsLeft, int brick) {
    // Only threads observing positive prior counts retain hits; excess hits get refunded.
    if (atomicSub(&brickHitsLeft[brick], 1) > 0) {
        return true;
    }
    atomicAdd(&brickHitsLeft[brick], 1);
    return false;
}
#else
__device__ __forceinline__ bool consumeBrickHit(int* brickHitsLeft, int brick) {
    // Warp-level matching aggregates peer hits, dropping step latency from 150 ms to 1 ms (Volta+).
    const unsigned int peers = __match_any_sync(__activemask(), brick);
    const int lane = static_cast<int>(threadIdx.x) & 31;
    const int leader = __ffs(peers) - 1;
    const int wanted = __popc(peers);
    int granted = 0;
    if (lane == leader) {
        const int previous = atomicSub(&brickHitsLeft[brick], wanted);
        granted = max(0, min(previous, wanted));
        if (granted < wanted) {
            atomicAdd(&brickHitsLeft[brick], wanted - granted);
        }
    }
    granted = __shfl_sync(peers, granted, leader);
    return __popc(peers & ((1u << lane) - 1u)) < granted;
}
#endif

The visibility grid and cache-line bouncing

Filtering rasterization writes to empty slots stops cache-line bouncing, cutting 9M-ball frame times from 36 ms to 4 ms.

Source: commit 24d0da320, source/backends/cpu/Avx2Backend.cpp

source/backends/cpu/Avx2Backend.cpp lines 363-378
// Only an empty slot is written: on a board much bigger than the launch fan, millions of balls stay over a few
// thousand pixels for minutes, and every thread rewriting the same slots bounced their cache lines between cores.
// A filled slot is only read, which cores can share. The grid is cleared every frame, so the first sampled ball of
// the frame keeps the slot, as random a pick as the last.
void storeSlotRelaxed(uint32_t& slot, uint32_t value) {
// Cache lines bounced between cores when 9M balls rewrote empty slots, taking 36 ms (commit 24d0da320).
#if defined(__cpp_lib_atomic_ref)
    std::atomic_ref<uint32_t> shared(slot);
    // Filled slots stay in shared cache state across cores; only empty slots invalidate lines.
    if (shared.load(std::memory_order_relaxed) == emptyRenderSlot) {
        // First ball sampled each frame claims the slot, dropping frame time from 36 ms to 4 ms.
        shared.store(value, std::memory_order_relaxed);
    }
#else
    if (__atomic_load_n(&slot, __ATOMIC_RELAXED) == emptyRenderSlot) {
        __atomic_store_n(&slot, value, __ATOMIC_RELAXED);
    }
#endif
}

Making huge levels playable

Direct id indexing and change tracking keep massive 67M-cell levels playable instead of scaling with total board area.

Source: commits cfc1993f9, acf3d1fb1, 4705f5628

source/core/level/LevelParser.cpp lines 257-276
// Ids are almost always 0..N-1, so a flag per id finds duplicates and unknown ids without hashing 67M of them.
// 1024x1120 (834k bricks) took ~100 s load and 110 ms/tick before scaling with changes.
// Operations on 2048x2240 failed entirely without change scaling (cfc1993f9, acf3d1fb1, 4705f5628).
int largestId = -1;
bool negativeId = false;
// Replaces per-cell linear scans with O(1) dense array or hash lookups.
for (const BrickType& type : layout.types) {
    largestId = std::max(largestId, type.id);
    negativeId = negativeId || type.id < 0;
}
// Dense 0..N-1 ids index directly into a vector without hashing 67M cells.
const bool dense = !negativeId && static_cast<size_t>(largestId) < layout.types.size() * denseIdSlack + denseIdFloor;
if (dense) {
    std::vector<uint8_t> known(static_cast<size_t>(largestId) + 1, 0);
    std::erase_if(layout.types, [&](const BrickType& type) {
        return std::exchange(known[static_cast<size_t>(type.id)], uint8_t{1}) != 0;
    });
    for (int& id : layout.cellIds) {
        // String views cut 4096x4096 parse time from 18.7 s to 3.1 s; snapshot change logs saved 2 s copies.
        // Background flood fills and sparse GPU hit lists avoid full grid scans on massive boards.
        if (id != emptyBrickId && (id > largestId || !known[static_cast<size_t>(id)])) {
            id = emptyBrickId;
        }
    }
    return typeCount - layout.types.size();
}

Fitting the most balls into the available memory

Virtual address reservations and background prefaulting prevent paging stalls when scaling to physical RAM limits.

Source: commit 671e1cb82, source/backends/cpu/ReservedArray.h, source/backends/cpu/CompactBallStore.cpp

source/backends/cpu/ReservedArray.h lines 20-36
// An array that never moves: it reserves address space for the whole of physical memory once and commits pages as
// it grows. Growing a std::vector copies into a new block while the old one still exists, which near the memory
// ceiling is exactly the moment that pushes the process into swap; here growth costs only the new pages.
//
// The next granule past the elements in use is committed early and its pages faulted in on a background thread.
// Within the last few gigabytes of free memory Windows can hold a faulting thread for up to a second while it trims
// working sets; that thread is then this one, not the frame adding balls.
// Reserves RAM upfront; std::vector reallocation copies buffers and triggers swap (commit 671e1cb82).
template <typename T>
class ReservedArray {
public:
    ReservedArray() = default;
    ReservedArray(const ReservedArray&) = delete;
    ReservedArray& operator=(const ReservedArray&) = delete;
    ~ReservedArray() {
        // Background thread prefaults pages; near RAM limits, Windows pauses faulting threads up to 1 s.
        settlePrefault();
        // Pinned host RAM fits 8192x8192 in 4 GB VRAM; free RAM sets ball limits (83ea252c4, 0b631930a, 0ef0199cf).
        releaseAddressSpace();
    }

Notable bugs

The hardest bug

A 2D DDA raycast validates line-of-sight to target bricks, preventing balls from collapsing into trapped columns.

Source: commit d11443834, source/core/sim/kernels/BallStepCore.h

source/core/sim/kernels/BallStepCore.h lines 123-156
                                     int paddedWidth) {
    // Targeting occluded bricks trapped thousands of balls in vertical bounce loops (commit d11443834).
    constexpr float unreachable = 1e30f;
    const float toX = static_cast<float>(targetColumn) + 0.5f - x;
    const float toY = static_cast<float>(targetRow) + 0.5f - y;
    int column = static_cast<int>(floorf(x));
    int row = static_cast<int>(floorf(y));
    const int stepColumn = toX > 0.0f ? 1 : -1;
    const int stepRow = toY > 0.0f ? 1 : -1;
    const float deltaX = toX != 0.0f ? fabsf(1.0f / toX) : unreachable;
    const float deltaY = toY != 0.0f ? fabsf(1.0f / toY) : unreachable;
    float nextX = toX != 0.0f ? (stepColumn > 0 ? static_cast<float>(column + 1) - x : x - static_cast<float>(column)) * deltaX
                              : unreachable;
    float nextY = toY != 0.0f ? (stepRow > 0 ? static_cast<float>(row + 1) - y : y - static_cast<float>(row)) * deltaY
                              : unreachable;
    const int cellsToCross = (column > targetColumn ? column - targetColumn : targetColumn - column) +
                             (row > targetRow ? row - targetRow : targetRow - row);
    // 2D DDA raycast accepts heading changes only when the straight line to the target brick is clear.
    for (int crossed = 0; crossed < cellsToCross; ++crossed) {
        if (nextX < nextY) {
            nextX += deltaX;
            column += stepColumn;
        } else {
            nextY += deltaY;
            row += stepRow;
        }
        if (column == targetColumn && row == targetRow) {
            return true;
        }
        int color = -1;
        // Obstructed rays abort heading change; shared across CPU, AVX2, CUDA, and Vulkan (verified by bench).
        if (board.occupant(paddedCellIndex(column, row, paddedWidth), color) >= 0) {
            return false;
        }
    }
    return column == targetColumn && row == targetRow;
}

A crash on one machine only

Intentionally leaking GPU plugins and device contexts at exit prevents crashes from driver unload race conditions.

Source: commit 8033753a8, source/core/sim/BackendFactory.cpp

source/core/sim/BackendFactory.cpp lines 201-213
const PluginModule& queryPlugin(BackendKind kind) {
    // Releasing Vulkan devices after driver exit crashed dual AMD+NVIDIA setups (commits 8033753a8, 9e521886c).
    static std::mutex mutex;
    // Never destroyed: unloading a GPU plugin from a static destructor at exit closes its devices after the
    // drivers' own exit handlers ran, which crashes (seen with RADV and NVIDIA together).
    // Deliberately leaked: letting the OS reclaim memory avoids undefined driver teardown order at exit.
    static auto& cache = *new std::map<std::string_view, std::shared_ptr<PluginModule>>();
    std::lock_guard lock(mutex);
    const std::string_view fileName = backendPluginFileName(kind);
    auto& module = cache[fileName];
    if (!module) {
        module = loadPlugin(fileName, true);
    }
    return *module;
}

A bug from an extreme input

Clamping kill buffer allocations to max kills per step prevents terabyte OOM crashes on high-hit-point boards.

Source: commit 3519d09ee

source/backends/vulkan/VulkanBackend.cpp lines 420-428
// 2,304 bricks with 1B hits requested terabytes of VRAM when sized by total hits (commit 3519d09ee).
BallCount possibleKills = 0;
for (int32_t hits : hostBrickHitsLeft) {
    possibleKills += static_cast<BallCount>(std::max(hits, 0));
}
// A step cannot kill more balls than it moves; clamping to 4M bounds VRAM and defers excess kills.
const uint32_t requiredKills =
    static_cast<uint32_t>(std::clamp<BallCount>(possibleKills, minimumKilledCapacity, maximumKillsPerStep));
const std::vector<int32_t> words = buildCellWords(board);
std::vector<int32_t> hitsWithSentinel = hostBrickHitsLeft;
hitsWithSentinel.push_back(0);

A compatibility problem

Embedding native SASS cubins alongside PTX lets precompiled CUDA kernels run on drivers older than the build toolkit.

Source: commit cbcfff6d0, source/tools/, source/backends/cuda/CudaDriver.cpp

source/backends/cuda/CudaDriver.cpp lines 26-52
std::vector<const BallKernelImage*> imagesToTry(int computeMajor, int computeMinor) {
    if (const std::string forced = forcedImageName(); !forced.empty()) {
        std::vector<const BallKernelImage*> images;
        for (const BallKernelImage& image : ballKernelImages) {
            if (imageName(image) == forced) {
                images.push_back(&image);
            }
        }
        return images;
    }
    // Older drivers fail to JIT newer PTX; ptxgen embeds SASS sm_50..sm_89 plus fallback PTX (commit cbcfff6d0).
    const int deviceVersion = computeMajor * 10 + computeMinor;
    const auto versionOf = [](const BallKernelImage& image) { return image.computeMajor * 10 + image.computeMinor; };
    std::vector<const BallKernelImage*> images;
    for (const BallKernelImage& image : ballKernelImages) {
        // Binary cubins contain machine code that bypasses driver JIT and runs on older drivers.
        const bool binaryRuns = image.computeMajor == computeMajor && image.computeMinor <= computeMinor;
        if (image.binary ? binaryRuns : versionOf(image) <= deviceVersion) {
            images.push_back(&image);
        }
    }
    // Prefers native binary cubins ordered newest to oldest, falling back to PTX JIT only if needed.
    std::ranges::stable_sort(images, [&](const BallKernelImage* left, const BallKernelImage* right) {
        if (left->binary != right->binary) {
            return left->binary;
        }
        return versionOf(*left) > versionOf(*right);
    });
    return images;
}

1 · Architecture and build

Architecture overview

The three layers and their dependencies

Layering the codebase into core, physics plugins, and front ends allows headless testing and interchangeable backends.

Source: CMakeLists.txt, source/core/CMakeLists.txt, source/core/sim/PhysicsBackend.h

source/core/CMakeLists.txt lines 63-85
# balls_core contains rules and level formats without engine headers, enabling headless testing.
add_library(balls_core STATIC ${BALLS_CORE_SOURCES})
target_include_directories(balls_core PUBLIC
    $<BUILD_INTERFACE:${CMAKE_CURRENT_SOURCE_DIR}>
    $<INSTALL_INTERFACE:include>)
target_compile_features(balls_core PUBLIC cxx_std_20)
# The core is static, but hosts link it into shared objects (libballs_unity.so, the physics plugins), so its objects
# have to be position independent or the ELF link fails.
# Static PIC allows shared targets (libballs_unity.so, plugins) to link core without ELF errors.
set_target_properties(balls_core PROPERTIES POSITION_INDEPENDENT_CODE ON)
target_compile_options(balls_core PRIVATE ${BALLS_WARNINGS} ${BALLS_OPTIMIZE})
balls_precompile(balls_core)
target_link_options(balls_core INTERFACE ${BALLS_LINK_OPTIMIZE})
if(EMSCRIPTEN)
    # The backend headers come straight from the source tree, since the scalar backend is linked in rather than loaded.
    target_include_directories(balls_core PRIVATE ${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/source/backends)
else()
    # WorkerPool needs threads and DynamicLibrary loads physics plugins with dlopen on POSIX.
    find_package(Threads REQUIRED)
    # WorkerPool requires threads; DynamicLibrary uses dlopen on POSIX for plugin loading.
    target_link_libraries(balls_core PUBLIC Threads::Threads PRIVATE ${CMAKE_DL_LIBS})
endif()

# C++20 interface target in PhysicsBackend.h decouples core from dynamic physics plugins.
add_library(balls_physics_interface INTERFACE)
target_include_directories(balls_physics_interface INTERFACE ${CMAKE_CURRENT_SOURCE_DIR})
target_compile_features(balls_physics_interface INTERFACE cxx_std_20)

An engine-free core and how platform types are abstracted

Plain C++20 data aggregates decouple the core from engine headers, allowing headless simulation across all platforms.

Source: source/core/ui/Theme.h, source/core/game/FrameTimings.h, source/core/ui/Layout.h

source/core/ui/Theme.h lines 8-15
// The game's "Look and feel" as plain data, typed once for every front end: each converts these to its own color type
// (an SDL Color, an Unreal FColor, a Unity Color through balls_theme_color) instead of copying the numbers.
// Avoids including SDL.h or Unreal CoreMinimal.h, eliminating header bloat and allowing headless tests.
struct ThemeColor {
    // Plain RGBA data mapped by front ends to SDL_Color, Unreal FColor, or Unity balls_theme_color.
    uint8_t r = 0;
    uint8_t g = 0;
    uint8_t b = 0;
    uint8_t a = 255;
};

The plugin interface contract between core and backends

A versioned C ABI contract isolates hardware physics backends into dynamic plugins queried at runtime.

Source: source/core/sim/PhysicsBackend.h, source/backends/cpu/CpuPlugin.cpp

source/core/sim/PhysicsBackend.h lines 15-21
// Standardizes dynamic library export visibility across MSVC and GCC/Clang.
#if defined(_WIN32)
#define BALLS_PLUGIN_EXPORT extern "C" __declspec(dllexport)
#else
#define BALLS_PLUGIN_EXPORT extern "C" __attribute__((visibility("default")))
#endif

// Version 5 ABI contract covers step routines, spawning, GPU UUIDs, PCI IDs, and memory sizes.
inline constexpr int physicsPluginInterfaceVersion = 5;
source/backends/cpu/CpuPlugin.cpp lines 45-67
// DynamicLibrary queries interface version to prevent loading incompatible plugin builds.
BALLS_PLUGIN_EXPORT int ballsPhysicsInterfaceVersion() { return physicsPluginInterfaceVersion; }

BALLS_PLUGIN_EXPORT const char* ballsBackendUnavailableReason(const char* kindName) {
    const std::string_view kind(kindName);
    if (kind == scalarName) {
        return noReason;
    }
    // Runtime CPU feature detection selects optimal backend (Scalar, AVX2, or Zen4).
    if (kind == avx2Name) {
#if defined(BALLS_HAS_AVX2_BACKEND)
        return Avx2Backend::isSupportedByCpu() ? noReason : missingAvx2;
#else
        return avx2NotBuilt;
#endif
    }
    if (kind == zen4Name) {
#if defined(BALLS_HAS_ZEN4_BACKEND)
        return Zen4Backend::isSupportedByCpu() ? noReason : missingZen4;
#else
        return zen4NotBuilt;
#endif
    }
    return unknownKind;
}

Build system (CMake)

The main build targets and how they relate

Centralizing binary outputs in bin/ ensures desktop loaders find dynamic physics plugins colocated with executables.

Source: CMakeLists.txt, source/core/CMakeLists.txt, source/backends/CMakeLists.txt

CMakeLists.txt lines 83-89
# The browser build is a handful of files a static server publishes together, so they land in the build root.
# Dependency graph links static balls_core (with PIC) into front ends, tests, and dynamic plugins.
if(EMSCRIPTEN)
    # Web outputs land in build root for static hosting; desktop outputs go to bin/ for plugin co-location.
    set(CMAKE_RUNTIME_OUTPUT_DIRECTORY ${CMAKE_BINARY_DIR})
else()
    set(CMAKE_RUNTIME_OUTPUT_DIRECTORY ${CMAKE_BINARY_DIR}/bin)
endif()
set(CMAKE_LIBRARY_OUTPUT_DIRECTORY ${CMAKE_BINARY_DIR}/bin)

Important CMake options and their defaults

Desktop builds compile all backends by default, while browser builds disable tools, CUDA, ROCm, and LTO.

Source: CMakeLists.txt

CMakeLists.txt lines 13-42
set(CMAKE_CXX_STANDARD 20)
set(CMAKE_CXX_STANDARD_REQUIRED ON)
set(CMAKE_CXX_EXTENSIONS OFF)

set(BALLS_FRONTEND "sdl" CACHE STRING "Game front end to build: sdl or none")
set_property(CACHE BALLS_FRONTEND PROPERTY STRINGS sdl none)
# The browser build has no native tools, no CUDA driver and no separate plugin files to load: its physics is the
# scalar backend linked in, and wasm SIMD128 is universal there.
# Browser targets use statically linked scalar physics, disabling tools, plugins, and LTO (-msimd128).
if(EMSCRIPTEN)
    set(BALLS_BUILD_TOOLS_DEFAULT OFF)
    set(BALLS_BUILD_UNITY_PLUGIN_DEFAULT OFF)
    set(BALLS_ENABLE_CUDA_DEFAULT OFF)
    set(BALLS_ENABLE_ROCM_DEFAULT OFF)
    set(BALLS_LTO OFF CACHE BOOL "Enable whole-program optimization" FORCE)
    add_compile_options(-msimd128)
    add_link_options(-msimd128)
else()
    set(BALLS_BUILD_TOOLS_DEFAULT ON)
    set(BALLS_BUILD_UNITY_PLUGIN_DEFAULT ON)
    set(BALLS_ENABLE_CUDA_DEFAULT ON)
    set(BALLS_ENABLE_ROCM_DEFAULT ON)
endif()
# Commit 6f2d4054d enables all backends on desktop; plugins require shared CRT (BALLS_STATIC_CRT OFF).
option(BALLS_BUILD_TOOLS "Build tests and benchmarks" ${BALLS_BUILD_TOOLS_DEFAULT})
option(BALLS_BUILD_UNITY_PLUGIN "Build the C ABI plugin the Unity front end loads" ${BALLS_BUILD_UNITY_PLUGIN_DEFAULT})
option(BALLS_ENABLE_CUDA "Build the CUDA physics backend (needs NVRTC at build time)" ${BALLS_ENABLE_CUDA_DEFAULT})
option(BALLS_STATIC_CRT "Link the static MSVC runtime (physics plugins need the shared runtime)" OFF)
# Off by default: it made no measurable difference to the physics (AVX2 972 vs 985 M ball-steps/s, the CUDA work
# runs on the GPU) and redid code generation for the whole program at every link, which made a one-line change
# take 19 s to rebuild instead of 5.
# LTO gave negligible gain (AVX2 972 vs 985 M steps/s) but slowed rebuilds from 5 s to 19 s.
option(BALLS_WHOLE_PROGRAM_OPTIMIZATION "Enable whole-program optimization (/GL /LTCG)" OFF)

Precompiled headers and build throughput

Precompiling common C++20 standard headers saves ~1 s per translation unit on MSVC while skipping ISA-specialized files.

Source: CMakeLists.txt, source/backends/CMakeLists.txt

CMakeLists.txt lines 92-102
# <filesystem> alone are about a second each with MSVC), so they are precompiled once per target.
# Heavy C++20 headers like <format> and <filesystem> cost ~1 s each on MSVC without PCH.
option(BALLS_PRECOMPILED_HEADERS "Precompile the common standard headers" ON)
set(BALLS_COMMON_PCH
    <algorithm> <array> <atomic> <chrono> <cmath> <cstdint> <cstdio> <cstring> <filesystem> <format> <functional>
    <map> <memory> <mutex> <numbers> <optional> <random> <span> <sstream> <string> <string_view> <thread>
    <unordered_map> <unordered_set> <vector>)
# Avx2 and Zen4 skip PCH due to ISA flag conflicts; nvrhi_vk uses a dedicated PCH saving ~9 s per file.
function(balls_precompile target)
    if(BALLS_PRECOMPILED_HEADERS)
        target_precompile_headers(${target} PRIVATE ${BALLS_COMMON_PCH} ${ARGN})
    endif()
endfunction()

    FetchContent_Declare(nvrhi
        GIT_REPOSITORY https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/NVRHI.git
        GIT_TAG 6b96fb03e07539f08327aea76c56d55f1de9d906
        GIT_SHALLOW FALSE)
    FetchContent_MakeAvailable(nvrhi)
endif()

Plugins and runtime loading

How physics plugins are built

Dedicated translation units isolate AVX2, AVX-512, and CUDA kernel compilation from baseline project compiler flags.

Source: source/backends/CMakeLists.txt, CMakeLists.txt

source/backends/CMakeLists.txt lines 23-39
# Compiles Avx2Backend and Zen4Backend with architecture-specific ISA flags on x86/x64.
if(CMAKE_SYSTEM_PROCESSOR MATCHES "^(AMD64|amd64|x86_64|x86|i[3-6]86)$")
    target_sources(balls_physics_cpu PRIVATE cpu/Avx2Backend.cpp)
    target_compile_definitions(balls_physics_cpu PRIVATE BALLS_HAS_AVX2_BACKEND=1)
    set_source_files_properties(cpu/Avx2Backend.cpp PROPERTIES COMPILE_OPTIONS "${BALLS_AVX2_FLAGS}")

    target_sources(balls_physics_cpu PRIVATE cpu/Zen4Backend.cpp)
    target_compile_definitions(balls_physics_cpu PRIVATE BALLS_HAS_ZEN4_BACKEND=1)
    set_source_files_properties(cpu/Zen4Backend.cpp PROPERTIES COMPILE_OPTIONS "${BALLS_AVX512_FLAGS}")
endif()
target_include_directories(balls_physics_cpu PRIVATE ${CMAKE_CURRENT_SOURCE_DIR})
target_link_libraries(balls_physics_cpu PRIVATE balls_physics_interface)
target_compile_options(balls_physics_cpu PRIVATE ${BALLS_WARNINGS} ${BALLS_OPTIMIZE})
balls_precompile(balls_physics_cpu)
# Compiled with /arch:AVX2, which a header precompiled without it cannot be used with.
# /arch:AVX2 and AVX-512 flags conflict with base target precompiled headers.
set_source_files_properties(cpu/Avx2Backend.cpp PROPERTIES SKIP_PRECOMPILE_HEADERS ON)
# Compiled with AVX-512 flags, which a header precompiled without them cannot be used with.
set_source_files_properties(cpu/Zen4Backend.cpp PROPERTIES SKIP_PRECOMPILE_HEADERS ON)
source/backends/CMakeLists.txt lines 53-58
# balls_ptxgen compiles BallStepCore.h and BallKernels.cu ahead-of-time into BallKernelImages.h.
add_custom_command(
    OUTPUT "${BALLS_KERNEL_HEADER}"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND} -E env "PATH=${BALLS_CUDA_SDK_DIR}/bin" "LD_LIBRARY_PATH=${BALLS_CUDA_SDK_DIR}/lib"
            $<TARGET_FILE:balls_ptxgen> ${BALLS_PTXGEN_FLAGS} "${BALLS_KERNEL_HEADER}" ${BALLS_KERNEL_SOURCES}
    DEPENDS balls_ptxgen ${BALLS_KERNEL_SOURCES}
    COMMENT "Compiling CUDA kernels to SASS and PTX with NVRTC")

Finding and loading plugins at run time

DynamicLibrary wraps LoadLibrary and dlopen to load backend plugins colocated with the executable at runtime.

Source: source/core/CMakeLists.txt, source/core/common/DynamicLibrary.h, CMakeLists.txt

source/core/common/DynamicLibrary.h lines 5-26
// Cross-platform wrapper around LoadLibrary (Windows) and dlopen (POSIX).
class DynamicLibrary {
public:
    explicit DynamicLibrary(const std::filesystem::path& fileName);
    ~DynamicLibrary();
    DynamicLibrary(const DynamicLibrary&) = delete;
    DynamicLibrary& operator=(const DynamicLibrary&) = delete;

    bool isLoaded() const { return handle != nullptr; }

    template <typename Function>
    // Resolves versioned C ABI symbols; omitted on Emscripten where scalar physics is statically linked.
    bool resolve(const char* symbolName, Function& target) const {
        target = reinterpret_cast<Function>(findSymbol(symbolName));
        return target != nullptr;
    }

    // Locates plugins colocated alongside the host executable in bin/.
    static std::filesystem::path executableDirectory();

private:
    void* findSymbol(const char* symbolName) const;

    void* handle = nullptr;
};

Compile-time kernel/shader embedding

Compiling and embedding GPU programs (no runtime compile)

Ahead-of-time compiled GPU compute kernels and shaders embed as C++ byte arrays, preventing runtime compilation stutter.

Source: source/backends/CMakeLists.txt, CMakeLists.txt

source/backends/CMakeLists.txt lines 43-59
# Ahead-of-time compilation embeds SPIR-V (DXC), WGSL (naga), and ROCm byte arrays into headers.
if(BALLS_ENABLE_CUDA AND EXISTS "${BALLS_CUDA_SDK_DIR}/include/nvrtc.h")
    # balls_ptxgen links NVRTC to compile CUDA kernels during build instead of requiring runtime SDKs.
    add_executable(balls_ptxgen ${BALLS_SOURCE_DIR}/tools/ptxgen/PtxGen.cpp)
    target_include_directories(balls_ptxgen PRIVATE "${BALLS_CUDA_SDK_DIR}/include")
    target_compile_options(balls_ptxgen PRIVATE ${BALLS_WARNINGS})

    set(BALLS_KERNEL_SOURCES
        "${BALLS_SOURCE_DIR}/core/sim/kernels/BallStepCore.h"
        "${CMAKE_CURRENT_SOURCE_DIR}/cuda/BallKernels.cu")
    set(BALLS_KERNEL_HEADER "${CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR}/generated/BallKernelImages.h")
    set(BALLS_PTXGEN_FLAGS "")
    add_custom_command(
        OUTPUT "${BALLS_KERNEL_HEADER}"
        # Generates SASS for sm_50..sm_89 and PTX for compute_50/75 directly into BallKernelImages.h.
        COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND} -E env "PATH=${BALLS_CUDA_SDK_DIR}/bin" "LD_LIBRARY_PATH=${BALLS_CUDA_SDK_DIR}/lib"
                $<TARGET_FILE:balls_ptxgen> ${BALLS_PTXGEN_FLAGS} "${BALLS_KERNEL_HEADER}" ${BALLS_KERNEL_SOURCES}
        DEPENDS balls_ptxgen ${BALLS_KERNEL_SOURCES}
        COMMENT "Compiling CUDA kernels to SASS and PTX with NVRTC")
    add_custom_target(balls_kernels DEPENDS "${BALLS_KERNEL_HEADER}")

Compiler flags and per-ISA builds

Compiler flags and the per-ISA strategy

Release builds retain debug symbols with full optimizations, applying AVX2 and AVX-512 flags only to specific units.

Source: CMakeLists.txt, source/backends/CMakeLists.txt

CMakeLists.txt lines 60-81
if(MSVC)
    set(BALLS_WARNINGS /W4 /permissive- /utf-8 /wd4324)
    add_compile_options(/MP)
    # Release keeps its optimizations but also writes PDBs, so a crash on a player's machine or in a bench run has a
    # readable stack. /OPT:REF and /OPT:ICF restore the dead-code and folding passes that /DEBUG turns off.
    # /DEBUG:FULL with /OPT:REF /OPT:ICF retains crash PDB symbols without disabling optimizations.
    add_compile_options($<$<CONFIG:Release>:/Zi>)
    add_link_options($<$<CONFIG:Release>:/DEBUG:FULL> $<$<CONFIG:Release>:/OPT:REF> $<$<CONFIG:Release>:/OPT:ICF>)
    set(BALLS_OPTIMIZE $<$<CONFIG:Release,RelWithDebInfo>:/O2 /Oi /fp:fast>)
    if(BALLS_WHOLE_PROGRAM_OPTIMIZATION)
        list(APPEND BALLS_OPTIMIZE $<$<CONFIG:Release,RelWithDebInfo>:/GL>)
        set(BALLS_LINK_OPTIMIZE $<$<CONFIG:Release,RelWithDebInfo>:/LTCG>)
    endif()
    set(BALLS_AVX2_FLAGS /arch:AVX2)
    set(BALLS_AVX512_FLAGS /arch:AVX512)
else()
    # Aggregates here rely on default member initializers for the members they leave out, which -Wextra reports as
    # missing initializers.
    # Silences -Wmissing-field-initializers for C++20 aggregates (commit 206816b5a); tunes Zen4 with AVX-512.
    set(BALLS_WARNINGS -Wall -Wextra -Wpedantic -Wno-missing-field-initializers)
    set(BALLS_OPTIMIZE $<$<CONFIG:Release,RelWithDebInfo>:-O3 -ffast-math>)
    set(BALLS_AVX2_FLAGS -mavx2 -mfma)
    set(BALLS_AVX512_FLAGS -mavx512f -mavx512bw -mavx512dq -mavx512vl -mavx512vbmi2 -mavx512vpopcntdq -mfma -mbmi2 -mtune=znver4)
endif()

How the Web/Emscripten build differs from desktop builds

The Emscripten build compiles into a monolithic WebGPU artifact with statically linked scalar physics and virtual VFS.

Source: source/core/sim/BackendFactoryWeb.cpp, source/frontends/sdl/CMakeLists.txt, CMakeLists.txt

source/frontends/sdl/CMakeLists.txt lines 260-276
# Statically links scalar physics with -msimd128; disables desktop GPU plugins, tools, and LTO.
if(EMSCRIPTEN)
    # The assets are packed into the .wasm's own file system, the physics backend is linked in, and the browser
    # supplies WebGPU through Emscripten's port. ASYNCIFY lets adapter and device requests wait without blocking.
    target_link_libraries(${BALLS_SDL_TARGET} PRIVATE balls_physics_cpu)
    # WebGPU rendering uses Emscripten emdawnwebgpu port with generated WGSL shaders.
    target_compile_options(${BALLS_SDL_TARGET} PRIVATE --use-port=emdawnwebgpu)
    target_link_options(${BALLS_SDL_TARGET} PRIVATE
        --use-port=emdawnwebgpu
        "--shell-file=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/scripts/web/shell.html"
        # Preloads /assets into VFS; ControlServerWebStub replaces TCP sockets due to sandbox limits.
        "--preload-file=${BALLS_ASSET_DIR}@/assets"
        -sALLOW_MEMORY_GROWTH=1
        -sASYNCIFY
        # ASYNCIFY (256 KB stack) suspends execution while waiting for async WebGPU adapter requests.
        -sASYNCIFY_STACK_SIZE=262144
        # _main as well: replacing the list drops emscripten's default, and without it the linker prunes the game.
        -sEXPORTED_FUNCTIONS=_main,_ballsControl
        -sEXPORTED_RUNTIME_METHODS=ccall,UTF8ToString
    )
endif()

Target hardware and design rationale

The hardware that shaped the design

GPU memory bandwidth limits simulation during brick clearing, while RAM capacity limits ball count during free flight.

Source: data/docs/target-hardware.md, source/core/sim/PhysicsBackend.h

data/docs/target-hardware.md lines 31-50
# Profiles span Dell Inspiron 5567 (2C/4T, 16 GB) up to i9-14900K (32T, 64 GB) + GTX 1650 (4 GB VRAM).
### Measured desktop machine

Baseline high-throughput measurements were conducted on a desktop configuration:

| Part | Desktop PC | What it means for the game |
|---|---|---|
# 8-byte ball layout (fixed-point pos + state) generates 16 bytes memory traffic over two fused substeps.
| CPU | Intel i9-14900K: 8 P-cores and 16 E-cores, 32 threads | AVX2 and FMA everywhere; no AVX-512 |
| RAM | 64 GB DDR5 | room for billions of 8-byte balls once they stop being stepped |
| GPU | GTX 1650: Turing TU117, `sm_75`, 14 SMs, 4 GB, 75-82 W board power limit | memory bandwidth bounds the step; the power limit bounds sustained brick-heavy steps |
| Display | landscape monitor | standard landscape layout |

Two limits follow from this hardware, and the design is built around them:

# CUDA achieves 153.8 GB/s (93.7% of 165.9 GB/s copy ceiling); power limits drop clocks 30-40%.
- **While bricks are alive, the GPU's memory bandwidth sets the ball count.** A step reads and writes every
  ball. On an empty board the CUDA kernel moves 153.8 GB/s, 93.7% of the card's measured copy bandwidth
  ([cuda-limits.md](cuda-limits.md), measured on a GTX 1650 in the development PC). Under sustained brick contacts the card also hits its board power limit and lowers
  its clock by 30-40%, so instructions per ball matter too.
# Closed-form free flight ceases per-tick stepping, allowing ball counts to scale to RAM/VRAM capacity.
- **Once the board is cleared, memory size sets it.** A ball that only meets walls follows a closed-form path and is
  not stepped at all ([free-flight.md](free-flight.md)). From then on the count is limited by how many balls fit in
  64 GB of RAM plus 4 GB of VRAM, not by time.

Build scripts (Windows/Linux)

Root build scripts and front-end dispatch

Root build.bat and build.sh provide uniform CLI flags and dispatch build commands across all front ends.

Source: build.bat, build.sh

build.bat lines 22-40
:: Invoking without arguments defaults to building the standard SDL desktop front end.
set "SETUP_FLAG="
if /i "%~1"=="--setup" (set "SETUP_FLAG=--setup" & shift /1)
if "%~1"=="" (
    call "%ROOT%source\frontends\sdl\build.bat" %SETUP_FLAG%
    goto :finish
)
:: --menu queries available variants; --setup runs frontend dependency checks.
if /i "%~1"=="--menu" goto :menu
if /i "%~1"=="-m" goto :menu
if /i "%~1"=="--help" goto :usage
if /i "%~1"=="-h" goto :usage
if /i "%~1"=="/?" goto :usage
:: Dispatches to front-end build.bat and stages self-contained packages to dist/.
if not exist "source\frontends\%~1\build.bat" (
    echo Unknown front end "%~1".
    call :usage
    cmd /c exit 1
    goto :finish
)
call "%ROOT%source\frontends\%~1\build.bat" %SETUP_FLAG% %2
goto :finish
build.sh lines 20-39
setup_flag=()
if [ "${1:-}" = "--setup" ]; then
    setup_flag=(--setup)
    shift
fi

# Empty arguments run SDL build; test binaries land in bin/ (e.g. balls_tests).
case "${1:-}" in
    "") exec source/frontends/sdl/build.sh "${setup_flag[@]}" ;;
    --help | -h) usage; exit 0 ;;
    --menu | -m) ;;
    *)
        script="source/frontends/$1/build.sh"
        if [ ! -x "$script" ]; then
            echo "Unknown front end \"$1\"." >&2
            usage >&2
            exit 1
        fi
        exec "$script" "${setup_flag[@]}" "${@:2}"
        ;;
esac

Structure of the automated tests

A macro- and throw-based test harness avoids external test dependencies and executes self-contained suites.

Source: source/tests/TestFramework.h, source/tests/TestMain.cpp, CMakeLists.txt

source/tests/TestFramework.h lines 50-65
// Lightweight custom harness avoids third-party test framework dependencies.
#define BALLS_CONCAT_INNER(a, b) a##b
#define BALLS_CONCAT(a, b) BALLS_CONCAT_INNER(a, b)

// TEST_CASE statically registers test functions into a global registry via TestRegistrar.
#define TEST_CASE(name)                                                                                          \
    static void BALLS_CONCAT(testBody, __LINE__)();                                                              \
    static TestRegistrar BALLS_CONCAT(testRegistrar, __LINE__)(name, BALLS_CONCAT(testBody, __LINE__));          \
    static void BALLS_CONCAT(testBody, __LINE__)()

// Formats failure details into an ostringstream and throws TestFailure to abort test cases.
#define EXPECT(condition)                                                                                        \
    do {                                                                                                         \
        if (!(condition)) {                                                                                      \
            std::ostringstream failureStream;                                                                    \
            failureStream << __FILE__ << ":" << __LINE__ << ": EXPECT(" #condition ") failed";                   \
            throw TestFailure{failureStream.str()};                                                              \
        }                                                                                                        \
    } while (false)

Build history (real problems and choices)

The move to one build with every backend

Desktop builds compile every physics backend into one binary tree, replacing static profiles with runtime detection.

Source: git log --format='%h %s%n%b' -n 8 -- CMakeLists.txt (commit 6f2d4054d)

CMakeLists.txt lines 19-37
# The browser build has no native tools, no CUDA driver and no separate plugin files to load: its physics is the
# scalar backend linked in, and wasm SIMD128 is universal there.
# Removed BALLS_PROFILE to compile all backends in one desktop build (commit 6f2d4054d).
# Target renamed to win32-sdl; baseline shifted to 2-core laptop and i9-14900K + GTX 1650.
if(EMSCRIPTEN)
    # Commit 5005cd283 rebuilt web: disabled GPU backends, embedded assets in VFS, and added HTML shell.
    set(BALLS_BUILD_TOOLS_DEFAULT OFF)
    set(BALLS_BUILD_UNITY_PLUGIN_DEFAULT OFF)
    set(BALLS_ENABLE_CUDA_DEFAULT OFF)
    set(BALLS_ENABLE_ROCM_DEFAULT OFF)
    set(BALLS_LTO OFF CACHE BOOL "Enable whole-program optimization" FORCE)
    add_compile_options(-msimd128)
    add_link_options(-msimd128)
else()
    set(BALLS_BUILD_TOOLS_DEFAULT ON)
    set(BALLS_BUILD_UNITY_PLUGIN_DEFAULT ON)
    set(BALLS_ENABLE_CUDA_DEFAULT ON)
    set(BALLS_ENABLE_ROCM_DEFAULT ON)
endif()
option(BALLS_BUILD_TOOLS "Build tests and benchmarks" ${BALLS_BUILD_TOOLS_DEFAULT})
option(BALLS_BUILD_UNITY_PLUGIN "Build the C ABI plugin the Unity front end loads" ${BALLS_BUILD_UNITY_PLUGIN_DEFAULT})
option(BALLS_ENABLE_CUDA "Build the CUDA physics backend (needs NVRTC at build time)" ${BALLS_ENABLE_CUDA_DEFAULT})

The MSVC version fix

Injecting VCToolsVersion into generated Visual Studio projects prevents builds from reverting to incomplete defaults.

Source: git log --format='%h %s%n%b' -n 8 -- CMakeLists.txt (commit 1b7e8cb16), CMakeLists.txt

CMakeLists.txt lines 4-11
# A PC can have MSBuild's default MSVC version installed without its libraries (the target PC does). build.bat names
# a complete one in VCToolsVersion for the first configure; the projects keep it, so later builds use it without it.
# cmake --build reverted to incomplete MSBuild defaults without VCToolsVersion (commit 1b7e8cb16).
if(CMAKE_GENERATOR MATCHES "Visual Studio")
    # Injects VCToolsVersion into CMAKE_VS_GLOBALS so subsequent builds use the full toolset.
    set(BALLS_VCTOOLS_VERSION "$ENV{VCToolsVersion}" CACHE STRING "MSVC version the Visual Studio projects build with (empty: MSBuild's default)")
    if(BALLS_VCTOOLS_VERSION)
        list(APPEND CMAKE_VS_GLOBALS "VCToolsVersion=${BALLS_VCTOOLS_VERSION}")
    endif()
endif()

Key facts

  • balls_core is static C++20, no front-end includes, position-independent (source/core/CMakeLists.txt:63-81).
  • balls_core abstracts types (e.g. ThemeColor, layout coordinates, frame timings) to run and test headlessly without GUI/engine dependencies.
  • Physics backends are plugins in source/backends/*, loaded via DynamicLibrary; interface source/core/sim/PhysicsBackend.h:149-207, interface version 5.
  • To add a new backend: implement PhysicsBackend, export C ABI functions, link balls_physics_interface, and register in BackendFactory.cpp.
  • Windows plugin loader uses shadow copies (%TEMP%/balls/physics-plugins) so running games do not lock DLL files during rebuilds.
  • GPU kernels/shaders embedded at build time as arrays (CUDA via balls_ptxgen/NVRTC, Vulkan via DXC, ROCm via ROCm clang, SDL shaders via DXC).
  • Per-ISA: AVX2/Zen4 compiled with specific flags and skip PCH (source/backends/CMakeLists.txt:23-39); runtime cpuid dispatch.
  • PCH precompiles 25 standard headers via balls_precompile; NVRHI precompiles vulkan.hpp saving ~9s per file (CMakeLists.txt:90-101, 131-136).
  • Emscripten: scalar backend linked in statically, BackendFactoryWeb.cpp, assets in wasm FS via --preload-file, SIMD128 (source/core/CMakeLists.txt:53-62, CMakeLists.txt:21-29).
  • Unity uses balls_unity (C ABI) staging plugins to balls_plugins/; Unreal integrates via modules.
  • Root build.bat/build.sh dispatch to source/frontends/*/build.*; --menu lists variants.
  • Ball format: 8 bytes (16+16 pos + 32-bit state), 16 bytes traffic/tick; speed not stored.
  • Low-end target: i7-7500U + HD620 + R7 M445 (16GB); desktop baseline i9-14900K + GTX 1650.
  • All runtime binaries in <build>/bin (build root on Emscripten).
  • Default since 6f2d4054d: all backends, backend menu, machine benchmark included.
  • CUDA kernels compiled for sm_50/60/70/75/80/86/89 and PTX for compute_50/75 (no CUDA toolkit needed at runtime).
  • Whole-program optimization off by default (small perf gain, slow rebuilds).
  • balls_core links Threads and dl on non-Emscripten; uses PCH by default.
  • Plugins need shared CRT (BALLS_STATIC_CRT off by default).
  • NVRHI pinned to 6b96fb03e07539f08327aea76c56d55f1de9d906 (CMakeLists.txt:125-129).
  • MSVC version cached via VCToolsVersion into projects (CMakeLists.txt:4-11, commit 1b7e8cb16).

2 · The core game

Game rules and game modes

Core gameplay rules, ball dimensions and win conditions

Balls bounce off walls and matching bricks until cleared; Performance mode spawns indefinitely without a win condition.

Source: source/core/level/GameplayRules.h, data/docs/gdd.html

source/core/level/GameplayRules.h lines 9-19, condensed
struct GameplayRules {
    // Ball is half a cell across; exactly four balls fit inside one 1x1 brick cell.
    float ballRadius = 0.25f;
    // Matching hits destroy ball and damage brick; non-matching colours bounce without damage.
    float ballSpeed = 14.0f;
    int ticksPerSecond = 120;
    int maxTicksPerFrame = 4;
    // ... after this many bounces a ball heads for the nearest brick of its color.
    // Targets a visible brick to prevent infinite loops; game has no loss condition (Ready, Running, Won only).
    int retargetBounces = 3;
    float launchHalfAngleDegrees = 55.0f;
    float normalSpawnRate = 60.0f;
};

Normal and Performance modes: mechanics, launchers and limits

Normal mode uses manual launches to clear bricks; Performance mode streams all colours to stress-test throughput.

Source: source/core/game/GameSession.cpp, source/core/game/GameSession.h, data/docs/gdd.html

source/core/game/GameSession.cpp lines 87-97
bool GameSession::chooseLauncher(int column, int row) {
    // Normal mode has >=3 colours; launcher balls equal brick hits. Clearing shows Win overlay.
    // Performance mode has >=7 colours and defaults to Max spawn rate (commit ce31c93be) for high ball counts.
    if (gameMode != GameMode::Normal || sessionState != SessionState::Running) {
        return false;
    }
    // Normal mode clicks release one slot; Performance mode releases all colours at up to 1,000,000 balls/s.
    const auto launcher = launcherQueue.take(column, row);
    if (!launcher) {
        return false;
    }
    // Ball limits: Level empties counts, Auto fills to backend RAM, Max fills to 2^64-1 in swap.
    releaseLauncher(*launcher);
    return true;
}

Gameplay rules configured and parsed at run time

Simulation rules parse from Gameplay.txt with compiled defaults and range checks so tuning needs no rebuild.

Source: source/core/level/GameplayRules.h, source/core/level/GameplayRules.cpp, source/tests/GameplayRulesTests.cpp

source/core/level/GameplayRules.cpp lines 88-106
// Omitted keys retain compiled defaults so tuning requires no rebuild.
Result<GameplayRules> parseGameplayRules(std::string_view text) {
    const SectionMap sections = splitSections(text);
    GameplayRules rules;
    for (const Result<bool>& read :
         // Padded cells * colours <= 0x7FFFFFFF to fit 32-bit signed indexing (up to 306,783,377 total cells).
         {readRule(sections, {"Balls", "Radius"}, rules.ballRadius), readRule(sections, {"Balls", "Speed"}, rules.ballSpeed),
          readRule(sections, {"Balls", "RetargetAfterBounces"}, rules.retargetBounces),
          readRule(sections, {"Balls", "LaunchHalfAngleDegrees"}, rules.launchHalfAngleDegrees),
          readRule(sections, {"Simulation", "TicksPerSecond"}, rules.ticksPerSecond),
          readRule(sections, {"Simulation", "MaxTicksPerFrame"}, rules.maxTicksPerFrame),
          readRule(sections, {"Normal", "SpawnRate"}, rules.normalSpawnRate)}) {
        if (!read) {
            return Error{read.error()};
        }
    }
    if (const std::string problem = validate(rules); !problem.empty()) {
        return Error{problem};
    }
    return rules;
}

Optional ball-vs-ball collisions and the Scalar-only restriction

Ball-ball collisions require the Scalar backend because broadphase hashing for millions of balls would stall GPUs.

Source: source/core/game/GameSession.cpp, source/core/sim/Simulation.cpp, source/tests/SimulationTests.cpp

source/core/game/GameSession.cpp lines 234-247
// Free flight requires disabled ball collisions because inter-ball collisions alter flight paths.
BallCollisionsChange GameSession::setBallCollisions(bool enabled) {
    // GPU/SIMD omit contact because broadphase hashing and resolution for millions of balls kills throughput.
    physicsSimulation.setBallCollisions(enabled);
    if (!enabled || physicsSimulation.ballCollisionsActive()) {
        return BallCollisionsChange::None;
    }
    if (!isBackendSelectable(BackendKind::Scalar)) {
        return BallCollisionsChange::ScalarNotOffered;
    }
    // Switches dynamically to Scalar when enabling; disabling leaves current backend active.
    if (pendingBackend() != BackendKind::Scalar) {
        requestBackend(BackendKind::Scalar);
    }
    pendingBuildForCollisions = true;
    return BallCollisionsChange::SwitchingToScalar;
}

// Front ends run SessionHost on engine ticks while physics steps in a dedicated 120 Hz thread.
void SessionHost::tick(const PhysicsFrameInput& input, float deltaSeconds) {
    // Briefly locks a mutex to pull the latest SessionView snapshot and consume UI events.
    if (runner) {
        runner->tick(input);
        if (auto newest = runner->view()) {
            latest = std::move(newest);
        }
        for (SessionEvent& event : runner->takeEvents()) {
            events.push_back(event);
        // Physics thread backend allocation or compile failures are reported as UI toasts.
        }
        return;
    }

    if (!input.paused) {
        // Processes async backend switches on UI thread so errors dispatch where UI widgets live.
        const PhysicsRate::Clock::time_point workStart = PhysicsRate::Clock::now();
        session->reportFrameWorkSeconds(input.uiWorkSeconds);
        session->update(deltaSeconds);
        rate.frame(workStart, PhysicsRate::Clock::now(), session->simulation().stats().ticksLastFrame);
    }
    refreshView();
}

UI board snapshots without copying millions of bricks

Replaying an incremental change log across double-buffered snapshots avoids 2-second freezes on huge boards.

Source: source/core/sim/BrickBoard.h, source/core/sim/BrickBoard.cpp, source/core/game/BoardSnapshots.cpp, git commit 4705f5628

source/core/game/BoardSnapshots.cpp lines 5-29
// Deep copy took 0.2 s (2048x2048) and 2.0 s (4096x4096); commit 4705f5628 added incremental change log.
std::shared_ptr<const BrickBoard> BoardSnapshots::publish(const BrickBoard& live,
                                                          const std::shared_ptr<const BrickBoard>& previous) {
    const bool previousIsOurs = previous && std::ranges::any_of(boards, [&](const auto& board) { return board == previous; });
    if (previousIsOurs && previous->layoutSerial() == live.layoutSerial() &&
        previous->followedPosition() == live.changeLogEnd()) {
        return previous;
    }
    // Ours is the only reference to a board the UI no longer holds.
    // Selects an unpinned buffer where use_count == 1 from two BrickBoard instances.
    const auto free = std::ranges::find_if(boards, [](const auto& board) { return !board || board.use_count() == 1; });
    if (free == boards.end()) {
        return previous;
    }
    if (!*free) {
        *free = std::make_shared<BrickBoard>();
    }
    // Replays changes since followedPosition; full copy only on layout change.
    (*free)->catchUpWith(live);
    uint64_t oldest = (*free)->followedPosition();
    for (const auto& board : boards) {
        if (board && board->layoutSerial() == live.layoutSerial()) {
            oldest = std::min(oldest, board->followedPosition());
        }
    }
    live.trimChangeLog(oldest);
    return *free;
}

Simulation tick and stepping pipeline

One simulation tick inside Simulation::tick

A simulation tick flushes spawns, syncs board changes, steps physics kernels, and resolves host or device collisions.

// Frame durations exceeding 1/120 s accumulate ticks passed as substeps.
void Simulation::tick(int substeps) {
    flushSpawns();
    const Clock::time_point syncStart = Clock::now();
    // Consumes finished target-field rebuilds and uploads dirty cells or sparse diffs.
    syncBoardIfChanged();
    hits.clear();
    const Clock::time_point stepStart = Clock::now();
    physics->step(stepParams(substeps), hits);
    const Clock::time_point stepEnd = Clock::now();
    elapsedTicks += static_cast<uint32_t>(substeps);
    statistics.lastHostHits = hits.size();
    Clock::time_point deviceHitsEnd = stepEnd;
    // CUDA resolves collisions on device; CPU backends resolve host hits.
    if (physics->resolvesHitsOnDevice()) {
        applyDeviceHits();
        deviceHitsEnd = Clock::now();
        if (!hits.empty()) {
            resolveHits();
            hostHitsPending = !killedBalls.empty();
        }
    } else {
        resolveHits();
    }
    // ...
}

The collision and reflection pipeline in BallStepCore.h

The collision kernel destroys matching hits, reflects non-matching bounces, and steers balls after repeated bounces.

Source: source/core/sim/kernels/BallStepCore.h, source/tests/SimulationTests.cpp

source/core/sim/kernels/BallStepCore.h lines 293-316
// nearBrickCells bitmask skips empty regions before cell testing.
if (bestBrick >= 0) {
    // Matching colour hit destroys the ball and damages the brick.
    if (bestColor == color) {
        return bestBrick;
    }
    const float distance = sqrtf(bestDistanceSquared);
    // Projects the ball outside cell if overlapping closely.
    if (distance > 1e-6f) {
        normalX = bestOffsetX / distance;
        normalY = bestOffsetY / distance;
        x += normalX * (radius - distance);
        y += normalY * (radius - distance);
    } else {
        pushOutOfCell(x, y, normalX, normalY, bestColumn, bestRow, radius);
    }
    reflectVelocity(vx, vy, normalX, normalY);
    bounced = true;
}

if (bounced) {
    // Triggers colour-guided steering once bounces reach retargetBounceThreshold.
    bounces += 1;
    if (bounces >= params.retargetBounceThreshold) {
        retargetTowardNearestBrick(x, y, vx, vy, color, normalX, normalY, board, params);
    }
}
return noBrickHit;

Colour-guided steering after the third bounce

Balls steer toward the nearest matching brick after three bounces, using DDA raycasts to ensure an unobstructed path.

Source: source/core/sim/kernels/BallStepCore.h, source/tests/SimulationTests.cpp

source/core/sim/kernels/BallStepCore.h lines 161-189
                                                  // Guides balls after 3 bounces (retargetBounceThreshold = 3).
                                                  const StepParams& params) {
    const int column = static_cast<int>(floorf(x));
    const int row = static_cast<int>(floorf(y));
    // TargetField returns -1 if no brick of that colour exists, keeping reflection.
    const int target =
        board.targetCell(color, paddedCellIndex(column, row, params.paddedWidth), params.paddedCellCount);
    if (target < 0) {
        return;
    }
    const float targetX = static_cast<float>(target % params.paddedWidth) - 0.5f;
    const float targetY = static_cast<float>(target / params.paddedWidth) - 0.5f;
    const float toTargetX = targetX - x;
    const float toTargetY = targetY - y;
    const float distance = sqrtf(toTargetX * toTargetX + toTargetY * toTargetY);
    if (distance < 1e-4f) {
        return;
    }
    const float directionX = toTargetX / distance;
    const float directionY = toTargetY / distance;
    // Prevents steering back into the surface just bounced off.
    if (directionX * normalX + directionY * normalY < 0.0f) {
        return;
    }
    const int targetColumn = target % params.paddedWidth - 1;
    const int targetRow = target / params.paddedWidth - 1;
    // Amanatides & Woo DDA raycast aborts if foreign bricks block sight.
    if (!targetInSight(x, y, targetColumn, targetRow, board, params.paddedWidth)) {
        return;
    }
    vx = directionX * params.speed;
    vy = directionY * params.speed;
}

The rounding dither on ball positions in BallStepCore.h

Fixed-point ball coordinates use a SplitMix hash dither to eliminate rounding drift deterministically.

Source: source/core/sim/kernels/BallStepCore.h, data/docs/physics-accuracy.md

source/core/sim/kernels/BallStepCore.h lines 97-114
// Whether the straight line from (x, y) to the centre of the target cell crosses no other brick. The nearest
// same-color brick is found by distance alone, so it is often behind other bricks; aiming at it anyway sends the
// ball into the blocking brick, back off the wall behind it, and at the same target again, forever, and every
// ball near that column ends up on the same line. Cells are walked with a grid DDA (Amanatides and Woo).
// Stepped balls are stored back to 16-bit positions every frame, and a frame moves a ball a fixed ~255.4 units,
// so rounding to nearest dropped the same fraction every frame: a steady speed error of up to 0.2% per axis and
// 0.12 cells of drift in 10 s against an exact engine. Adding a pseudo-random fraction before truncating makes the
// rounding unbiased. The fraction comes from a hash of the ball's previous stored position, which every stepper
// has and which changes every frame; low 16 bits for x, high 16 bits for y.
// Truncation caused 0.2% speed loss and 0.12 cells drift in 10 s; dither eliminates bias deterministically.
BALLS_HOST_DEVICE unsigned int roundingDither(unsigned int previousPosition) {
    // SplitMix constants hash 16-bit positions without global RNG state or locks.
    unsigned int hash = previousPosition * 0x9E3779B1u;
    hash ^= hash >> 15;
    hash *= 0x85EBCA77u;
    hash ^= hash >> 13;
    hash *= 0xC2B2AE3Du;
    hash ^= hash >> 16;
    return hash;
}

Brick hits, board settlement and ball removal

Resolving brick hits and filtering redundant hits within a tick

Hit resolution ignores redundant hits on already-destroyed bricks and reverses killed ball indices for safe removal.

Source: source/core/sim/Simulation.cpp, source/core/sim/BrickBoard.cpp

source/core/sim/Simulation.cpp lines 316-342, condensed
// Host-side resolution; device backends resolve hits via applyDeviceHits().
void Simulation::resolveHits() {
    killedBalls.clear();
    bool anyDestroyed = false;
    for (const BallHit& hit : hits) {
        // Ignores redundant hits when multiple balls hit the same brick in one tick.
        const bool wasAlive = brickBoard.bricks()[hit.brick].isAlive();
        if (!wasAlive) {
            continue;
        }
        brickBoard.applyHit(hit.brick);
        anyDestroyed = anyDestroyed || !brickBoard.bricks()[hit.brick].isAlive();
        killedBalls.push_back(hit.ball);
        // ...
    }
    // Descending order prevents back-swaps from shifting lower pending deletion indices.
    if (std::ranges::is_sorted(killedBalls)) {
        std::ranges::reverse(killedBalls);
    } else {
        std::ranges::sort(killedBalls, std::greater<>{});
    }
    physics->removeBalls(killedBalls);
    // Settles unsupported upper bricks when bricks are destroyed.
    if (anyDestroyed) {
        brickBoard.settleColumns();
    }
}

Reversing killed ball indices instead of sorting them

Reversing ascending hit indices in O(N) saves over 1 ms per frame compared to sorting before back-swap removals.

Source: source/core/sim/Simulation.cpp, git commit ebf445077

source/core/sim/Simulation.cpp lines 333-339
// Backends report hits in ball order, so this is usually a reversal rather than a sort.
// Ascending hit collection enables O(N) reverse, saving >1 ms/frame over sorting (commit ebf445077).
if (std::ranges::is_sorted(killedBalls)) {
    std::ranges::reverse(killedBalls);
} else {
    // Fallback sort if non-linear collection leaves indices unsorted.
    std::ranges::sort(killedBalls, std::greater<>{});
}
// AVX2 pairs removed slots with tail survivors across worker threads (for 16,384+ balls).
physics->removeBalls(killedBalls);

Column settling and the visual rise when bricks break

Unsupported bricks rise to fill gaps when lower bricks break, using animated rise offsets to avoid visual snapping.

Source: source/core/sim/BrickBoard.h, source/core/sim/BrickBoard.cpp, source/tests/BrickBoardTests.cpp

source/core/sim/BrickBoard.cpp lines 323-338
// Multi-cell bricks rise only if all spanned columns are unobstructed.
bool BrickBoard::raiseFully(int brickIndex) {
    const int distance = freeRowsAbove(brickIndex);
    if (distance == 0) {
        return false;
    }
    Brick& brick = brickList[brickIndex];
    placeBrick(brickIndex, emptyCell);
    for (BrickCell& cell : brick.cells) {
        cell.row -= distance;
    }
    placeBrick(brickIndex, brickIndex);
    // Interpolates downward in BrickBoard::animate() to prevent visual snapping.
    brick.riseOffset += static_cast<float>(distance);
    startAnimating(brickIndex);
    // Logs modified brick to changeLog for incremental UI snapshot sync.
    logChange(LoggedChange::Brick, brickIndex);
    return true;
}

BrickBoard storage for boards of tens of millions of cells

Single-cell bricks are stored inline, so huge boards load without a heap allocation per brick.

Source: source/core/sim/BrickBoard.h, source/core/sim/BrickBoard.cpp, git commit 756449403

source/core/sim/BrickBoard.h lines 47-57
// Cut 8192x8192 load time from 55 s to 2.5 s across 67M cells (commit 756449403).
void push_back(BrickCell cell) {
    // 1-cell bricks take 16 bytes with zero heap allocations.
    if (!spilled && cellCount == 0) {
        single = cell;
        cellCount = 1;
        return;
    }
    if (!spilled || cellCount == spilledCapacity) {
        // Spills to heap array only when a brick spans multiple cells.
        grow(cellCount * 2 + firstSpill);
    }
    spilled[cellCount++] = cell;
}

Target field: BFS steering and asynchronous rebuilding

TargetField steering guidance per colour

Multi-source BFS floods compute nearest-brick guidance per colour across 8 directions for O(1) kernel steering.

Source: source/core/sim/TargetField.cpp, data/docs/codebase.html

source/core/sim/TargetField.cpp lines 166-189
std::vector<int32_t> frontier;
for (int row = 1; row <= board.rows; ++row) {
    for (int column = 1; column <= board.columns; ++column) {
        const int index = indexOf(column, row);
        const int brick = board.cells[index];
        if (brick >= 0 && board.brickColors[brick] == colorIndex) {
            // 8-way connectivity prevents zigzag artifacts along diagonal trajectories.
            colorTargets[index] = index;
            frontier.push_back(index);
        }
    }
}

const std::array<int, 8> neighborOffsets{-1, 1, -paddedWidth, paddedWidth,
                                         -paddedWidth - 1, -paddedWidth + 1, paddedWidth - 1, paddedWidth + 1};
// Multi-source BFS flood outward from surviving bricks for each colour.
for (size_t head = 0; head < frontier.size(); ++head) {
    const int index = frontier[head];
    for (int offset : neighborOffsets) {
        const int neighbor = index + offset;
        // Provides O(1) runtime guidance per cell and colour.
        if (colorTargets[neighbor] == noTarget) {
            colorTargets[neighbor] = colorTargets[index];
            frontier.push_back(neighbor);
        }
    }
}

Rebuilding the target field asynchronously

Large target fields rebuild asynchronously at lowest thread priority to keep multi-colour floods from starving physics.

Source: source/core/sim/TargetField.h, source/core/sim/TargetField.cpp, git commit 217a044d4

source/core/sim/TargetField.cpp lines 19-33
// Rebuilds run beside the frame; on a big board their seven flood fills took every core for a hundred milliseconds
// and starved the physics threads (a 149 ms Hybrid CPU step). They run at the lowest priority, restored after, since
// std::async may hand the same pooled thread to other work.
// Small boards (<65k cells) rebuild synchronously; large boards run via std::async (commit 217a044d4).
class LowestPriority {
public:
// Prevents 7 colour floods from starving physics workers (avoids a 149 ms spike).
#if defined(_WIN32)
    LowestPriority() : previous(GetThreadPriority(GetCurrentThread())) {
        SetThreadPriority(GetCurrentThread(), THREAD_PRIORITY_LOWEST);
    }
    // Restores priority so pooled threads remain unaffected.
    ~LowestPriority() { SetThreadPriority(GetCurrentThread(), previous); }

private:
    int previous;
#endif
};

TargetField board uploads on large boards

Target fields send sparse diffs under 1/8 board changes, avoiding 40 ms GPU transfer stalls on massive boards.

Source: source/core/sim/TargetField.cpp, source/core/sim/Simulation.cpp, git commits 6ea6f768f, dcc5e0105

source/core/sim/TargetField.cpp lines 97-121
// Runs on the rebuild thread: a 2048x2048 board's field is 146 MB, and sending all of it after every rebuild held a
// frame for 40 ms, while a rebuild usually changes only the cells around the bricks that broke.
// Full 146 MB uploads on 2048x2048 boards stalled frames for 40 ms (commits 6ea6f768f, dcc5e0105).
TargetField::Rebuilt TargetField::computeChanges(const BoardCopy& board,
                                                 std::shared_ptr<const std::vector<int32_t>> previous) {
    Rebuilt rebuilt;
    auto cells = std::make_shared<std::vector<int32_t>>(compute(board, true));
    if (previous && previous->size() == cells->size()) {
        // Below 1/8 total cells sends sparse diffs; above reverts to full DMA upload.
        const size_t limit = cells->size() / wholeUploadDivisor;
        rebuilt.sparse = true;
        for (size_t cell = 0; cell < cells->size(); ++cell) {
            if ((*cells)[cell] == (*previous)[cell]) {
                continue;
            }
            if (rebuilt.changes.size() / 2 >= limit) {
                rebuilt.sparse = false;
                rebuilt.changes.clear();
                break;
            }
            // Compact (cell, target) index pairs keep GPU synchronization under 1 ms.
            rebuilt.changes.push_back(static_cast<int32_t>(cell));
            rebuilt.changes.push_back((*cells)[cell]);
        }
    }
    rebuilt.cells = std::move(cells);
    return rebuilt;
}

Addition governor and frame-budget pacing

AdditionGovernor and frame-stall prevention

AdditionGovernor meters ball additions against spare frame time to maintain 60 FPS without frame drops.

Source: source/core/game/AdditionGovernor.h, source/core/game/AdditionGovernor.cpp, data/docs/codebase.html

source/core/game/AdditionGovernor.cpp lines 55-69
// Holds 60 FPS by halting additions when stepping consumes the budget.
double AdditionGovernor::additionSeconds(BallCount liveBalls, bool freeFlight) const {
    const double frameBudgetSeconds = (freeFlight ? freeFlightBudgetShare : steppingBudgetShare) / targetFramesPerSecond;
    const double chargedOtherSeconds = std::min(otherSeconds, frameBudgetSeconds * largestOtherShare);
    return std::max(0.0, frameBudgetSeconds - chargedOtherSeconds - stepSecondsPerBall() * static_cast<double>(liveBalls));
}

// Divides remaining spare time by step and addition rates.
BallCount AdditionGovernor::allowance(BallCount liveBalls, bool freeFlight) const {
    const double spare = additionSeconds(liveBalls, freeFlight);
    if (spare <= 0.0) {
        return 0;
    }
    const double balls = spare / (stepSecondsPerBall() + secondsPerAddedBall);
    // Caps additions at 2x workload (or smallestScaledBalls) to avoid non-linear scaling error.
    const BallCount most = std::max(liveBalls, smallestScaledBalls);
    return balls >= static_cast<double>(most) ? most : static_cast<BallCount>(balls);
}

Budget shares: active stepping (0.50) and free flight (0.85)

Free flight allocates 85% of frame time for ball spawning, whereas active stepping reserves 50% for collision spikes.

Source: source/core/game/AdditionGovernor.cpp, git commit e7844aed8

source/core/game/AdditionGovernor.cpp lines 8-13
// 60 FPS target floor from design document.
constexpr double targetFramesPerSecond = 60.0;
// The share of 1/60 s the physics frame plans to use; the rest absorbs a frame that costs more than the last one.
// 85% budget (commit e7844aed8, up from 0.75) boosted additions 5% with max frame 15.6 ms.
constexpr double freeFlightBudgetShare = 0.85;
// 50% cushion absorbs collision bursts, settling, and GPU rebalancing.
constexpr double steppingBudgetShare = 0.5;
// Below this many balls a step is mostly fixed cost, so its per-ball cost is taken over at least this many.
// 65,536 balls baseline prevents fixed overhead from dominating per-ball costs.
constexpr BallCount smallestScaledBalls = 1 << 16;

The bug where slow rendering blocked every ball addition

Capping non-physics time deductions at half the budget ensures balls still launch during slow rendering.

Source: source/core/game/AdditionGovernor.cpp, source/tests/AdditionGovernorTests.cpp, git commit cc2684980

source/tests/AdditionGovernorTests.cpp lines 35-42
// Unreal Cinematic took 15-48 ms, freezing additions (commit cc2684980); largestOtherShare = 0.5 caps it.
TEST_CASE("the governor still adds balls when the front end alone draws slower than the budget") {
    AdditionGovernor governor(tickSeconds);
    governor.recordStep(0.0001, 1, 1'000'000);
    governor.recordAdditions(0.0001, 100'000);
    // 20 ms render time previously starved additionSeconds() to 0.
    governor.recordFrame(0.020);
    EXPECT(governor.allowance(1'000'000, true) > 0);
    EXPECT(governor.allowance(1'000'000, false) > 0);
}

Capping additions at double the live ball count

Ball additions are capped at double the live count to prevent cache blow-out when extrapolating linear scaling.

Source: source/core/game/AdditionGovernor.cpp, git commit 1ae4a07ae

source/core/game/AdditionGovernor.cpp lines 61-69
// Additions below 4,096 are ignored to avoid timing measurement jitter.
BallCount AdditionGovernor::allowance(BallCount liveBalls, bool freeFlight) const {
    const double spare = additionSeconds(liveBalls, freeFlight);
    if (spare <= 0.0) {
        return 0;
    }
    const double balls = spare / (stepSecondsPerBall() + secondsPerAddedBall);
    // Caps additions at double live count (or 65,536, commit 1ae4a07ae) to prevent cache blow-out.
    const BallCount most = std::max(liveBalls, smallestScaledBalls);
    return balls >= static_cast<double>(most) ? most : static_cast<BallCount>(balls);
}

Spawning and launcher management

SpawnController: launcher streams and spawn rates

SpawnController meters launcher releases, interleaving balls across active colours in round-robin order.

Source: source/core/game/SpawnController.h, source/core/game/SpawnController.cpp, source/tests/GameTests.cpp

source/core/game/SpawnController.cpp lines 32-57
// Accrues budget from ballsPerSecond (claims all capacity if 0/Max).
BallCount SpawnController::release(Simulation& simulation, BallCount count) {
    BallCount allowance = std::min(count, static_cast<BallCount>(budget));
    const BallCount released = allowance;
    while (allowance > 0 && !streams.empty()) {
        // Divides allowance equally across active streams so colours spawn concurrently.
        const BallCount share =
            interleaved ? std::max<BallCount>(1, allowance / static_cast<BallCount>(streams.size())) : allowance;
        for (auto stream = streams.begin(); stream != streams.end() && allowance > 0;) {
            const BallCount launched = std::min({share, allowance, stream->remaining});
            simulation.launchBalls(stream->color, launched);
            stream->remaining -= launched;
            allowance -= launched;
            pending -= launched;
            stream = stream->remaining == 0 ? streams.erase(stream) : std::next(stream);
        }
    }
    budget -= static_cast<double>(released - allowance);
    // A release whose balls do not cover every stream starts the next one with the stream after.
    // Rotates front stream to back if ending mid-rotation to preserve round-robin fairness.
    if (interleaved && streams.size() > 1) {
        streams.push_back(streams.front());
        streams.pop_front();
    }
    if (streams.empty()) {
        budget = 0.0;
    }
    return released - allowance;
}

High-throughput, deterministic launches in Simulation::launchBalls

Chunked parallel spawning uses SplitMix64 hashing and Taylor approximations for deterministic 2.1 ns launches.

Source: source/core/sim/Simulation.cpp, git commits 83e9b9705, 1f061893e

source/core/sim/Simulation.cpp lines 138-148
// Taylor series replaced std::sin/cos (2.1 ns vs 3.9 ns, commits 83e9b9705, 1f061893e).
// Flushes spawns (20 B vs 8 B stored) in 4M chunks to bound memory overhead.
parallelChunks(batch, launchChunkBalls, [&](size_t, size_t begin, size_t end) {
    for (size_t i = begin; i < end; ++i) {
        // SplitMix64 hashes seed and index for core-count-independent determinism.
        const uint64_t bits = mixBits(seed + (batchStart + i) * launchStride);
        const float angle = (2.0f * unitFromBits(bits) - 1.0f) * halfAngle;
        const float jitter = (2.0f * unitFromBits(bits >> 24) - 1.0f) * radius;
        float sine = 0.0f;
        float cosine = 0.0f;
        // Taylor series accurate to 4e-6 over +-90 deg enables SIMD auto-vectorization.
        launchSinCos(angle, sine, cosine);
        spawns[i] = {originX + jitter, originY, sine * speed, -cosine * speed, colorIndex};
    }
});

LauncherQueue: HUD launcher slots and column rise offsets

LauncherQueue manages column slots and animates extracted launchers upward to prevent visual snapping.

Source: source/core/game/LauncherQueue.h, source/core/game/LauncherQueue.cpp, source/tests/GameTests.cpp

source/core/game/LauncherQueue.cpp lines 24-46
void LauncherQueue::animate(float deltaTime) {
    for (auto& column : columns) {
        for (QueuedLauncher& queued : column) {
            // Interpolates riseOffset to zero at 8.0 rows/s to animate advancing slots.
            queued.riseOffset = std::max(0.0f, queued.riseOffset - riseSpeedRowsPerSecond * deltaTime);
        }
    }
}

// Normal mode extracts clicked slot; Performance mode cycles columns round-robin.
std::optional<Launcher> LauncherQueue::take(int column, int row) {
    if (column < 0 || column >= columnCount() || row < 0 || row >= visibleRowCount) {
        return std::nullopt;
    }
    auto& queue = columns[column];
    if (row >= static_cast<int>(queue.size())) {
        return std::nullopt;
    }
    const Launcher taken = queue[row].launcher;
    queue.erase(queue.begin() + row);
    // Increments riseOffset for behind launchers to animate an upward shift.
    for (size_t behind = static_cast<size_t>(row); behind < queue.size(); ++behind) {
        queue[behind].riseOffset += 1.0f;
    }
    return taken;
}

Free flight closed-form physics

The free-flight optimisation and when it applies

Free flight freezes physical simulation once all bricks clear, sampling closed-form trajectories for rendering.

Source: source/core/sim/kernels/BallStepCore.h, data/docs/free-flight.md

data/docs/free-flight.md lines 25-37
# Triggers when all bricks are cleared and ball collisions are disabled.
## How the backends use it

- `Simulation` passes a tick clock and a `freeFlight` flag in `StepParams`: set when no brick is alive and
  # Freezes on epoch tick E; ball arrays remain untouched during steps.
  ball-vs-ball collisions are off.
- CUDA and AVX2 freeze on the first free-flight step: they record `E` and stop touching the ball arrays.
# Visibility grid sampling takes ~0.05 ms GPU time on GTX 1650 at any ball count.
- A frame only needs the balls that bid for a visibility-grid slot, so whole 256-ball chunks are sampled with the
  bidder stride. The reads stay contiguous and the cost follows the size of the grid, not the ball count:
  about 0.05 ms of GPU time per frame on the GTX 1650 at any count.
- Balls added while frozen (launchers, fills) are projected back to `E` with the quantized direction the
  # New balls reverse-project to epoch E so forward flight matches spawn point.
  kernels decode, so flying forward lands exactly on the spawn point.
- Any normal step, export or transfer between devices first writes every ball back at the current tick
  (`materializeFreeFlightKernel`, `CompactBallStore::materializeRange`), so the rest of each backend never sees
  # Materializes balls back to current tick before physical stepping or backend transfer.
  the epoch form.

The closed-form free-flight path

Free-flight unfolds wall bounces into a periodic linear phase, eliminating numerical drift over infinite bounces.

Source: source/core/sim/kernels/BallStepCore.h, data/docs/free-flight.md

source/core/sim/kernels/BallStepCore.h lines 343-355
// Unfolds triangle bounces into period 2*span; maps 1:1 to 32-bit phase in standard 8-byte records.
BALLS_HOST_DEVICE unsigned int freeFlightPhase(float position, float velocity, float extent, float radius) {
    const float span = freeFlightSpan(extent, radius);
    const float along = clampFloat(position - radius, 0.0f, span);
    const float unfolded = velocity < 0.0f ? 2.0f * span - along : along;
    const float fraction = unfolded / (2.0f * span);
    const float wrapped = fraction >= 1.0f ? 0.0f : fraction;
    // 32-bit fixed-point phase uses unsigned overflow for modulo arithmetic.
    return static_cast<unsigned int>(wrapped * freeFlightPhaseUnits);
}

// phase(t) = phase(E) + advance * (t - E) wraps naturally with zero drift.
BALLS_HOST_DEVICE unsigned int freeFlightAdvance(float velocity, float deltaTime, float extent, float radius) {
    const float perTick = fabsf(velocity) * deltaTime / (2.0f * freeFlightSpan(extent, radius));
    return static_cast<unsigned int>(perTick * freeFlightPhaseUnits + 0.5f);
}
