Memory preallocation and capacity limits

ballCapacityLimit, ballHardLimit and the Auto and Max ball limits

Auto limits ball counts to physical RAM and VRAM, while Max expands into system swap space up to 2^64-1.

Source: source/core/sim/PhysicsBackend.h, source/core/sim/Simulation.cpp, source/core/game/GameSession.cpp, data/docs/free-flight.md

source/core/sim/Simulation.cpp lines 160-165
// CUDA caps at free VRAM minus 256 MB (no paging, Max == Auto); AVX2 uses free RAM minus 1 GB.
// Auto fills to ballCapacityLimit(); Max enables roomPastCapacity up to ballHardLimit() (2^64-1).
BallCount Simulation::ballRoom() const {
    const BallCount limit =
        static_cast<BallCount>(roomPastCapacity ? physics->ballHardLimit() : physics->ballCapacityLimit());
    const BallCount live = liveBallCount();
    return limit > live ? limit - live : 0;
}

Memory preallocation with BALLS_PREALLOCATE=1

With BALLS_PREALLOCATE=1, CUDA resizes its preallocated ball pool around massive boards without failing.

Source: source/core/sim/PhysicsBackend.h, source/tests/PreallocationTests.cpp, data/docs/free-flight.md

source/tests/PreallocationTests.cpp lines 26-45
// BALLS_PREALLOCATE=1 triggers preallocateBalls(); AVX2 clamps on Windows low-memory signals.
TEST_CASE("a preallocated CUDA backend carves its balls around a board too big for the reserve") {
    if (!isBackendAvailable(BackendKind::Cuda)) {
        return;
    }
    // CUDA reserves free VRAM minus 512 MB at 16 B/ball (positions, states, cell-sort buffers) in 1M chunks.
    BackendHandle cuda = createBackend(BackendKind::Cuda);
    const size_t preallocated = cuda ? cuda->preallocateBalls() : 0;
    if (preallocated == 0) {
        return;
    }
    uploadEmptyBoard(*cuda, 30, 38);
    EXPECT(cuda->ballCapacityLimit() == preallocated);
    // 67M cells take about 0.5 GB of cell arrays, more than the whole reserve.
    // 8192x8192 boards allocate 67M cells (~0.5 GB), dynamically carving down ball capacity.
    uploadEmptyBoard(*cuda, 8192, 8192);
    const size_t underHugeBoard = cuda->ballCapacityLimit();
    EXPECT(underHugeBoard > 0 && underHugeBoard < preallocated);
    EXPECT(cuda->faultMessage().empty());
    // Reloading smaller boards restores ball capacity without reallocating device buffers.
    uploadEmptyBoard(*cuda, 30, 38);
    EXPECT(cuda->ballCapacityLimit() == preallocated);
    EXPECT(cuda->faultMessage().empty());
}

Migrating balls between backends in replaceBackend

Migrating balls in 1M chunks bounds transfer pauses when switching backends, refilling excess balls later.

Source: source/core/sim/Simulation.h, source/core/sim/Simulation.cpp, source/tests/SimulationTests.cpp

source/core/sim/Simulation.cpp lines 68-104
// Runs on the physics thread, where it only delays the next tick: the UI keeps drawing the last published frame. Every
// ball is streamed across with its position and velocity, so the new backend continues the same simulation. Only
// when the new backend cannot hold them all are the balls it does not have handed back to be launched again: when it
// says so up front, or when partway through it runs out of host memory, refuses a chunk or faults, as the Vulkan
// backend does when device memory runs out. What it holds is counted, not what was sent.
// 1M chunks (~20 MB) halved peak UI frame time during 100M-ball migration vs 4M chunks.
BackendReplacement Simulation::replaceBackend(BackendHandle backend, size_t chunkBalls) {
    BackendReplacement replacement{std::move(physics), 0};
    physics = std::move(backend);
    uploadedRevision = ~0ull;
    targetsUploadPending = true;
    hitsUploadPending = true;
    syncBoardIfChanged();
    physics->setBallCollisions(ballCollisionsWanted);
    PhysicsBackend& previous = *replacement.retired;
    const size_t total = previous.ballCount();
    // Returns excess in ballsToRefill if target backend cannot hold all balls.
    if (total > physics->ballCapacityLimit()) {
        replacement.ballsToRefill = total;
        return replacement;
    }
    // Aborts on bad_alloc or backend fault (such as Vulkan VRAM exhaustion).
    while (previous.ballCount() > 0 && physics->faultMessage().empty()) {
        const std::vector<BallSpawn> chunk = previous.takeTail(std::min(previous.ballCount(), chunkBalls));
        if (chunk.empty()) {
            break;
        }
        const size_t expected = physics->ballCount() + chunk.size();
        try {
            physics->addBalls(chunk);
        } catch (const std::bad_alloc&) {
            break;
        }
        if (physics->ballCount() != expected) {
            break;
        }
    }
    replacement.ballsToRefill = total - std::min(physics->ballCount(), total);
    return replacement;
}

Fixed vs variable time step and determinism

The fixed time step in Simulation::advance and the spiral of death

Clamping the 120 Hz accumulator to 4 ticks per frame prevents spiral-of-death lockups during frame hitches.

Source: source/core/sim/Simulation.h, source/core/sim/Simulation.cpp, source/core/game/PhysicsRate.h

source/core/sim/Simulation.cpp lines 384-391, condensed
// Fixed 120 Hz; setTicksPerSecond is restricted to run before balls spawn.
void Simulation::advance(float frameSeconds) {
    const auto start = std::chrono::steady_clock::now();
    // Clamps to maxTicksPerFrame (<=4 ticks, 1/30 s) so hitches slow simulation instead of locking the engine.
    accumulator = std::min(accumulator + frameSeconds, settings.tickSeconds * settings.maxTicksPerFrame);
    const int ticks = static_cast<int>(accumulator / settings.tickSeconds);
    accumulator -= static_cast<float>(ticks) * settings.tickSeconds;
    if (ticks > 0) {
        tick(ticks);
    }
    // ...
}

Determinism across thread counts and between CPU and GPU

Index-based hashing and shared math kernels guarantee identical physics trajectories across threads, CPU, and GPU.

Source: source/core/common/ParallelChunks.h, source/core/sim/Simulation.cpp, source/tests/SimulationTests.cpp, source/tests/FreeFlightTests.cpp

source/core/sim/Simulation.cpp lines 132-151
// Tests inject pre-generated ball vectors to verify spatial parity against naive reference engine.
for (BallCount launched = 0; launched < count;) {
    const size_t batch = static_cast<size_t>(std::min<BallCount>(count - launched, spawnFlushBalls));
    const size_t first = pendingSpawns.size();
    pendingSpawns.resize(first + batch);
    BallSpawn* const spawns = pendingSpawns.data() + first;
    const uint64_t batchStart = launched;
    parallelChunks(batch, launchChunkBalls, [&](size_t, size_t begin, size_t end) {
        for (size_t i = begin; i < end; ++i) {
            // Index-based SplitMix hashing decouples ball parameters from thread scheduling.
            const uint64_t bits = mixBits(seed + (batchStart + i) * launchStride);
            const float angle = (2.0f * unitFromBits(bits) - 1.0f) * halfAngle;
            const float jitter = (2.0f * unitFromBits(bits >> 24) - 1.0f) * radius;
            float sine = 0.0f;
            float cosine = 0.0f;
            // Shared math and rounding dither in BallStepCore.h guarantee cross-backend parity.
            launchSinCos(angle, sine, cosine);
            spawns[i] = {originX + jitter, originY, sine * speed, -cosine * speed, colorIndex};
        }
    });
    launched += batch;
    flushSpawnsIfLarge();
}

Error handling with Result<T>

Result<T> in the core: rationale and implementation

Recoverable operations return Result<T> to avoid exceptions and allocate no heap memory on success.

Source: source/core/common/Result.h, data/docs/codebase.html

source/core/common/Result.h lines 12-31
template <typename T>
// Replaces exceptions; Result<void> wraps std::optional<Error> for valueless returns.
class [[nodiscard]] Result {
public:
    // Stores T inline with zero heap allocation on success.
    Result(T value) : storage(std::in_place_index<0>, std::move(value)) {}
    // Error stores human-readable message targeted directly at UI dialogs.
    Result(Error error) : storage(std::in_place_index<1>, std::move(error)) {}

    bool hasValue() const { return storage.index() == 0; }
    explicit operator bool() const { return hasValue(); }

    T& value() { return std::get<0>(storage); }
    const T& value() const { return std::get<0>(storage); }
    T& operator*() { return value(); }
    const T& operator*() const { return value(); }
    T* operator->() { return &value(); }
    const T* operator->() const { return &value(); }
    const std::string& error() const { return std::get<1>(storage).message; }

private:
    std::variant<T, Error> storage;
};

Threading and concurrency within the core

SessionRunner's threading model and view sharing

SessionRunner recycles triple-buffered snapshots on a physics thread, resetting board handles to prevent starvation.

Source: source/core/game/SessionRunner.h, source/core/game/SessionRunner.cpp

source/core/game/SessionRunner.cpp lines 81-99, condensed
// SDL/Unity use SessionRunner directly; Unreal uses SessionHost; Emscripten runs synchronously.
std::shared_ptr<SessionView> freeView(std::array<std::shared_ptr<SessionView>, viewPoolSize>& pool,
                                      const std::shared_ptr<const SessionView>& latest) {
    std::shared_ptr<SessionView> chosen;
    for (std::shared_ptr<SessionView>& view : pool) {
        if (!view) {
            view = std::make_shared<SessionView>();
        }
        if (view.use_count() == 1 && view != latest) {
            // BoardSnapshots has only two buffers, so held boards starve updates.
            view->board.reset();
            if (!chosen) {
                chosen = view;
            }
        }
    }
    return chosen ? chosen : std::make_shared<SessionView>();
}

Other multithreading in the core

A static worker pool avoids thread-creation latency and power throttling during parallel launches and ball removals.

Source: source/core/common/WorkerPool.h, source/core/common/ParallelChunks.cpp, source/core/sim/TargetField.cpp

source/core/common/ParallelChunks.cpp lines 8-20
// One pool for the process, made on first use: the Auto ramp calls this every frame, and a task per core per call
// cost more than the launches it split.
// Static threads sleep on condition variables and opt out of power throttling to stay on P-cores.
void parallelChunks(size_t count, size_t chunkSize, const std::function<void(size_t, size_t, size_t)>& task) {
    chunkSize = std::max<size_t>(chunkSize, 1);
    // Small workloads run inline on the calling thread to avoid pool dispatch overhead.
    if (count <= chunkSize) {
        if (count > 0) {
            task(0, 0, count);
        }
        return;
    }
    // Powers parallel launches and AVX2 ball removals; TargetField runs async BFS floods.
    static WorkerPool pool(std::max(1u, std::thread::hardware_concurrency()));
    pool.runChunks(count, chunkSize, task);
}

Key facts

  • A ball has a radius of 0.25 cells (half a cell across), so four balls fit in one 1x1 brick cell; default speed is 14.0 cells/sec.
  • The fixed simulation rate defaults to 120 ticks per second (tickSeconds = 1/120), with maxTicksPerFrame = 4 (1/30 s catch-up limit).
  • Color retargeting occurs after the 3rd bounce (retargetBounceThreshold = 3), using an Amanatides & Woo grid DDA to check line-of-sight.
  • In Normal mode, total balls across launchers equal total brick hits of that color; clearing all bricks triggers the Win overlay.
  • In Performance mode, the game requires 7+ colors, 2000+ balls, 60 FPS min, and has no win overlay (it runs indefinitely).
  • Performance mode spawn rates include 10, 50, 200, 1000, 2000, 10k, 50k, 200k, 1M, and Max (0), defaulting to Max.
  • Ball limits are Level (launches level counts), Auto (fills to backend physical memory capacity), and Max (fills up to 2^64-1 into paging).
  • AdditionGovernor budgets 50% of 1/60 s during active stepping, 85% during free flight, and caps non-physics time at 50% (largestOtherShare = 0.5).
  • Free flight closed form unfolds wall reflections into a straight line with 32-bit fixed-point phase mod 2^32, guaranteeing zero drift.
  • BrickCells uses small-vector optimization to store 1-cell bricks inline (16 bytes), avoiding 67 million heap allocations on 8192x8192 boards.
  • TargetField uses 8-neighbor multi-source BFS; fields over 65,536 cells rebuild via std::async with THREAD_PRIORITY_LOWEST.
  • SessionRunner shares immutable SessionView snapshots using a triple-buffered pool (viewPoolSize = 3) without locking physics.
  • SessionHost is used exclusively by Unreal to switch between the physics thread and the game thread; SDL and Unity use SessionRunner directly.
  • Simulation::replaceBackend migrates balls in 1,048,576-ball chunks (~20 MB), returning dropped balls in ballsToRefill.
  • Ball launch vectors are generated deterministically across cores in 2.1-2.4 ns/ball using SplitMix64 hashing and a Taylor series sin/cos.

3 · Levels, editor, UI and control

Level file format and parser

The level file format and what makes a playable map

Levels pair a brick file with a launcher file, separating geometry from launch pacing for independent testing.

Source: source/core/level/LevelData.h, data/docs/level-format.md

data/docs/level-format.md lines 1-16
# Level format

# Loads into LevelData (BrickLayout, LauncherLayout, GameplayRules).
Each level is a pair of files in `data/assets/levels/normal` or `data/assets/levels/performance`, in the format these sample levels use:

- `<Name>_Bricks.txt`
  - `[Board] Width / Height / PlayableHeight`: brick rows, plus total rows including the empty play space.
    Without `PlayableHeight`, or with one below `Height`, the board gets a quarter of its brick rows as open space
    below them, at least 7 rows.
  # Skips zero or negative hits and duplicate IDs.
  - `[Layout]`: a grid of brick ids. Cells that share an id form one multi-cell brick. `-1` marks an empty cell.
  - `[BrickTypes]`: one `id Color hits` line per brick.
- `<Name>_SpawnBall.txt`
  - `[Board] Width` (launcher columns) and `Height` (visible rows).
  - `[Layout]`: `Color(count)` or `Empty` per slot.

Colors: Red, Orange, Yellow, Green, Blue, Indigo, Violet.

Repairing level files that stray from the specification

The level parser automatically repairs minor syntax quirks and ragged rows so imperfect community maps still load.

Source: source/core/level/LevelParser.cpp, source/core/level/SectionText.cpp, commit 6c2d193c9

source/core/level/SectionText.cpp lines 43-64
// Tolerant parsing repairs syntax quirks rather than failing (commit 6c2d193c9).
SectionMap splitSections(std::string_view text) {
    SectionMap sections;
    std::vector<std::string_view>* current = &sections[""];
    // Strips leading UTF-8 byte order mark (\xEF\xBB\xBF).
    if (text.starts_with(byteOrderMark)) {
        text.remove_prefix(byteOrderMark.size());
    }
    size_t lineStart = 0;
    while (lineStart <= text.size()) {
        const size_t lineEnd = std::min(text.find('\n', lineStart), text.size());
        const std::string_view line = trim(text.substr(lineStart, lineEnd - lineStart));
        lineStart = lineEnd + 1;
        if (line.empty() || isComment(line)) {
            continue;
        }
        // Section and header names match case-insensitively; accepts 'Key: value', 'Key=value', or 'Key value'.
        if (line.front() == '[' && line.back() == ']') {
            current = &sections[std::string(trim(line.substr(1, line.size() - 2)))];
            continue;
        }
        // Pads ragged rows; treats Empty, '.', '-', '_', and 'x' as empty cells.
        // Missing PlayableHeight defaults to 1/4 brick rows, at least 7 (commit 9b4332d48).
        current->push_back(line);
    }
    return sections;
}

Fatal parse errors and structural size limits

The parser returns Result<T> on fatal faults or oversized boards to prevent integer overflow in distance kernels.

Source: source/core/level/LevelParser.cpp, commit 5e42da351

source/core/level/LevelParser.cpp lines 129-136
// Returns Result<T> without exceptions; front ends abort to FlowScreen::LoadError.
std::string checkBoardSize(int width, int playableHeight) {
    // Limits prevent integer overflow in distance kernels (commit 5e42da351).
    const uint64_t paddedCells = (static_cast<uint64_t>(width) + 2) * (static_cast<uint64_t>(playableHeight) + 2);
    // Padded cells * 7 colors capped at 0x7FFFFFFF (~306M cells); GPU packs IDs into 28 bits.
    if (paddedCells * ballColorCount > mostTargetFieldEntries) {
        return std::format("the board is too large: {}x{} cells, at most {} cells including the open rows", width,
                           playableHeight, mostTargetFieldEntries / ballColorCount);
    }
    return {};
}

Reporting map repairs to the player

The game tracks parser repairs and shows an 8-second toast on start so authors know nonstandard maps were modified.

Source: source/core/level/LevelParser.cpp, source/tests/LevelTests.cpp, commit 0273e6f4a

source/core/level/LevelParser.cpp lines 548-558
// Tracks repairs applied during load; repo levels must load with zero repairs (commit 0273e6f4a).
std::string levelRepairNotice(const LevelData& level) {
    if (level.repairs.empty()) {
        return {};
    }
    std::string notice = std::format("{} does not follow the map format and was repaired: {}", level.name,
                                     level.repairs.front());
    if (level.repairs.size() > 1) {
        notice += std::format(" (and {} more)", level.repairs.size() - 1);
    }
    // Displayed as an 8-second toast (theme::repairToastSeconds) at game start.
    return notice;
}

Where simulation rules come from, and tunnelling prevention

Simulation rules bound speed to ball radius per tick to prevent balls from tunnelling through brick corners.

Source: source/core/level/GameplayRules.h, source/core/level/GameplayRules.cpp, commit 7f21b2fc0

source/core/level/GameplayRules.cpp lines 70-79
// Defaults (Gameplay.txt): 60 ticks/s, max 5 ticks/frame, maxDelta=0.1s, speed=16.0, radius=0.25.
if (rules.ticksPerSecond < fewestTicksPerSecond || rules.ticksPerSecond > mostTicksPerSecond) {
    return std::format("[Simulation] TicksPerSecond must be {} to {}", fewestTicksPerSecond, mostTicksPerSecond);
}
// A ball that moved further than its radius in one tick could pass through the corner of a brick.
// Prevents tunnelling through corners when displacement per tick exceeds radius (commit f3f5454fa).
if (rules.ballSpeed / static_cast<float>(rules.ticksPerSecond) > rules.ballRadius) {
    return "[Balls] Speed must not move a ball more than its radius in one tick";
}
if (rules.maxTicksPerFrame < 1) {
    return "[Simulation] MaxTicksPerFrame must be at least 1";
}

Large-map loading performance

Redesigning the level parser for large maps

The level parser uses string views over a single file buffer to parse multi-gigabyte boards without heap allocation.

Source: source/core/level/SectionText.h, source/core/level/SectionText.cpp, commit acf3d1fb1

source/core/level/SectionText.h lines 24-43
// Replaced istringstream (commit acf3d1fb1): 8192x8192 (1.8 GB) took 85s; 4096x4096 dropped from 18.7s to 3.1s.
// Calls visit(token) for each token of the line without allocating; stops early when visit returns false.
template <typename Visit>
// Zero-allocation token scanner yields string_views pointing directly into contiguous buffer.
void forEachToken(std::string_view line, Visit&& visit) {
    size_t position = 0;
    while (position < line.size()) {
        while (position < line.size() && (line[position] == ' ' || line[position] == '\t' || line[position] == '\r')) {
            ++position;
        }
        if (position == line.size()) {
            return;
        }
        const size_t start = position;
        while (position < line.size() && line[position] != ' ' && line[position] != '\t' && line[position] != '\r') {
            ++position;
        }
        if (!visit(line.substr(start, position - start))) {
            return;
        }
    }
}

Deduplicating brick types without hash tables

Dense brick IDs are deduplicated with a flat boolean lookup table, avoiding hash table overhead on huge maps.

Source: source/core/level/LevelParser.cpp, commit acf3d1fb1

source/core/level/LevelParser.cpp lines 264-276
// Replaced unordered_set with flat table (commit acf3d1fb1); falls back to hash set if sparse.
const bool dense = !negativeId && static_cast<size_t>(largestId) < layout.types.size() * denseIdSlack + denseIdFloor;
if (dense) {
    // Flat boolean table avoids hashing overhead across up to 67M dense IDs (0..N-1).
    std::vector<uint8_t> known(static_cast<size_t>(largestId) + 1, 0);
    std::erase_if(layout.types, [&](const BrickType& type) {
        return std::exchange(known[static_cast<size_t>(type.id)], uint8_t{1}) != 0;
    });
    for (int& id : layout.cellIds) {
        if (id != emptyBrickId && (id > largestId || !known[static_cast<size_t>(id)])) {
            id = emptyBrickId;
        }
    }
    return typeCount - layout.types.size();
}

Live loading progress on multi-second stress levels

Loading runs on a background thread while reporting step timers to the UI to keep the window responsive.

Source: source/core/common/LoadProgress.h, source/core/ui/LoadingScreen.cpp, commit 67feeed56

source/core/ui/LoadingScreen.cpp lines 21-41
// Background physics thread records phase progress (stride 64 for rows, commit 67feeed56).
LoadingScreenText loadingScreenText(const std::string& levelName, const LoadProgress* progress) {
    LoadingScreenText screen{"Loading " + levelName, {}, {}};
    if (!progress) {
        return screen;
    }
    const std::vector<LoadProgress::Step> steps = progress->steps();
    // Scrolls older steps off screen, displaying at most 12 recent lines (loadingScreenMaxLines).
    const size_t first = steps.size() > loadingScreenMaxLines ? steps.size() - loadingScreenMaxLines : 0;
    for (size_t index = first; index < steps.size(); ++index) {
        const LoadProgress::Step& step = steps[index];
        std::string text = step.text;
        if (!step.detail.empty()) {
            text += ": " + step.detail;
        }
        // Active steps animate cycling dots at dotsPerSecond = 3.0.
        if (!step.done) {
            text += runningDots(step.seconds);
        }
        screen.lines.push_back({std::move(text), stepTime(step.seconds), !step.done});
    }
    screen.total = std::format("{} total", stepTime(progress->elapsedSeconds()));
    return screen;
}

Practical limits on level dimensions and brick counts

Board sizes are capped by 32-bit distance fields and 28-bit GPU brick IDs, with large boards bounded by RAM.

Source: source/core/level/LevelParser.cpp, data/docs/edge-cases.md, commit 5e42da351

source/core/level/LevelParser.cpp lines 27-34
// The simulation indexes the target field (one entry per padded cell and color) and every padded cell with 32-bit
// integers, and the GPU packs a brick's index into 28 bits of a cell word: boards past these would overflow them.
// 11585x11585 (134M bricks) parsed in 148s, runs 20M balls at 106 FPS on 32GB RAM (commit 5e42da351).
// 2048x2048 (4.19M cells) runs at 60 FPS; sparse boards stress BFS wavefronts across empty cells.
constexpr uint64_t mostTargetFieldEntries = 0x7FFFFFFF;
// GPU backend packs brick IDs into 28 bits (at most 268M bricks).
constexpr size_t mostBricks = size_t{1} << 28;
// A board that leaves PlayableHeight out gets this much open space below its bricks: a quarter of the brick rows, and
// at least the sample DA_49Bricks gap.
// Missing PlayableHeight gets 1/4 brick rows as falling space, at least 7 (DA_49Bricks floor).
constexpr int minimumDefaultOpenRows = 7;
constexpr int defaultOpenRowsDivisor = 4;

Hot reload and file watching

LevelWatcher: detecting disk changes without reading partial writes

LevelWatcher debounces file timestamp changes with a 150ms delay to avoid reading partial writes.

Source: source/core/level/LevelWatcher.h, source/core/level/LevelWatcher.cpp, source/tests/LevelWatcherTests.cpp

source/core/level/LevelWatcher.cpp lines 48-63
// Polls every 250ms; missing files return FileTime::min() to settle as Removed.
const FileTime stamp = readStamp();
if (stamp != lastStamp && (!hasPending || stamp != pendingStamp)) {
    pendingStamp = stamp;
    // Debounces for settleDelay (150ms) so file flushes complete before reading.
    pendingSince = now;
    hasPending = true;
    return false;
}
if (hasPending && now - pendingSince >= settleDelay) {
    hasPending = false;
    lastStamp = pendingStamp;
    std::error_code error;
    const bool present = std::filesystem::exists(watchedFiles.bricksPath, error) &&
                         std::filesystem::exists(watchedFiles.launchersPath, error);
    state = present ? LevelDiskState::Changed : LevelDiskState::Removed;
    return true;
}

Hot reload notifies instead of reloading the running simulation

Disk changes notify the player to restart instead of hot-reloading, avoiding invalidating GPU simulation buffers.

Source: source/core/level/LevelWatcher.h, source/core/ui/ScreenFlow.cpp, data/docs/level-format.md

source/core/level/LevelWatcher.h lines 9-37
// Whether the level in play still matches its files (a change on disk is not loaded until Restart).
// Hot-reloading mid-simulation would corrupt GPU distance fields and drop balls.
enum class LevelDiskState { Unchanged, Changed, Removed };

// The header notice for the state; empty for Unchanged.
// Header shows 'Map changed on disk - Restart to load it' or 'Map removed from disk'.
std::string_view levelDiskNotice(LevelDiskState state);

class LevelWatcher {
public:
    void watch(const LevelFiles& files);
    // True once per settled change of the files; it also updates diskState.
    bool pollChanged(std::chrono::steady_clock::time_point now);
    // Changed stays until markLoaded, so the notice outlives an edit that is undone; Removed follows the files.
    LevelDiskState diskState() const { return state; }
    // The game has just loaded the files as they are now.
    void markLoaded() { state = LevelDiskState::Unchanged; }

private:
    using FileTime = std::filesystem::file_time_type;

    FileTime readStamp() const;

    LevelFiles watchedFiles;
    FileTime lastStamp{};
    FileTime pendingStamp{};
    std::chrono::steady_clock::time_point nextPoll{};
    std::chrono::steady_clock::time_point pendingSince{};
    bool hasPending = false;
    LevelDiskState state = LevelDiskState::Unchanged;
};

LevelWriter: editor saves without corrupted reads or watcher races

LevelWriter saves to a .tmp file and renames atomically to prevent watchers reading half-written data.

Source: source/core/level/LevelWriter.cpp, source/core/level/LevelWriter.h

source/core/level/LevelWriter.cpp lines 10-26
// Writes active brick types only, discarding unused orphans from layout.cellIds.
Result<void> writeTextFileAtomically(const std::filesystem::path& path, const std::string& text) {
    std::error_code error;
    std::filesystem::create_directories(path.parent_path(), error);
    const auto temporaryPath = std::filesystem::path(path).concat(".tmp");
    {
        std::ofstream file(temporaryPath, std::ios::binary | std::ios::trunc);
        if (!file) {
            return Error{std::format("cannot write {}", temporaryPath.string())};
        }
        file << text;
    }
    // Atomic rename prevents file watchers and scanners from observing half-written files.
    std::filesystem::rename(temporaryPath, path, error);
    if (error) {
        return Error{std::format("cannot replace {}: {}", path.string(), error.message())};
    }
    return {};
}

LevelImporter: ingesting level file pairs and folders

LevelImporter copies launchers before bricks so directory scanners never see half-copied levels.

Source: source/core/level/LevelImporter.cpp, source/tests/LevelImportTests.cpp

source/core/level/LevelImporter.cpp lines 109-124
// Copies the spawn file first: the level list only shows a name once its bricks file exists, so it never sees
// a half-copied pair.
// Normal mode warns if total ball count cannot cover total brick hits.
std::optional<std::string> copyPair(const LevelFiles& source, const LevelFiles& target) {
    // Copies SpawnBall first: findLevels scans for Bricks, avoiding partial pairs.
    for (const auto& [from, to] : {std::pair{source.launchersPath, target.launchersPath},
                                   std::pair{source.bricksPath, target.bricksPath}}) {
        std::error_code error;
        if (std::filesystem::exists(to, error) && std::filesystem::equivalent(from, to, error)) {
            continue;
        }
        std::filesystem::copy_file(from, to, std::filesystem::copy_options::overwrite_existing, error);
        if (error) {
            return std::format("{}: {}", fileNameOf(to), error.message());
        }
    }
    return std::nullopt;
}

The level editor

// The game's map editor ("Map editor") as one state machine for every front end: the tool, brush and launcher
// count, the selected brick, strokes and their undo steps, launcher clicks, the shortcuts, the steppers' ranges, the
// name field's rules, saving with its messages and the unsaved-changes confirmation on Back. LevelEditor keeps the
// level and its undo history; this keeps everything a player does to it. Front ends map pointer and key events onto
// these calls and draw the level, the labels and the message.
// Bounds: max hits 999, balls 100k, board 200x200 (400 rows), launchers 8x8.
enum class EditorTool { Paint, Extend, Erase, Pick };

    Erase, // X

Drag gestures as atomic undo transactions in EditorSession

Drag gestures are coalesced into single undo transactions to keep history clean during continuous mouse painting.

Source: source/core/editor/EditorSession.cpp, source/core/editor/LevelEditor.cpp

source/core/editor/EditorSession.cpp lines 113-135
// Restoring an undo step resets selectedBrickId to empty if its type no longer exists.
void EditorSession::beginStroke(bool erase) {
    // Snapshots LevelData onto undoStack (capped at maxUndoSteps = 100); clears redoStack.
    editor.beginEdit();
    strokeActive = true;
    strokeErases = erase;
    strokeDragging = false;
    lastStrokeCell.reset();
    confirmLeave = false;
}

void EditorSession::strokeCell(int column, int row) {
    const BrickLayout& bricks = editor.level().bricks;
    if (!strokeActive || column < 0 || row < 0 || column >= bricks.width || row >= bricks.height) {
        return;
    }
    if (lastStrokeCell && lastStrokeCell->first == column && lastStrokeCell->second == row) {
        return;
    }
    applyBrickTool(column, row);
    // Extend picks the brick under the first cell of a stroke and grows it over the cells after that.
    strokeDragging = lastStrokeCell.has_value() || currentTool != EditorTool::Extend || selectedBrickId != emptyBrickId;
    lastStrokeCell = std::make_pair(column, row);
    edited();
}

Editor tools and brick extension

Editor tools support paint, extend, erase, and pick, with right-click erasing regardless of active tool.

Source: source/core/editor/EditorSession.h, source/core/editor/EditorSession.cpp, source/core/editor/LevelEditor.cpp

source/core/editor/EditorSession.cpp lines 137-164
// Tools: Paint (P), Extend (E), Erase (X), Pick (K); autoFill balances ball and hit counts.
void EditorSession::applyBrickTool(int column, int row) {
    // Right-clicking or dragging with right mouse button erases regardless of active tool.
    if (strokeErases || currentTool == EditorTool::Erase) {
        editor.eraseCell(column, row);
        return;
    }
    const int brickUnderCursor = editor.brickIdAt(column, row);
    switch (currentTool) {
    case EditorTool::Paint:
        editor.paintCell(column, row, currentColor, currentHits);
        break;
    // Extend locks onto initial brick and grows it over orthogonally adjacent cells.
    case EditorTool::Extend:
        if (!strokeDragging && brickUnderCursor != emptyBrickId) {
            selectedBrickId = brickUnderCursor;
        } else if (selectedBrickId != emptyBrickId) {
            editor.extendBrick(selectedBrickId, column, row);
        }
        break;
    case EditorTool::Pick:
        selectedBrickId = brickUnderCursor;
        if (const BrickType* type = editor.level().bricks.findType(brickUnderCursor)) {
            currentColor = type->color;
            currentHits = type->hits;
        }
        break;
    case EditorTool::Erase:
        break;
    }
}

Validation before saving or testing a level

Validation enforces brick presence, launcher coverage, and minimum color counts before saving or testing.

Source: source/core/editor/LevelEditor.cpp, source/core/editor/EditorSession.cpp

source/core/editor/LevelEditor.cpp lines 301-340
// Back requires a second click ('Discard changes?') when unsaved modifications exist.
EditorValidation LevelEditor::validate(GameMode mode) const {
    EditorValidation validation;
    validation.brickHits = countBrickHitsByColor(levelData.bricks);
    validation.balls = countBallsByColor(levelData.launchers);
    bool hasBricks = false;
    for (const int id : levelData.bricks.cellIds) {
        if (id != emptyBrickId) {
            hasBricks = true;
            break;
        }
    }
    if (!hasBricks) {
        validation.problems.push_back("level has no bricks");
    }
    // Normal mode requires total ball count for each color to cover total brick hits of that color.
    for (const BallColor color : allBallColors) {
        const int hits = validation.brickHits[toIndex(color)];
        const int balls = validation.balls[toIndex(color)];
        if (hits > 0 && balls < hits) {
            validation.problems.push_back(
                std::format("{}: {} balls for {} hits", ballColorName(color), balls, hits));
        }
    }
    // Requires at least 3 distinct brick colors in Normal mode, or all 7 in Performance mode.
    const int requiredColors = mode == GameMode::Performance ? minPerformanceColors : minNormalColors;
    const int distinctColors = countDistinctColors(validation.brickHits);
    if (distinctColors < requiredColors) {
        validation.problems.push_back(std::format("only {} brick {}, need at least {}", distinctColors,
                                                  distinctColors == 1 ? "color" : "colors", requiredColors));
    }
    bool hasLaunchers = false;
    for (const auto& slot : levelData.launchers.slots) {
        if (slot) {
            hasLaunchers = true;
            break;
        }
    }
    if (!hasLaunchers) {
        validation.problems.push_back("level has no launchers");
    }
    return validation;
}

UI models shared by front ends

Why balls_core owns the UI models

If each front end (SDL, Web, Unreal, Unity) implemented its own UI flow, menu logic, and layout calculations, behavior would inevitably diverge across platforms, requiring bug fixes to be written four times.

By centralizing UI state machines in balls_core, the front ends remain thin rendering shells. The core computes exact bounding rectangles, option availability, text labels, and validation rules; the front end simply maps input events to core method calls and renders primitive shapes and text using its native graphics API.

Source: source/core/ui/ScreenFlow.h, source/core/ui/MenuModel.h, data/docs/codebase.html

How thin is the Unreal Engine UI implementation? SBallsGameplayHud, SBallsMainMenu, and SBallsSettingsPanel are Slate widgets that query SessionView, ScreenFlow, and MenuModel every tick, binding button clicks directly to flow.activate(action).

ScreenFlow: navigation, screens and Escape routing

ScreenFlow centralizes top-level screen navigation, modal overlays, and Escape key routing across platforms.

Source: source/core/ui/ScreenFlow.h, source/core/ui/ScreenFlow.cpp, source/tests/ScreenFlowTests.cpp

source/core/ui/ScreenFlow.cpp lines 357-373
// Benchmark can only be launched from MainMenu to prevent destroying active gameplay sessions.
bool ScreenFlow::escape() {
    switch (currentScreen) {
    case FlowScreen::MainMenu:
        return false;
    // Escape is ignored in gameplay except to close the in-game Settings overlay back to Pause.
    case FlowScreen::Gameplay:
        // No keyboard shortcuts in gameplay: Pause is the header. Only the Settings panel keeps Esc as its Back.
        return currentOverlay == FlowOverlay::Settings && activate(FlowAction::Back);
    case FlowScreen::MachineBenchmark:
        return activate(benchmarkDone ? FlowAction::ContinueFromBenchmark : FlowAction::SkipBenchmark);
    case FlowScreen::LevelSelect:
    case FlowScreen::LoadError:
    case FlowScreen::MapEditor:
    case FlowScreen::Settings:
        return activate(FlowAction::Back);
    }
    return false;
}

MenuModel: metrics, device options and brick hits

MenuModel formats large numbers with K, M, or B suffixes and keeps at least one compute device enabled.

Source: source/core/ui/MenuModel.h, source/core/ui/MenuModel.cpp

source/core/ui/MenuModel.cpp lines 71-92
// Formats spawn rates (10..1M, Max); GPU rows appear only in Hybrid mode (at least one device enabled).
std::string brickHitLabel(int64_t hitsLeft) {
    constexpr int64_t wholeBelow = 10'000;
    if (hitsLeft < wholeBelow) {
        return std::to_string(hitsLeft);
    }
    // Values >= 10,000 scale to three significant digits with unit suffixes K, M, or B.
    constexpr std::array<std::pair<double, std::string_view>, 3> units{{{1e9, "B"}, {1e6, "M"}, {1e3, "K"}}};
    for (const auto& [scale, suffix] : units) {
        const double value = static_cast<double>(hitsLeft) / scale;
        if (value >= 1.0) {
            const int decimals = value >= 100.0 ? 0 : value >= 10.0 ? 1 : 2;
            std::string text = std::format("{:.{}f}", value, decimals);
            if (text.find('.') != std::string::npos) {
                // Trims trailing zeroes and dangling decimal points (e.g. 12.4K, 1.50M).
                text.erase(text.find_last_not_of('0') + 1);
                if (text.back() == '.') {
                    text.pop_back();
                }
            }
            return text + std::string(suffix);
        }
    }
    return std::to_string(hitsLeft);
}

// Switches between Portrait and Landscape at aspect ratio 1.0 unless overridden.
const float columnHeight = std::max(frameHeight - outerMargin * 2.0f, 1.0f);
const float boardWidth = frameWidth - outerMargin * 2.0f - landscapeGadgetWidth * 2.0f -
                         gadgetGap * 2.0f - boardPadding * 2.0f;
const float boardHeight = frameHeight - outerMargin * 2.0f - boardPadding * 2.0f;
// Scales cell sizes uniformly so large boards fit, shrinking below 1.0f (commit 5f73f9b59).
layout.cellSize = std::max(smallestCellSize, std::min(boardWidth / static_cast<float>(columns),
                                                      boardHeight / static_cast<float>(rows)));
const float frameBoardWidth = layout.cellSize * static_cast<float>(columns) + boardPadding * 2.0f;
const float frameBoardHeight = layout.cellSize * static_cast<float>(rows) + boardPadding * 2.0f;
const float minimumGroupWidth = landscapeGadgetWidth * 2.0f + gadgetGap * 2.0f + frameBoardWidth;
// The board keeps its size; the room it leaves goes to the gadgets until the group reaches 16:9, so
// ultrawide windows don't stretch them across. Any room past that widens the board's section, which always
// fills its column, unless the player keeps wide screens to a centred 16:9 group.
// Ultra-wide displays anchor gadgets to edges or center them around a 16:9 frame.
const float groupFrame = wideScreen == WideScreenAnchor::Centered
                             ? std::min(frameWidth, frameHeight * widestLandscapeAspect)
                             : frameWidth;
const float groupWidth = std::max(groupFrame - outerMargin * 2.0f, minimumGroupWidth);
const float widestGadgetGroup = std::min(groupWidth, frameHeight * widestLandscapeAspect - outerMargin * 2.0f);
const float gadgetGrowth = std::max(0.0f, widestGadgetGroup - minimumGroupWidth) * 0.5f;
const float gadgetWidth = landscapeGadgetWidth + gadgetGrowth;
const float mainWidth = groupWidth - gadgetWidth * 2.0f - gadgetGap * 2.0f;
const float groupX = (frameWidth - groupWidth) * 0.5f;
layout.leftGadget = {groupX, outerMargin, gadgetWidth, columnHeight};
layout.header = {groupX, outerMargin, gadgetWidth, landscapeHeaderHeight};
layout.interaction = {groupX, outerMargin + landscapeHeaderHeight + gadgetGap, gadgetWidth,
                      columnHeight - landscapeHeaderHeight - gadgetGap};
layout.main = {groupX + gadgetWidth + gadgetGap, outerMargin, mainWidth,
               std::max(columnHeight, frameBoardHeight)};
layout.rightGadget = {layout.main.x + mainWidth + gadgetGap, outerMargin, gadgetWidth, columnHeight};
layout.debug = layout.rightGadget;

Screen flow requests

FlowRequestKind: decoupling user actions from execution

ScreenFlow queues user actions as abstract requests, leaving window and thread execution to front ends.

Source: source/core/ui/ScreenFlow.h, source/core/ui/ScreenFlow.cpp

source/core/ui/ScreenFlow.cpp lines 44-55
// Decouples core UI transitions from platform windowing, threading, and dialog APIs.
void ScreenFlow::request(FlowRequestKind kind, std::optional<LevelFiles> files, std::filesystem::path folder) {
    requests.push_back(FlowRequest{kind, currentMode, std::move(files), std::move(folder)});
}

// Front ends drain queued requests once per frame.
std::optional<FlowRequest> ScreenFlow::takeRequest() {
    if (requests.empty()) {
        return std::nullopt;
    }
    FlowRequest next = std::move(requests.front());
    requests.erase(requests.begin());
    return next;
}

How SDL processes FlowRequest commands in its update

The SDL application loop drains FlowRequest commands each frame to create screens, popups, and sessions.

Source: source/frontends/sdl/app/App.cpp, source/core/ui/ScreenFlow.cpp

source/frontends/sdl/app/App.cpp lines 663-704
// ReloadGame recreates GameplayScreen, reallocating GPU textures, buffers, and worker threads.
void App::processFlowRequests() {
    while (const auto request = flowInstance.takeRequest()) {
        switch (request->kind) {
        case FlowRequestKind::StartGame:
        case FlowRequestKind::ReloadGame:
            if (request->files) {
                currentScreen = std::make_unique<GameplayScreen>(*this, request->mode, *request->files);
            }
            break;
        case FlowRequestKind::RestartGame:
            if (auto* gameplay = dynamic_cast<GameplayScreen*>(currentScreen.get())) {
                gameplay->restartSession();
            }
            break;
        case FlowRequestKind::EndGame:
            if (dynamic_cast<GameplayScreen*>(currentScreen.get())) {
                currentScreen.reset();
            }
            break;
        case FlowRequestKind::OpenEditor:
            currentScreen = std::make_unique<MapEditorScreen>(*this, request->mode, request->files);
            break;
        // Passes hardware fingerprint to benchmark screen for reproducible scoring.
        case FlowRequestKind::StartBenchmark:
            currentScreen = std::make_unique<MachineBenchmarkScreen>(*this, currentHardwareFingerprint(gameVersion()));
            break;
        case FlowRequestKind::SkipBenchmark:
            if (auto* benchmark = dynamic_cast<MachineBenchmarkScreen*>(currentScreen.get())) {
                benchmark->skip();
            }
            break;
        case FlowRequestKind::PickLevelFiles:
            openLevelImportDialog(request->folder);
            break;
        case FlowRequestKind::OpenFolder:
            openFolder(request->folder);
            break;
        case FlowRequestKind::Quit:
            quit();
            break;
        }
    }
}

Pause, settings overlays and level-complete transitions

In-game transitions halt physics during pause while rendering continues, and route level completion to the next map.

Source: source/core/ui/ScreenFlow.cpp, source/tests/ScreenFlowTests.cpp

source/core/ui/ScreenFlow.cpp lines 306-355
// NextLevel enqueues EndGame then StartGame; finishing the last level returns to LevelSelect.
bool ScreenFlow::activateInGame(FlowAction action) {
    switch (currentOverlay) {
    case FlowOverlay::None:
        // Freezes physics stepping while continuing to render simulation state.
        if (action == FlowAction::TogglePause) {
            currentOverlay = FlowOverlay::Pause;
            return true;
        }
        return false;
    case FlowOverlay::Pause:
        switch (action) {
        case FlowAction::TogglePause:
        case FlowAction::Resume:
            currentOverlay = FlowOverlay::None;
            return true;
        case FlowAction::Restart:
            restart();
            return true;
        case FlowAction::PauseSettings:
            currentOverlay = FlowOverlay::Settings;
            return true;
        case FlowAction::MainMenu:
            showMainMenu();
            return true;
        default:
            return false;
        }
    case FlowOverlay::Win:
        switch (action) {
        case FlowAction::NextLevel:
            nextLevel();
            return true;
        case FlowAction::Restart:
            restart();
            return true;
        case FlowAction::MainMenu:
            showMainMenu();
            return true;
        default:
            return false;
        }
    case FlowOverlay::Settings:
        // The header still reads Resume under the Settings overlay; the game stays paused until Back.
        if (action == FlowAction::Back) {
            currentOverlay = FlowOverlay::Pause;
            return true;
        }
        return false;
    }
    return false;
}

HTTP control API

The HTTP control API: design and purpose

The Control API provides headless test automation and telemetry without depending on socket libraries.

Source: source/core/control/ControlProtocol.h, source/core/control/ControlApi.h

source/core/control/ControlProtocol.h lines 9-26
// One request of the game's HTTP control API ("Launch options and automation"): a GET path with query parameters.
// Pure string parsing and dispatch in balls_core without socket dependencies.
struct ControlRequest {
    std::string path;
    // Uses transparent comparator std::less<> for zero-copy string_view lookups.
    std::map<std::string, std::string, std::less<>> query;

    std::optional<std::string> text(std::string_view key) const;
    std::optional<int> integer(std::string_view key) const;
    // Ball counts pass 2^31 (the target PC holds about 7.1B), so they are read as 64-bit.
    std::optional<uint64_t> count(std::string_view key) const;
};

struct ControlResponse {
    int status = 200;
    std::string json = "{\"ok\":true}";

    static ControlResponse ok(std::string json = "{\"ok\":true}") { return {200, std::move(json)}; }
    static ControlResponse error(int status, std::string_view message);
};

Control API routes and the /state telemetry

The /state route reports frametimes, active compute backends, and ball metrics in a single JSON payload.

Source: source/core/control/ControlApi.cpp, source/core/control/ControlProtocol.cpp

source/core/control/ControlApi.cpp lines 66-86
// Exposes 22 routes; flag() accepts 1, 0, true, false, on, and off case-insensitively.
std::string stateJson(ControlHost& host) {
    const ControlFrontEndState front = host.frontEndState();
    static const FrameTimings noFrames;
    const FrameTimings& frames = front.frames ? *front.frames : noFrames;
    // Averages total frameMs, cpuMs, gpuMs, renderMs, and presentMs.
    const FrameSample& sample = frames.average();
    ControlGame* game = host.game();
    std::shared_ptr<const SessionView> view = game ? game->sessionView() : nullptr;
    const BackendKind active = view ? view->backend : front.preferredBackend;
    const std::string details = game ? controlGameJson(*game)
                                     : front.screenDetails.empty() ? std::string("{}") : front.screenDetails;
    return std::format(
        "{{\"ok\":true,\"frontend\":\"{}\",\"renderer\":\"{}\",\"screen\":\"{}\",\"window\":[{},{}],"
        "\"fps\":{:.1f},\"minFps\":{:.1f},\"workFps\":{:.1f},\"minWorkFps\":{:.1f},\"shownFps\":{:.1f},"
        "\"frameMs\":{:.3f},\"cpuMs\":{:.3f},\"gpuMs\":{:.3f},\"renderMs\":{:.3f},\"presentMs\":{:.3f},"
        "\"preferredBackend\":\"{}\",\"backend\":\"{}\",\"vsync\":{},\"toast\":\"{}\",{}{}\"details\":{}}}",
        front.frontEnd, jsonEscape(front.renderer), front.screen, front.windowWidth, front.windowHeight,
        frames.framesPerSecond(), frames.worstFpsLastSecond(), frames.workFramesPerSecond(),
        frames.worstWorkFpsLastSecond(), frames.shownFramesPerSecond(), sample.frameMs, sample.cpuMs, sample.gpuMs,
        sample.renderMs, sample.presentMs, backendKindName(front.preferredBackend), backendKindName(active),
        front.vsync, jsonEscape(front.toast), front.extraFields, front.extraFields.empty() ? "" : ",", details);
}

/fill: 64-bit counts and the no-argument default

/fill accepts 64-bit counts up to billions of balls, defaulting to maximum hardware capacity when omitted.

Source: source/core/control/ControlApi.cpp, source/core/control/ControlProtocol.h, commit 823973ef9

source/core/control/ControlApi.cpp lines 173-185
// 64-bit counts support up to 7.1B balls; SpawnPacer caps generation per frame (commit 823973ef9).
if (path == "/fill") {
    // Without a count, /fill is Auto: up to the backend's capacity, the most balls this machine holds.
    if (!request.text("count")) {
        game.post([](GameSession& session) { session.fillToBallLimit(); });
        return done;
    }
    const std::optional<uint64_t> count = request.count("count");
    if (!count) {
        return ControlResponse::error(400, "count must be a whole number of balls");
    }
    game.post([count = static_cast<BallCount>(*count)](GameSession& session) { session.fillWithBalls(count); });
    return done;
}

Asynchronous control operations such as screenshots

Screenshot capture is deferred until swapchain presentation completes, resolving the HTTP promise asynchronously.

Source: source/core/control/ControlApi.h, source/frontends/sdl/control/ControlServer.cpp, source/tests/ControlApiTests.cpp

source/frontends/sdl/control/ControlServer.cpp lines 157-170
// Times out after 10s (responseTimeout) with HTTP 504 Gateway Timeout if game thread hangs.
void ControlServer::pump(const Handler& handler) {
    std::deque<PendingCommand> commands;
    {
        std::lock_guard lock(queueMutex);
        commands.swap(queue);
    }
    for (PendingCommand& command : commands) {
        if (std::optional<ControlResponse> response = handler(command.request)) {
            command.response.set_value(std::move(*response));
        } else {
            // Commands returning nullopt are held in deferred queue until swapchain presentation completes.
            deferred.push_back(std::move(command));
        }
    }
}

The control API in WebAssembly builds without TCP sockets

In WebAssembly builds, the Control API binds to window.ballsControl() for synchronous browser automation.

Source: source/frontends/sdl/control/ControlBridgeWeb.cpp, source/frontends/sdl/control/WebControlBridge.h, commit 2fd7d321a

source/frontends/sdl/control/ControlBridgeWeb.cpp lines 21-32
// Runs URL command sequences (?cmd=/play...;/start) once ballsReady fires (commit 2fd7d321a).
// The page's entry point: the request target of the desktop API ("/play?mode=performance&level=DA_1000Bricks") in,
// that route's JSON out. The result stays valid until the next call, which is all a JavaScript string decode needs.
extern "C" EMSCRIPTEN_KEEPALIVE const char* ballsControl(const char* target) {
    static std::string response;
    // Returns error JSON if called before engine initialization finishes.
    if (!handler) {
        response = R"({"ok":false,"error":"the game is still loading"})";
        return response.c_str();
    }
    const std::optional<ControlResponse> answer = handler(parseControlTarget(target));
    response = answer ? answer->json : std::string(R"({"ok":false,"error":"unknown route"})");
    return response.c_str();
}

Front-end implementations of ControlHost

The ControlHost interface contract

ControlHost abstracts engine-specific lifecycle, benchmarks, and settings behind a shared interface.

Source: source/core/control/ControlApi.h, source/core/control/ControlApi.cpp

source/core/control/ControlApi.h lines 72-105
// Unhandled routes fall through to handleOwnRoute, or return HTTP 404.
class ControlHost {
public:
    virtual ~ControlHost() = default;

    virtual ControlFrontEndState frontEndState() const = 0;
    // Null outside gameplay.
    virtual ControlGame* game() = 0;
    virtual std::filesystem::path levelDirectory(GameMode mode) const = 0;

    // Nothing, when the answer has to wait for the frame that writes the file; the front end then answers itself.
    // Screenshot returns nullopt until the frame presents and writes the file.
    virtual std::optional<ControlResponse> takeScreenshot(const std::string& path) = 0;
    virtual void play(GameMode mode, const LevelFiles& level) = 0;
    virtual void showMainMenu() = 0;
    // False where the front end has no such screen (the route then answers 404).
    virtual bool showLevelSelect(GameMode mode) = 0;
    virtual bool openEditor(GameMode mode, const std::optional<LevelFiles>& level) = 0;
    virtual void quit() = 0;

    virtual const Settings& settings() const = 0;
    // Saves the settings and applies them, the default backend too when one is given, as the Settings page does.
    virtual void applySettings(const Settings& settings, std::optional<BackendKind> defaultBackend) = 0;
    // Opens the Settings page, or the in-game settings overlay during a game.
    virtual void openSettings() = 0;
    virtual void setVsync(bool enabled) = 0;

    virtual ControlBenchmarkState benchmarkState() const = 0;
    virtual void runBenchmark() = 0;
    // False when the benchmark screen is not in a state where the action applies.
    virtual bool skipBenchmark() = 0;
    virtual bool continueFromBenchmark() = 0;

    // Routes only this front end has (renderer and window controls, its own screens); nothing when unknown.
    // Platform-specific extension hook for routes like /hitch or /import.
    virtual std::optional<ControlResponse> handleOwnRoute(const ControlRequest&) { return std::nullopt; }
};

SDL's ControlServer: socket threads and the main thread

The SDL control server queues socket requests on a background thread and drains them on the main thread.

Source: source/frontends/sdl/control/ControlServer.h, source/frontends/sdl/control/ControlServer.cpp, source/frontends/sdl/app/App.cpp

source/frontends/sdl/control/ControlServer.cpp lines 157-170
// Socket thread enqueues requests; on shutdown, stop() drains queues with HTTP 503.
void ControlServer::pump(const Handler& handler) {
    std::deque<PendingCommand> commands;
    {
        std::lock_guard lock(queueMutex);
        // Swaps command queues under lock to minimize contention with socket thread.
        commands.swap(queue);
    }
    for (PendingCommand& command : commands) {
        if (std::optional<ControlResponse> response = handler(command.request)) {
            command.response.set_value(std::move(*response));
        } else {
            // Deferred commands (like screenshots) wait across multiple frames.
            deferred.push_back(std::move(command));
        }
    }
}

Unreal's ControlHost on UE's HTTPServer module

Unreal binds the Control API using FHttpServerModule, routing requests onto the game thread.

Source: source/frontends/unreal/Source/Balls/Private/BallsControlServer.cpp, source/frontends/unreal/Source/Balls/Private/BallsApp.cpp

source/frontends/unreal/Source/Balls/Private/BallsControlServer.cpp lines 25-51
// /state telemetry reads GPU name directly from Unreal's RHI string *GRHIAdapterName.
bool FBallsControlServer::Start(int32 Port) {
    if (bRunning) {
        return true;
    }
    if (Port <= 0) {
        return false;
    }
    // The module is not loaded by anything else in this game, so load it rather than only asking whether it is up.
    FHttpServerModule& Module = FModuleManager::LoadModuleChecked<FHttpServerModule>(TEXT("HTTPServer"));
    Router = Module.GetHttpRouter(static_cast<uint32>(Port), true);
    if (!Router.IsValid()) {
        UE_LOG(LogTemp, Warning, TEXT("BallsControl: port %d could not be bound"), Port);
        return false;
    }
    // UE's router binds paths one by one: every path the core answers, then Unreal's own.
    for (const std::string_view Path : controlApiPaths()) {
        Bind(*FString(UTF8_TO_TCHAR(std::string(Path).c_str())));
    }
    for (const FString& Path : FBallsApp::OwnControlPaths()) {
        Bind(*Path);
    }
    // Activates HTTP listeners on the bound port.
    Module.StartAllListeners();
    BoundPort = Port;
    bRunning = true;
    UE_LOG(LogTemp, Display, TEXT("BallsControl: listening on port %d"), Port);
    return true;
}

Unity's ControlHost across the managed and native boundary

Unity bridges the Control API across C# and native C++ via C ABI exports pumped on the main thread.

Source: source/frontends/unity/Assets/Balls/Runtime/App/ControlApi.cs, source/plugin/BallsUnityApi.cpp

source/frontends/unity/Assets/Balls/Runtime/App/ControlApi.cs lines 146-175
/// <summary>
/// Routes Unity keeps (import, compute GPU and the gameplay controls, which go through the HUD and BallsInput so
/// they work in Burst too) are answered here; everything else is the core's handler, the same one SDL and
/// Unreal use, and what it asks of the front end comes back as actions.
/// </summary>
// Screenshots poll disk via ScreenCapture.CaptureScreenshot in Pump() until written.
private void Handle(Request request)
{
    if (HandleOwn(request))
    {
        return;
    }
    NativeControlContext context = Context();
    int status = BallsNative.balls_control_handle(control, ref context, request.Target, json, json.Length, out int length);
    if (length > json.Length)
    {
        // Resizes buffer if native JSON response exceeds initial allocation.
        json = new byte[length + 1];
        status = BallsNative.balls_control_handle(control, ref context, request.Target, json, json.Length, out length);
    }
    if (status > 0)
    {
        request.Status = status;
        request.Body = Encoding.UTF8.GetString(json, 0, length);
        request.Answered = true;
    }
    else if (status < 0)
    {
        Error(request, 404, "unknown path");
    }
    RunActions(request);
}

Custom automation routes through ControlHost::handleOwnRoute

Front ends expose platform routes through handleOwnRoute for window sizing, hitch telemetry, and level import.

Source: source/core/control/ControlApi.cpp, source/frontends/sdl/app/App.cpp

source/frontends/sdl/app/App.cpp lines 853-901
// The routes only SDL has: renderer and window controls, level import and the frame hitch probe.
// Test runners query /hitch to verify background tasks or pipeline switches did not spike frametimes.
std::optional<ControlResponse> App::handleOwnControl(const ControlRequest& request) {
    const std::string& path = request.path;
    // Returns longest frame interval and resets counter for hitch testing.
    if (path == "/hitch") {
        const double longest = longestFrameIntervalMs;
        longestFrameIntervalMs = 0.0;
        return ControlResponse::ok(std::format("{{\"ok\":true,\"longestFrameMs\":{:.2f}}}", longest));
    }
    // Accepts pipe-separated ('|') paths for headless batch level imports.
    if (path == "/import") {
        std::vector<std::filesystem::path> paths;
        const std::string list = request.text("path").value_or("");
        for (size_t start = 0; start < list.size();) {
            const size_t end = std::min(list.find(importPathSeparator, start), list.size());
            if (end > start) {
                paths.push_back(pathFromUtf8(list.substr(start, end - start).c_str()));
            }
            start = end + 1;
        }
        if (paths.empty()) {
            return ControlResponse::error(400, "missing path");
        }
        flowInstance.importLevels(paths);
        return ControlResponse::ok(std::format("{{\"ok\":true,\"toast\":\"{}\"}}", jsonEscape(flowInstance.toast().text)));
    }
    if (path == "/renderer") {
        const std::string wantedRenderer = request.text("renderer").value_or(activeRenderer);
        const std::string wantedApi = request.text("api").value_or(activeGraphicsApi);
        requestRenderer(wantedRenderer, wantedApi);
        return ControlResponse::ok();
    }
    if (path == "/render-gpu") {
        const auto index = request.integer("index");
        if (!index || *index < 0 || *index >= static_cast<long long>(renderAdapterList.size())) {
            return ControlResponse::error(400, "index must name an adapter from /state renderAdapters");
        }
        requestRenderAdapter(static_cast<int>(*index));
        return ControlResponse::ok();
    }
    if (path == "/window") {
        int width = 0;
        int height = 0;
        SDL_GetWindowSize(window, &width, &height);
        width = request.integer("width").value_or(width);
        height = request.integer("height").value_or(height);
        SDL_SetWindowSize(window, std::max(width, minimumWindowWidth), std::max(height, minimumWindowHeight));
        return ControlResponse::ok();
    }
    return currentScreen ? currentScreen->handleControl(request) : std::nullopt;
}

Key facts

  • Each level is a file pair: <Name>_Bricks.txt (board dimensions, cell grid, brick types) and <Name>_SpawnBall.txt (launcher columns, visible rows, slot layout).
  • Level parser auto-repairs BOMs, comments, case differences, Key: value/Key=value syntax, ragged layout rows, alternate empty markers, and spacing in launcher ball counts (commit 6c2d193c9).
  • A missing PlayableHeight adds a quarter of the brick rows as open space, with a minimum floor of 7 rows (commit 9b4332d48).
  • Simulation limits enforce (width + 2) * (playableHeight + 2) * 7 <= 0x7FFFFFFF (~306M cells across 7 colors) and at most 2^28 (268M) bricks to avoid 32-bit and 28-bit index overflow (commit 5e42da351).
  • Zero-allocation parsing via std::string_view slices in splitSections and flat dense ID tables dropped 4096x4096 parse times from 18.7 s to 3.1 s (commit acf3d1fb1).
  • Level file loading runs asynchronously on the physics thread, reporting live stage updates to LoadProgress so the UI thread renders animated progress dots at 3 Hz (commit 67feeed56).
  • LevelWatcher polls file modification timestamps every 250 ms and requires a 150 ms settle delay to debounce external file saves before notifying the user.
  • Disk changes during gameplay do not mutate active simulation state; LevelWatcher sets LevelDiskState::Changed ("Map changed on disk - Restart to load it"), reloading only upon Restart.
  • LevelWriter::saveLevel writes atomically to a .tmp file before executing std::filesystem::rename, preventing partial reads by file watchers.
  • LevelEditor::eraseCell performs a 4-neighbor BFS over remaining cells of an erased multi-cell brick; if disconnected (pieces.size() > 1), each piece receives a new ID with original color and hits.
  • EditorSession::beginStroke captures a snapshot onto the 100-step undo stack, bundling multi-cell drag sweeps into a single undo operation.
  • ScreenFlow emits decoupled FlowRequestKind values (such as StartGame, OpenEditor, StartBenchmark, Quit) which front ends poll and drain via takeRequest() each frame.
  • computeCoreGameplayLayout computes responsive bounding boxes for header, main board, gadgets (with 8 px gaps), and debug overlay for landscape, portrait, and wide screen anchors.
  • ControlApi exposes 22 automation routes (/state, /play, /fill, /rate, /screenshot, ...) answering against an abstract ControlHost implemented by SDL, Unreal, and Unity.
  • The /fill automation route parses unsigned 64-bit integer counts to support testing machines holding up to 7.1 billion balls, defaulting to backend Auto capacity when empty (commit 823973ef9).

4 · Plugins and run time

Why physics lives in loadable plugins

Why physics lives in shared-library plugins

Physics backends are dynamic plugins to isolate GPU SDK dependencies and let simulation code rebuild in seconds.

Source: source/core/sim/PhysicsBackend.h, data/docs/physics-backends.md

data/docs/physics-backends.md lines 8-24
# Commit 655f38e0b moved physics into shared plugins to isolate SDK dependencies and allow hot reload.
## Plugins

# Core and front ends load plugins on demand so machines without CUDA/Vulkan start cleanly.
Single-device backends are shared library plugins loaded from next to the executable (`balls_physics_cpu`,
`balls_physics_cuda`, `balls_physics_vulkan`, and `balls_physics_rocm`), while the composite backends
(`CUDA+AVX2` and `Hybrid`) are built directly into `balls_core`. Physics plugin work only rebuilds its
plugin, not the core or a front end:

# Kernel tweaks only rebuild the plugin DLL in seconds, not the core or front ends.
```powershell
cmake --build build/win32-sdl --config Release --target balls_physics_cuda
```

# Windows shadow copies avoid DLL file locking during rebuild and hot reload.
On Windows, every time a backend is selected the game loads a fresh shadow copy of its DLL from
`%TEMP%\balls\physics-plugins`, so a plugin can be rebuilt while the game runs and
picked up by selecting the backend again (or `/backend?kind=cuda` on the control API); Linux and macOS load
the libraries directly. Plugins implement `source/core/sim/PhysicsBackend.h` and see
`source/core/sim/kernels/BallStepCore.h`. The build uses the shared MSVC runtime and copies its DLLs next
to the executable.

The plugin libraries and the backend kinds in each

Four single-device plugins isolate hardware drivers, while composite backends live in core to orchestrate them.

Source: source/core/sim/BackendFactory.h, source/core/sim/BackendFactory.cpp

source/core/sim/BackendFactory.h lines 13-23
// Composites (Hybrid, CudaAvx2) live in core to orchestrate handles; CudaAvx2 supports BALLS_PREALLOCATE=1.
// CPU plugin houses Scalar, Avx2, and Zen4 (AVX-512); ROCm drives AMD GPUs via Linux HSA AQL dispatch.
// CudaAvx2 is the fixed CUDA + AVX2 split (HybridBackend over those two); it was called "Hybrid" before the
// general device composer took that name. Hybrid is the composer (DeviceComposer): one backend on each device the
// player picked (BackendSelection.h).
// New kinds go at the end: the values are compared and stored by name, but keep the old numbering stable anyway.
// Zen4 is the AVX-512 CPU backend; ROCm drives AMD GPUs through the HSA runtime; VulkanMulti is one Vulkan backend
// that spans several GPUs itself (unlike Hybrid, which composes one backend per device).
enum class BackendKind { Scalar, Avx2, Cuda, CudaAvx2, Vulkan, Hybrid, Zen4, Rocm, VulkanMulti };

// Numbering kept stable for comparisons; all 9 backends listed in standard evaluation order.
inline constexpr std::array<BackendKind, 9> allBackendKinds{
    BackendKind::Scalar, BackendKind::Avx2,   BackendKind::Zen4,        BackendKind::Cuda,  BackendKind::CudaAvx2,
    BackendKind::Rocm,   BackendKind::Vulkan, BackendKind::VulkanMulti, BackendKind::Hybrid};

Physics plugins on the web without dynamic linking

Browsers lack dlopen, so the web build links the scalar backend statically and runs inline on the animation frame.

Source: source/core/sim/BackendFactoryWeb.cpp, CMakeLists.txt

source/core/CMakeLists.txt lines 53-62
# Web lacks dlopen: ticks run single-threaded inline on UI animation frame via BALLS_STATIC_CPU_BACKEND.
# No dlopen and no plugin files in the browser: the scalar backend is linked in, so the pieces that load and combine
# plugins are left out and BackendFactoryWeb.cpp stands in for the plugin-aware factory.
if(EMSCRIPTEN)
    # Strips plugin loader, composite backends, and multi-device composers from web build.
    list(REMOVE_ITEM BALLS_CORE_SOURCES
        common/DynamicLibrary.cpp
        sim/HybridBackend.cpp
        sim/DeviceComposer.cpp
        sim/BackendFactory.cpp)
    # BackendFactoryWeb.cpp statically instantiates ScalarBackend with no dynamic resolution.
    list(APPEND BALLS_CORE_SOURCES sim/BackendFactoryWeb.cpp)
endif()

When a plugin fails to load or the GPU is missing

Plugin libraries and CPU/GPU prerequisites are checked up front so missing features fail cleanly without crashing.

Source: source/core/sim/BackendFactory.cpp, source/backends/cpu/CpuPlugin.cpp

source/backends/cpu/CpuPlugin.cpp lines 47-67
// Settings queries cached BackendStatus records; unavailable backends are greyed out with this reason.
BALLS_PLUGIN_EXPORT const char* ballsBackendUnavailableReason(const char* kindName) {
    const std::string_view kind(kindName);
    if (kind == scalarName) {
        return noReason;
    }
    // Checks both compile-time build flags and CPU runtime support for 8-lane AVX2.
    if (kind == avx2Name) {
#if defined(BALLS_HAS_AVX2_BACKEND)
        return Avx2Backend::isSupportedByCpu() ? noReason : missingAvx2;
#else
        return avx2NotBuilt;
#endif
    }
    // Validates runtime AVX-512 support to prevent illegal instruction crashes on older CPUs.
    if (kind == zen4Name) {
#if defined(BALLS_HAS_ZEN4_BACKEND)
        return Zen4Backend::isSupportedByCpu() ? noReason : missingZen4;
#else
        return zen4NotBuilt;
#endif
    }
    return unknownKind;
}

Plain arrays instead of domain objects across the plugin boundary

Board data crosses plugins as flat spans of 32-bit integers to prevent ABI coupling and allow direct DMA to GPUs.

Source: source/core/sim/PhysicsBackend.h, data/docs/physics-backends.md

source/core/sim/PhysicsBackend.h lines 80-94
// Passing BrickBoard directly couples ABIs; primitive spans let CUDA and Vulkan DMA buffers straight to GPU.
struct BoardUpload {
    int columns = 0;
    int rows = 0;
    std::span<const int32_t> paddedCells;
    std::span<const int32_t> brickColors;
    std::span<const int32_t> targetCells;
    std::span<const int32_t> nearBrickCells;
    std::span<const int32_t> brickHitsLeft;
    std::span<const int32_t> changedCells;
    bool onlyChangedCells = false;
    // Incremental (index, value) pairs sent instead of full arrays for hit bricks and background target rebuilds.
    // (index, value) pairs sent instead of the whole array to backends that accept them: the bricks the host hit, and
    // the target cells a finished target rebuild changed.
    std::span<const int32_t> brickHitUpdates;
    std::span<const int32_t> targetUpdates;
};

The plugin C ABI and memory ownership boundary

An unmangled C export ABI instead of exported C++ classes

Plugins export unmangled C functions returning interface pointers to guarantee binary stability across compilers.

Source: source/core/sim/PhysicsBackend.h

source/core/sim/PhysicsBackend.h lines 15-26
// extern "C" avoids compiler mangling differences when resolving via GetProcAddress or dlsym.
#if defined(_WIN32)
#define BALLS_PLUGIN_EXPORT extern "C" __declspec(dllexport)
#else
#define BALLS_PLUGIN_EXPORT extern "C" __attribute__((visibility("default")))
#endif

// Factory resolves C entry points that return PhysicsBackend pointers dispatched via vtable.
inline constexpr int physicsPluginInterfaceVersion = 5;

// Plain data layout ensures device descriptors cross the C ABI safely.
// One physical device a plugin can run a backend kind on, listed in the plugin's own order (the index that
// ballsCreateBackendOnDevice takes). Plain data, so it crosses the C boundary. The host matches one GPU across
// plugins by its UUID, which CUDA (cuDeviceGetUuid) and Vulkan (VkPhysicalDeviceIDProperties) report alike.
enum class PhysicsDeviceType : int32_t { Cpu, DiscreteGpu, IntegratedGpu, OtherGpu, Npu };

Plugin interface versioning against binary mismatches

The loader checks interface versions and required entry points to reject incompatible plugin builds before use.

Source: source/core/sim/PhysicsBackend.h, source/core/sim/BackendFactory.cpp, source/backends/cpu/CpuPlugin.cpp

source/core/sim/BackendFactory.cpp lines 175-198
// loadPlugin checks exported ballsPhysicsInterfaceVersion first to catch mismatched builds.
InterfaceVersionFunction interfaceVersion = nullptr;
const DynamicLibrary& library = *module->library;
// All ten core entry points must resolve; incomplete plugins are rejected immediately.
const bool complete = library.resolve("ballsPhysicsInterfaceVersion", interfaceVersion) &&
                      library.resolve("ballsCreateBackend", module->create) &&
                      library.resolve("ballsDestroyBackend", module->destroy) &&
                      library.resolve("ballsBackendUnavailableReason", module->unavailableReason) &&
                      library.resolve("ballsBackendDetail", module->detail) &&
                      library.resolve("ballsCreateBackendOnDevice", module->createOnDevice) &&
                      library.resolve("ballsDeviceCount", module->deviceCount) &&
                      library.resolve("ballsDeviceInfo", module->deviceInfo) &&
                      library.resolve("ballsDeviceUnavailableReason", module->deviceUnavailableReason) &&
                      library.resolve("ballsDeviceDetail", module->deviceDetail);
// Resolves up to five optional entry points for profiling and device management.
library.resolve("ballsMeasureRoofline", module->measureRoofline);
library.resolve("ballsStreamedBytesPerBall", module->streamedBytes);
library.resolve("ballsSetComputeGpu", module->setComputeGpu);
library.resolve("ballsReleaseIdleDevice", module->releaseIdleDevice);
library.resolve("ballsCreateBackendOnDevices", module->createOnDevices);
if (interfaceVersion && interfaceVersion() != physicsPluginInterfaceVersion) {
    module->error = std::format("{} was built for plugin interface {} (expected {})", fileName, interfaceVersion(),
                                physicsPluginInterfaceVersion);
} else if (!complete) {
    module->error = std::format("{} is missing plugin entry points", fileName);
}
return module;

Heap memory ownership across the DLL boundary

Backends are freed inside the plugin that allocated them to prevent Windows heap corruption across CRT boundaries.

Source: source/core/sim/BackendHandle.h, source/backends/cpu/CpuPlugin.cpp

source/core/sim/BackendHandle.h lines 8-19
// Calling plain delete across DLL boundaries corrupts heap; ballsDestroyBackend deletes inside the DLL.
struct BackendRelease {
    DestroyBackendFunction destroy = nullptr;
    // Keeps the DLL loaded until backend destruction finishes.
    std::shared_ptr<DynamicLibrary> library;

    void operator()(PhysicsBackend* backend) const {
        if (backend && destroy) {
            destroy(backend);
        }
    }
};

// BackendHandle bundles custom deleter for safe cross-DLL cleanup.
using BackendHandle = std::unique_ptr<PhysicsBackend, BackendRelease>;

Never unloading plugin libraries at process exit

Plugin DLLs are intentionally leaked at exit because unloading after GPU driver cleanup triggers shutdown crashes.

Source: source/core/sim/BackendFactory.cpp, 8033753a8

source/core/sim/BackendFactory.cpp lines 201-213
// Commit 8033753a8: unloading GPU DLLs at exit crashed when NVIDIA and RADV driver handlers ran first.
const PluginModule& queryPlugin(BackendKind kind) {
    static std::mutex mutex;
    // Leaked heap map intentionally prevents static destructor DLL unloading at shutdown.
    // Never destroyed: unloading a GPU plugin from a static destructor at exit closes its devices after the
    // drivers' own exit handlers ran, which crashes (seen with RADV and NVIDIA together).
    static auto& cache = *new std::map<std::string_view, std::shared_ptr<PluginModule>>();
    std::lock_guard lock(mutex);
    // Idle devices during gameplay are released via ballsReleaseIdleDevice instead of library unloads.
    const std::string_view fileName = backendPluginFileName(kind);
    auto& module = cache[fileName];
    if (!module) {
        module = loadPlugin(fileName, true);
    }
    return *module;
}

The Unity C ABI (`BallsUnityApi`) without leaking exceptions or types

A flat C interface passes primitive types to Unity and catches C++ exceptions before they cross into C#.

Source: source/plugin/BallsUnityApi.h, source/plugin/BallsUnityApi.cpp

source/plugin/BallsUnityApi.h lines 3-17
// Flat C API for Unity DllImport: primitives only, catches C++ exceptions into balls_last_error.
// Pointers reference core-owned memory valid until the next mutating simulation call.
// Flat C interface over the engine-agnostic core, for hosts whose own build system owns the main loop: the Unity
// front end (a native plugin loaded with DllImport) is the first user. Everything crosses the boundary as C types:
// no STL, no exceptions, no callbacks. Pointers handed out here point into core-owned memory and stay valid until
// the next call that mutates the same session (update, load, restart, backend switch), so a host copies them
// immediately or renders from them within that frame.

#include <stdint.h>

// Version guard verifies C# bindings match native binary ABI layout.
#define BALLS_UNITY_ABI_VERSION 7

#if defined(_WIN32)
#define BALLS_API __declspec(dllexport)
#else
#define BALLS_API __attribute__((visibility("default")))
#endif

Backend detection, device arbitration, and selection hierarchy

Detecting and enumerating devices across GPU APIs

The inventory unifies CPUID queries and GPU driver introspection into standardized device descriptors.

Source: source/core/sim/DeviceInventory.h, source/core/sim/DeviceInventory.cpp, source/backends/cuda/CudaPlugin.cpp

source/core/sim/DeviceInventory.cpp lines 59-77
std::string cpuBrandName() {
#if defined(_WIN32) || defined(__x86_64__) || defined(__i386__)
    // Extracts CPU brand string from CPUID leaves 0x80000002 through 0x80000004.
    constexpr unsigned firstBrandLeaf = 0x80000002u;
    constexpr unsigned brandLeafCount = 3;
    char brand[brandLeafCount * 16 + 1] = {};
    for (unsigned leaf = 0; leaf < brandLeafCount; ++leaf) {
        unsigned registers[4] = {};
#if defined(_WIN32)
        // CUDA/Vulkan query UUID and PCI; ROCm identifies devices by PCI location due to missing Linux Vulkan UUIDs.
        __cpuid(reinterpret_cast<int*>(registers), static_cast<int>(firstBrandLeaf + leaf));
#else
        __get_cpuid(firstBrandLeaf + leaf, &registers[0], &registers[1], &registers[2], &registers[3]);
#endif
        std::memcpy(brand + leaf * 16, registers, 16);
    }
    return trimmed(brand);
#else
    return "unknown CPU";
#endif
}

Deduplicating GPUs reported by several plugins

Duplicate GPU entries from multiple plugins are merged by UUID and PCI location into a single selectable device.

Source: source/core/sim/DeviceInventory.cpp, source/tests/DeviceTests.cpp

source/core/sim/DeviceInventory.cpp lines 94-110
// Consolidates adapters across plugins by 16-byte UUID or (domain, bus, device) PCI tuple (e.g. for ROCm).
std::array<uint8_t, 16> uuid{};
std::copy(std::begin(info.uuid), std::end(info.uuid), uuid.begin());
// Matches existing device so multi-API GPUs (e.g. RTX 2060 on CUDA + Vulkan) merge into one ID like gpu0.
// ROCm's agent UUID is not Vulkan's device UUID, so a GPU is also the same one at the same PCI location.
const auto same = std::ranges::find_if(gpus, [&](const GpuEntry& entry) {
    if (info.hasUuid != 0 && entry.device.hasUuid && entry.device.uuid == uuid) {
        return true;
    }
    return info.pciBus >= 0 && entry.pciLocation &&
           *entry.pciLocation == std::tuple{info.pciDomain, info.pciBus, info.pciDevice};
});
