// Backends ranked fastest first: Zen4, AVX2, Scalar on CPU; CUDA, ROCm, Vulkan on GPU.
if (same != gpus.end()) {
    same->device.backends.push_back(option);
    if (!same->pciLocation && info.pciBus >= 0) {
        same->pciLocation = std::tuple{info.pciDomain, info.pciBus, info.pciDevice};
    }
    continue;
}

Device ordering, identification and pinning

Hardware is assigned stable IDs (cpu, gpu0) and can be pinned by ID, name substring, or UUID prefix.

Source: source/core/sim/DeviceInventory.cpp, source/tests/DeviceTests.cpp

source/core/sim/DeviceInventory.cpp lines 162-185
// Devices are sorted deterministically: CPU is "cpu", GPUs sorted by PCI tuple become "gpu0", "gpu1".
const PhysicalDevice* findPhysicalDevice(std::string_view token, const std::vector<PhysicalDevice>& devices) {
    const std::string wanted = lowercase(trimmed(std::string(token)));
    if (wanted.empty()) {
        return nullptr;
    }
    for (const PhysicalDevice& device : devices) {
        if (device.id == wanted) {
            return &device;
        }
    }
    // Matches hex UUID prefixes of >= 8 chars, or case-insensitive name substrings (e.g. "6700").
    if (wanted.size() >= minimumUuidPrefixDigits) {
        for (const PhysicalDevice& device : devices) {
            if (device.hasUuid && uuidText(device.uuid).starts_with(wanted)) {
                return &device;
            }
        }
    }
    for (const PhysicalDevice& device : devices) {
        if (lowercase(device.name).find(wanted) != std::string::npos) {
            return &device;
        }
    }
    return nullptr;
}

The startup arbitration hierarchy in `chooseStartupBackend`

Startup arbitration evaluates CLI flags, saved settings, Hybrid, benchmark winners, then falls back to CPU.

Source: source/core/game/MachineProfile.cpp, source/tests/MachineProfileTests.cpp

source/core/game/MachineProfile.cpp lines 38-54
BackendKind chooseStartupBackend(std::optional<BackendKind> commandLine, std::string_view settingsDefault,
                                 const std::filesystem::path& targetRecordFile) {
    // Priority 1: CLI override (--backend=cuda) if available.
    if (commandLine && isBackendAvailable(*commandLine)) {
        return *commandLine;
    }
    const auto usable = [](BackendKind kind) { return isBackendAvailable(kind) && isBackendSelectable(kind); };
    // Priority 2: Saved user preference from settings.
    if (const auto saved = parseSavedBackendKind(settingsDefault); saved && usable(offeredBackend(*saved))) {
        return offeredBackend(*saved);
    }
    // Priority 3: Hybrid multi-device mode if >= 2 devices are available.
    if (usable(BackendKind::Hybrid)) {
        return BackendKind::Hybrid;
    }
    // Priority 4: Benchmark winner recorded for this machine fingerprint.
    if (const auto target = targetMachineWinner(targetRecordFile); target && usable(offeredBackend(target->kind))) {
        return offeredBackend(target->kind);
    }
    // Priority 5: Fallback to fastest CPU backend (Zen4 -> AVX2 -> Scalar).
    return fastestAvailableCpuBackend();
}

The backend pick grammar for Hybrid and VulkanMulti

Pick strings use + to compose backends across devices and | to group GPUs in VulkanMulti.

Source: source/core/sim/BackendSelection.h, source/core/sim/BackendSelection.cpp

source/core/sim/BackendSelection.cpp lines 77-125
// Grammar: "AVX2@cpu+CUDA@gpu0"; initial weights: CPU 0.05, iGPU 0.2, Vulkan 0.8, CUDA 1.0.
while (!trimmedView(text).empty()) {
    const size_t end = text.find(pickSeparator);
    const std::string_view item = text.substr(0, end);
    text = end == std::string_view::npos ? std::string_view{} : text.substr(end + 1);
    const size_t at = item.find(deviceSeparator);
    if (at == std::string_view::npos) {
        return std::nullopt;
    }
    const std::optional<BackendKind> kind = backendKindFromName(trimmedView(item.substr(0, at)));
    if (!kind) {
        return std::nullopt;
    }
    std::string deviceToken = std::string(trimmedView(item.substr(at + 1)));
    // VulkanMulti drives multiple GPUs from one backend; devices joined with '|' (e.g. VulkanMulti@gpu0|gpu1).
    if (*kind == BackendKind::VulkanMulti) {
        // A VulkanMulti pick may claim several devices separated by '|'.
        std::vector<std::string> claimed;
        size_t pos = 0;
        while (true) {
            const size_t next = deviceToken.find('|', pos);
            const std::string token = deviceToken.substr(pos, next == std::string::npos ? std::string::npos : next - pos);
            const PhysicalDevice* device = findPhysicalDevice(trimmedView(token));
            if (!device) {
                return std::nullopt;
            }
            claimed.push_back(device->id);
            if (next == std::string::npos) {
                break;
            }
            pos = next + 1;
        }
        if (claimed.size() < 2) {
            return std::nullopt;
        }
        std::string joined;
        for (size_t i = 0; i < claimed.size(); ++i) {
            if (i) {
                joined += '|';
            }
            joined += claimed[i];
        }
        picks.push_back({*kind, joined});
    } else {
        const PhysicalDevice* device = findPhysicalDevice(deviceToken);
        if (!device) {
            return std::nullopt;
        }
        picks.push_back({*kind, device->id});
    }
}

Hot reload, shadow copies, and off-frame compilation

Shadow copies of physics plugins on Windows

Windows locks open DLLs, so the engine runs shadow copies from %TEMP% to allow recompiling during gameplay.

Source: source/core/sim/BackendFactory.cpp

source/core/sim/BackendFactory.cpp lines 127-138
// Windows locks loaded DLLs; stamped copies in %TEMP%\balls\physics-plugins let source DLL rebuild.
std::filesystem::path makeShadowCopy(const std::filesystem::path& source) {
    static std::atomic<unsigned int> copyCounter = 0;
    removeStaleShadowCopies();
    std::error_code error;
    std::filesystem::create_directories(shadowDirectory(), error);
    // Stamped filename with atomic counter prevents naming collisions across reloads.
    const auto stamp = std::filesystem::last_write_time(source, error).time_since_epoch().count();
    const std::filesystem::path target =
        shadowDirectory() / std::format("{}-{}-{}{}", source.stem().string(), stamp, copyCounter++,
                                        source.extension().string());
    std::filesystem::copy_file(source, target, std::filesystem::copy_options::overwrite_existing, error);
    return error ? source : target;
}

In-game hot reload during play

New plugin DLLs load in the background and live balls migrate before retiring the old backend, avoiding pauses.

Source: source/core/sim/BackendFactory.cpp, data/docs/physics-backends.md

source/core/sim/BackendFactory.cpp lines 127-138
// Backend switch or /backend?kind=cuda forces a fresh shadow copy while old backend steps uninterrupted.
std::filesystem::path makeShadowCopy(const std::filesystem::path& source) {
    static std::atomic<unsigned int> copyCounter = 0;
    removeStaleShadowCopies();
    std::error_code error;
    std::filesystem::create_directories(shadowDirectory(), error);
    // Old DLL stays in memory until retired BackendHandle releases its shared_ptr<DynamicLibrary>.
    const auto stamp = std::filesystem::last_write_time(source, error).time_since_epoch().count();
    const std::filesystem::path target =
        shadowDirectory() / std::format("{}-{}-{}{}", source.stem().string(), stamp, copyCounter++,
                                        source.extension().string());
    std::filesystem::copy_file(source, target, std::filesystem::copy_options::overwrite_existing, error);
    return error ? source : target;
}

`BackendWorker`: creating and retiring backends off the frame

A background worker compiles GPU pipelines and frees gigabytes of VRAM off the main thread to prevent frame stutters.

Source: source/core/sim/BackendWorker.h, source/core/sim/BackendWorker.cpp

source/core/sim/BackendWorker.h lines 38-57
// Loading a plugin, opening a GPU device and compiling kernels take from tens of milliseconds to seconds, and
// freeing gigabytes of device memory is not free either, so neither may happen on the frame. A single process-wide
// thread does both, in the order they were asked for.
// Dedicated background thread handles multi-second GPU initialization, shader compilation, and VRAM freeing.
class BackendWorker {
public:
    static BackendWorker& shared();

    // Returns polled BackendBuild token; BackendBuild::abandon() discards it if user switches again before done.
    std::shared_ptr<BackendBuild> build(BackendKind kind);
    // Runs destructor and multi-gigabyte VRAM deallocation off physics and render threads.
    void retire(BackendHandle backend);
    void run(std::function<void()> job);

private:
    BackendWorker();
    void run();

    std::mutex mutex;
    std::condition_variable wake;
    std::deque<std::function<void()>> jobs;
    std::thread thread;
};

Migrating live balls across backends without memory exhaustion or hitches

Live balls migrate in chunks between backends, stopping cleanly on capacity limits and respawning remainder over time.

Source: source/core/sim/Simulation.cpp, source/core/game/GameSession.cpp

source/core/sim/Simulation.cpp lines 68-104
// Runs on the physics thread, where it only delays the next tick: the UI keeps drawing the last published frame. Every
// ball is streamed across with its position and velocity, so the new backend continues the same simulation. Only
// when the new backend cannot hold them all are the balls it does not have handed back to be launched again: when it
// says so up front, or when partway through it runs out of host memory, refuses a chunk or faults, as the Vulkan
// backend does when device memory runs out. What it holds is counted, not what was sent.
// Moves balls in chunkBalls batches via takeTail/addBalls to avoid host or device OOM on millions of balls.
BackendReplacement Simulation::replaceBackend(BackendHandle backend, size_t chunkBalls) {
    BackendReplacement replacement{std::move(physics), 0};
    physics = std::move(backend);
    uploadedRevision = ~0ull;
    targetsUploadPending = true;
    hitsUploadPending = true;
    syncBoardIfChanged();
    physics->setBallCollisions(ballCollisionsWanted);
    PhysicsBackend& previous = *replacement.retired;
    const size_t total = previous.ballCount();
    // Skips migration if total balls exceed destination capacity; schedules all balls to refill.
    if (total > physics->ballCapacityLimit()) {
        replacement.ballsToRefill = total;
        return replacement;
    }
    // Halts migration cleanly on capacity limit, std::bad_alloc, or GPU backend fault.
    while (previous.ballCount() > 0 && physics->faultMessage().empty()) {
        const std::vector<BallSpawn> chunk = previous.takeTail(std::min(previous.ballCount(), chunkBalls));
        if (chunk.empty()) {
            break;
        }
        const size_t expected = physics->ballCount() + chunk.size();
        try {
            physics->addBalls(chunk);
        } catch (const std::bad_alloc&) {
            break;
        }
        if (physics->ballCount() != expected) {
            break;
        }
    }
    // Unmigrated balls are smoothly respawned over subsequent ticks by refillBatch.
    replacement.ballsToRefill = total - std::min(physics->ballCount(), total);
    return replacement;
}

Recovering from a GPU fault or device loss

When a GPU backend faults or loses device access, the engine synchronously rescues simulation onto CPU without crash.

Source: source/core/game/GameSession.cpp

source/core/game/GameSession.cpp lines 447-454
// Monitored every update: catches device loss, OOM aborts, or invalid compute shader execution.
void GameSession::recoverFromBackendFault() {
    const std::string_view fault = physicsSimulation.backend().faultMessage();
    if (fault.empty()) {
        return;
    }
    backendFault = std::string(fault);
    // Synchronously rescues onto CPU; bypasses BackendWorker since dead GPU cannot step during background builds.
    switchBackendNow(fastestAvailableCpuBackend());
}

The physics thread, UI thread, and data handoff

Separate physics and rendering threads

Physics runs on a dedicated thread at fixed tick rates so heavy simulation never blocks UI rendering or input.

Source: source/core/game/SessionRunner.h, source/core/game/SessionRunner.cpp

source/core/game/SessionRunner.h lines 49-86
// Runs a GameSession on its own thread so that nothing the simulation does, from a slow physics step to building a
// backend, can hold up a UI frame. The UI posts commands, which run at the start of the next physics frame, and
// draws the latest SessionView. The two are not synchronised: the physics thread keeps its own pace of one frame
// per simulation tick, and the UI draws whichever tick is newest at its own frame rate.
// Physics steps at fixed rate on dedicated thread while UI renders latest view at display refresh rate.
class SessionRunner {
public:
    using Command = std::function<void(GameSession&)>;

    SessionRunner(GameMode mode, LevelData level, BackendKind backend, int ticksPerSecond = defaultTicksPerSecond);
    // Reads the level on the physics thread too, so even parsing a huge map leaves the UI free to draw the loading
    // screen from `progress`; takeLoadOutcome() says how the read went.
    // Huge map parsing runs on physics thread, leaving UI free to render animated loading screen.
    SessionRunner(GameMode mode, LevelFiles files, BackendKind backend, int ticksPerSecond,
                  std::shared_ptr<LoadProgress> progress);
    // Does not wait for the physics thread: it finishes its frame, destroys the session and exits on its own.
    ~SessionRunner();
    SessionRunner(const SessionRunner&) = delete;
    SessionRunner& operator=(const SessionRunner&) = delete;

    void post(Command command);
    // GameSession::setBallCollisions on the physics thread; anything other than None comes back as an event.
    void setBallCollisions(bool enabled);
    // Called once per UI frame with what the physics thread needs from the UI; it does not wait for or wake it.
    // UI pushes input and UI work duration per frame without waiting for or waking the physics thread.
    void tick(const PhysicsFrameInput& input);
    // Null until the physics thread has built the session.
    std::shared_ptr<const SessionView> view() const;
    std::vector<SessionEvent> takeEvents();
    // Once, after a runner given LevelFiles has read them; empty before then and for a runner given the level.
    std::optional<LevelLoadOutcome> takeLoadOutcome();

    // Waits, up to the timeout, for the physics threads of destroyed runners to finish; used when the game quits.
    static bool waitForStoppedRunners(std::chrono::milliseconds timeout);

    // The state the runner and its physics thread share; the thread keeps it alive after the runner is gone.
    struct Shared;

private:
    std::shared_ptr<Shared> shared;
};

Triple-buffered view publication in `SessionRunner`

A pool of three session views lets the physics thread publish state atomically without locking the UI thread.

Source: source/core/game/SessionRunner.cpp, source/core/game/SessionView.h

source/core/game/SessionRunner.cpp lines 81-99
// Triple-buffered pool (viewPoolSize = 3) avoids mutex locks between physics and UI rendering.
std::shared_ptr<SessionView> freeView(std::array<std::shared_ptr<SessionView>, viewPoolSize>& pool,
                                      const std::shared_ptr<const SessionView>& latest) {
    std::shared_ptr<SessionView> chosen;
    for (std::shared_ptr<SessionView>& view : pool) {
        if (!view) {
            view = std::make_shared<SessionView>();
        }
        // One reference is the pool's own; a second is the latest view or a frame the UI is still drawing.
        // Idle view has use_count == 1 (only pool holds it) and is not currently published as latest.
        if (view.use_count() == 1 && view != latest) {
            // An idle view must not pin a board: BoardSnapshots has two, and with one held here and the other by the
            // latest view it has none to bring up to date, so the UI kept drawing bricks long since broken.
            view->board.reset();
            if (!chosen) {
                chosen = view;
            }
        }
    }
    // Allocates fallback view if UI holds all pool views concurrently.
    return chosen ? chosen : std::make_shared<SessionView>();
}

The bug where idle session views pinned brick boards

Idle pool views pinned shared brick board pointers, starving follower boards and freezing UI brick updates for 16s.

Source: source/core/game/SessionRunner.cpp, 7e8012d9b

source/core/game/SessionRunner.cpp lines 81-99
// Commit 7e8012d9b: idle views pinned BrickBoard, starving follower boards in BoardSnapshots.
std::shared_ptr<SessionView> freeView(std::array<std::shared_ptr<SessionView>, viewPoolSize>& pool,
                                      const std::shared_ptr<const SessionView>& latest) {
    std::shared_ptr<SessionView> chosen;
    for (std::shared_ptr<SessionView>& view : pool) {
        if (!view) {
            view = std::make_shared<SessionView>();
        }
        // One reference is the pool's own; a second is the latest view or a frame the UI is still drawing.
        if (view.use_count() == 1 && view != latest) {
            // An idle view must not pin a board: BoardSnapshots has two, and with one held here and the other by the
            // latest view it has none to bring up to date, so the UI kept drawing bricks long since broken.
            // On DA_1000Bricks, UI froze unbroken bricks for 16s (count jumped 920 -> 442); reset() frees follower.
            view->board.reset();
            if (!chosen) {
                chosen = view;
            }
        }
    }
    return chosen ? chosen : std::make_shared<SessionView>();
}

`BoardSnapshots`: no per-frame board copies across threads

BoardSnapshots uses change logs and two follower boards, cutting 8192x8192 sync times from 53s to sub-millisecond.

Source: source/core/game/BoardSnapshots.h, source/core/game/BoardSnapshots.cpp, 4705f5628

source/core/game/BoardSnapshots.cpp lines 5-29
// Commit 4705f5628: 8192x8192 deep copy took 53s; two follower boards with change logs cut sync to < 1 ms.
std::shared_ptr<const BrickBoard> BoardSnapshots::publish(const BrickBoard& live,
                                                          const std::shared_ptr<const BrickBoard>& previous) {
    const bool previousIsOurs = previous && std::ranges::any_of(boards, [&](const auto& board) { return board == previous; });
    if (previousIsOurs && previous->layoutSerial() == live.layoutSerial() &&
        previous->followedPosition() == live.changeLogEnd()) {
        return previous;
    }
    // Ours is the only reference to a board the UI no longer holds.
    const auto free = std::ranges::find_if(boards, [](const auto& board) { return !board || board.use_count() == 1; });
    if (free == boards.end()) {
        return previous;
    }
    if (!*free) {
        *free = std::make_shared<BrickBoard>();
    }
    // Replays incremental edits; full copies only on level load or > 64M unread change log entries.
    (*free)->catchUpWith(live);
    uint64_t oldest = (*free)->followedPosition();
    for (const auto& board : boards) {
        if (board && board->layoutSerial() == live.layoutSerial()) {
            oldest = std::min(oldest, board->followedPosition());
        }
    }
    // Trims change log to oldest follower position across active views to reclaim memory.
    live.trimChangeLog(oldest);
    return *free;
}

Packing ball positions and colours for front-end rendering

Ball positions and colors pack into 32-bit words, with a visibility grid capping density to one ball per pixel.

Source: source/core/sim/PhysicsBackend.h, source/core/game/SessionRunner.cpp

source/core/sim/PhysicsBackend.h lines 60-69
// Packs render state into 32 bits: 14 bits X, 14 bits Y, 3 bits color index; 0xFFFFFFFF marks empty slot.
inline constexpr unsigned int compactColorShift = 26u;
inline constexpr unsigned int compactColorMask = 7u;

// Visibility grid (1 slot/radius) limits 60M balls to screen pixels, keeping frame transfer under a few MB.
inline uint32_t packCompactForRender(uint32_t position, uint32_t state) {
    constexpr uint32_t axisMask = 0xFFFFu;
    constexpr uint32_t positionToRenderShift = 2u;
    return ((position & axisMask) >> positionToRenderShift) |
           ((position >> 16 >> positionToRenderShift) << renderCoordinateBits) |
           (((state >> compactColorShift) & compactColorMask) << renderColorShift);
}

`SessionHost`: single-threaded and multithreaded execution behind one interface

SessionHost wraps threaded and synchronous execution behind one interface so Unreal can toggle threading at runtime.

Source: source/core/game/SessionHost.h, source/core/game/SessionHost.cpp

source/core/game/SessionHost.cpp lines 66-90
// Used by Unreal to toggle multithreaded and single-threaded modes at runtime via -BallsPhysicsThread=0.
void SessionHost::tick(const PhysicsFrameInput& input, float deltaSeconds) {
    if (runner) {
        runner->tick(input);
        if (auto newest = runner->view()) {
            latest = std::move(newest);
        }
        for (SessionEvent& event : runner->takeEvents()) {
            events.push_back(event);
        }
        return;
    }
    // Single-threaded fallback: polls background builds and updates GameSession directly on UI thread.
    // SessionRunner's order: adopt a backend built off the frame before the tick that would run on it.
    session->pollBackgroundWork();
    if (const auto outcome = session->takeBackendSwitchOutcome()) {
        events.push_back({outcome->succeeded ? SessionEventKind::BackendSwitched : SessionEventKind::BackendFailed,
                          outcome->kind, outcome->forBallCollisions});
    }
    if (!input.paused) {
        const PhysicsRate::Clock::time_point workStart = PhysicsRate::Clock::now();
        session->reportFrameWorkSeconds(input.uiWorkSeconds);
        session->update(deltaSeconds);
        rate.frame(workStart, PhysicsRate::Clock::now(), session->simulation().stats().ticksLastFrame);
    }
    refreshView();
}

Frame pacing and timing measurement

`FrameTimings`: measuring rendering, and `workMs` versus `frameMs`

Separating active computation (workMs) from presentation waits (frameMs) prevents VSync from skewing metrics.

Source: source/core/game/FrameTimings.h, source/core/game/FrameTimings.cpp, source/tests/FrameTimingsTests.cpp

source/core/game/FrameTimings.h lines 5-21
// One rendered frame as a front end measured it. Fields a front end cannot measure stay 0.
// Rolling 1s window: workMs measures active compute, while frameMs includes VSync/compositor sleep.
struct FrameSample {
    double frameMs = 0.0;
    // What the frame costs the machine when the display never makes it wait: what ball filling uses, so VSync,
    // a frame cap or a Remote Desktop compositor do not cap what it measures. Each front end knows its own waits.
    double workMs = 0.0;
    // CPU time of the frame (SDL), or the game thread (Unreal).
    double cpuMs = 0.0;
    // The render thread, where the engine has one.
    double renderMs = 0.0;
    double gpuMs = 0.0;
    double presentMs = 0.0;
    // 1 when the frame reached the screen, 0 when the compositor was not ready; averages to the shown share.
    // Average of 1 (shown) and 0 (compositor not ready) reflects frames actually presented.
    double presented = 1.0;
    // Time spent waiting rather than working: SDL's present wait, Unreal's frame-rate cap sleep.
    double waitMs = 0.0;
};

`PhysicsRate`: measuring tick throughput in ticks, not frames

PhysicsRate measures simulation throughput in ticks over 0.5s windows and counts frames exceeding 16.67ms (60 FPS).

Source: source/core/game/PhysicsRate.h, source/core/game/SessionRunner.cpp

source/core/game/PhysicsRate.h lines 6-53
// The debug gadget's Physics ms and Tick rate: how long the last physics frame took, and how many simulation ticks
// ran per second, averaged over half a second. A physics frame is not a picture: it runs however many ticks the
// fixed tick rate owes (two at the Physics frames setting's 60 with 120 ticks), so the meter counts ticks, which
// is the simulation's real speed. One meter for every way a session runs (its own physics thread, a front end's
// game thread, the Unity plugin), so they all read the same.
// Tracks simulation ticks over 0.5s sliding window; physics tick rate decoupled from display frames.
class PhysicsRate {
public:
    using Clock = std::chrono::steady_clock;

    // A physics frame that started at start, finished at end and ran this many simulation ticks.
    void frame(Clock::time_point start, Clock::time_point end, int ticks) {
        lastSeconds = std::chrono::duration<double>(end - start).count();
        longestSeconds = std::max(longestSeconds, lastSeconds);
        slowFrames += lastSeconds > slowFrameSeconds ? 1 : 0;
        ++framesInWindow;
        ticksInWindow += ticks;
        if (const double windowSeconds = std::chrono::duration<double>(end - windowStart).count();
            windowSeconds >= windowLengthSeconds) {
            perSecond = static_cast<double>(framesInWindow) / windowSeconds;
            ticksPerSecondValue = static_cast<double>(ticksInWindow) / windowSeconds;
            framesInWindow = 0;
            ticksInWindow = 0;
            windowStart = end;
        }
    }

    double lastFrameSeconds() const { return lastSeconds; }
    double lastFrameMs() const { return lastSeconds * millisecondsPerSecond; }
    double framesPerSecond() const { return perSecond; }
    double ticksPerSecond() const { return ticksPerSecondValue; }
    // Since the session started: its longest frame, and how many frames took longer than 1/60 s.
    double longestFrameMs() const { return longestSeconds * millisecondsPerSecond; }
    long long slowFrameCount() const { return slowFrames; }

private:
    static constexpr double windowLengthSeconds = 0.5;
    static constexpr double millisecondsPerSecond = 1000.0;
    // Flags frames exceeding 16.67 ms (1/60s) to count dropped real-time steps.
    static constexpr double slowFrameSeconds = 1.0 / 60.0;

    Clock::time_point windowStart = Clock::now();
    int framesInWindow = 0;
    long long ticksInWindow = 0;
    double perSecond = 0.0;
    double ticksPerSecondValue = 0.0;
    double lastSeconds = 0.0;
    double longestSeconds = 0.0;
    long long slowFrames = 0;
};

`AdditionGovernor` and spawning without frame hitches

The addition governor budgets ball spawns per frame to stay within 16.6ms, queuing excess balls to prevent stutters.

Source: source/core/game/AdditionGovernor.h, source/core/sim/Simulation.cpp

source/core/game/AdditionGovernor.cpp lines 55-69
// Commit b382cf33a: charges front-end UI work time against the 60 FPS (16.6 ms) frame budget.
double AdditionGovernor::additionSeconds(BallCount liveBalls, bool freeFlight) const {
    const double frameBudgetSeconds = (freeFlight ? freeFlightBudgetShare : steppingBudgetShare) / targetFramesPerSecond;
    const double chargedOtherSeconds = std::min(otherSeconds, frameBudgetSeconds * largestOtherShare);
    return std::max(0.0, frameBudgetSeconds - chargedOtherSeconds - stepSecondsPerBall() * static_cast<double>(liveBalls));
}

// Limits spawns to spare budget; excess balls stay queued in LauncherQueue to avoid frame drops.
BallCount AdditionGovernor::allowance(BallCount liveBalls, bool freeFlight) const {
    const double spare = additionSeconds(liveBalls, freeFlight);
    if (spare <= 0.0) {
        return 0;
    }
    const double balls = spare / (stepSecondsPerBall() + secondsPerAddedBall);
    const BallCount most = std::max(liveBalls, smallestScaledBalls);
    return balls >= static_cast<double>(most) ? most : static_cast<BallCount>(balls);
}

`SortSchedule`: pacing GPU spatial cell sorting

SortSchedule dynamically benchmarks radix sort intervals to balance GPU memory coherence against overhead.

Source: source/core/sim/SortSchedule.h, data/docs/gpu-compute-apis.md

source/core/sim/SortSchedule.h lines 7-32
// Benchmarks intervals (8, 16, 32 ticks) every 1200 ticks (16 warmup/32 measured); skips empty boards.
class SortSchedule {
public:
    static constexpr std::array<int, 3> intervalCandidates{8, 16, 32};
    static constexpr int warmupTicks = 16;
    static constexpr int measuredTicks = 32;
    static constexpr int ticksBetweenTrials = 1200;
    static constexpr size_t minimumBalls = size_t{1} << 20;
    static constexpr size_t bulkAddDenominator = 32;

    // Disables sorting under 1M balls (1 << 20) where memory coherence yields no benefit.
    bool sortThisTick(size_t balls) {
        if (balls < minimumBalls || intervalTicks <= 0 || ++ticksSinceSort < intervalTicks) {
            return false;
        }
        ticksSinceSort = 0;
        return true;
    }

    // Resets trial on bulk arrivals from device composers to avoid charging unsorted arrivals to candidate.
    // Balls appended in bulk (a composer moving them in from another device) arrive unsorted and change the set, so
    // a trial measuring meanwhile would charge the arrival to its candidate: that candidate is measured again.
    // Spawns in play are far below the threshold.
    void ballsAdded(size_t added, size_t balls) {
        if (ticksUntilTrial == 0 && added * bulkAddDenominator >= balls) {
            trialTick = 0;
            trialMsPerBall = 0.0;
        }
    }

Pausing without disturbing timing metrics or ball positions

On unpause, the runner zeros the first frame delta and caps delta time to 100ms to prevent simulation explosions.

Source: source/core/game/SessionRunner.h, source/core/game/SessionRunner.cpp, source/tests/SessionRunnerTests.cpp

source/core/game/SessionRunner.cpp lines 203-213
// Paused runner still publishes views so UI animations and highlights update without advancing physics.
const double sinceLastUpdate = secondsBetween(state.lastUpdate, workStart);
state.lastUpdate = workStart;
int ticks = 0;
// On unpause, wasPaused zeroes delta; maximumPhysicsFrameSeconds clamps deltas to 100 ms on debugger breaks.
if (!input.paused) {
    // Coming out of a pause, the paused time is not simulated.
    const double frameSeconds = state.wasPaused ? 0.0 : std::min(sinceLastUpdate, maximumPhysicsFrameSeconds);
    session.reportFrameWorkSeconds(std::max(state.rate.lastFrameSeconds(), input.uiWorkSeconds));
    session.update(static_cast<float>(frameSeconds));
    ticks = session.simulation().stats().ticksLastFrame;
}
state.wasPaused = input.paused;

Machine benchmark and capacity estimation

The machine benchmark and its candidate backends

The benchmark tests all single-device backends and multi-device combos to find the highest ball capacity at 60 FPS.

Source: source/core/sim/BackendBenchmark.h, source/core/sim/BackendBenchmark.cpp

source/core/sim/BackendBenchmark.cpp lines 164-208
// Evaluates candidates sequentially on background worker; cancel() halts safely and keeps partial results.
std::vector<BenchmarkCandidate> benchmarkCandidates() {
    constexpr size_t maximumComposedDevices = 6;
    std::vector<BenchmarkCandidate> candidates;
    for (const BackendKind kind : selectableBackends()) {
        if (kind != BackendKind::Hybrid) {
            candidates.push_back({kind, {}, candidateLabel(kind, {}), {}});
        }
    }
    const std::vector<PhysicalDevice>& devices = physicalDevices();
    std::vector<BackendPick> fastest;
    for (const PhysicalDevice& device : devices) {
        bool first = true;
        for (const BackendKind kind : pickableKinds(device)) {
            const DeviceBackendOption* option = device.option(kind);
            if (!option || !deviceBackendStatus(kind, option->pluginDevice).available) {
                continue;
            }
            if (first) {
                fastest.push_back({kind, device.id});
                first = false;
            }
            const PhysicalDevice* defaultDevice = defaultDeviceFor(kind);
            if (defaultDevice && defaultDevice->id != device.id) {
                const std::vector<BackendPick> single{{kind, device.id}};
                candidates.push_back({kind, {}, picksCandidateLabel(single), formatBackendPicks(single)});
            }
        }
    }
    // Generates all 2^N - 1 multi-device combinations across up to 6 physical devices.
    if (fastest.size() >= 2 && fastest.size() <= maximumComposedDevices) {
        for (uint32_t mask = 1; mask < (1u << fastest.size()); ++mask) {
            if (std::popcount(mask) < 2) {
                continue;
            }
            std::vector<BackendPick> picks;
            for (size_t index = 0; index < fastest.size(); ++index) {
                if ((mask >> index) & 1u) {
                    picks.push_back(fastest[index]);
                }
            }
            candidates.push_back(
                {BackendKind::Hybrid, {}, picksCandidateLabel(picks), formatBackendPicks(picks)});
        }
    }
    return candidates;
}

What invalidates a benchmark record, and the `HardwareFingerprint`

Benchmark records store CPU, GPU driver versions, and game revision to trigger re-benchmarking when hardware changes.

Source: source/core/sim/BackendBenchmark.h, source/core/sim/BackendBenchmark.cpp, source/tests/BenchmarkTests.cpp

source/core/sim/BackendBenchmark.h lines 16-25
// What the machine benchmark ran on. A change in any field (new GPU, driver update, game update) means the stored
// scores no longer describe this machine until Settings > Run machine benchmark measures it again.
// Stored scores invalidate if CPU cores, GPU driver strings, or game version change.
struct HardwareFingerprint {
    std::string cpuModel;
    unsigned logicalCores = 0;
    std::vector<std::string> gpus;  // "<name> | <driver version>", in enumeration order
    std::string gameVersion;

    // benchmarkNeeded flags re-benchmarking whenever stored fingerprint != currentHardwareFingerprint().
    bool operator==(const HardwareFingerprint&) const = default;
};

`estimateBallsAtTarget`: extrapolating capacity from two measurements

Frame times at 1M and 4M balls fit a linear slope to estimate the maximum balls fitting the 13.33ms physics budget.

Source: source/core/sim/BackendBenchmark.h, source/core/sim/BackendBenchmark.cpp

source/core/sim/BackendBenchmark.cpp lines 260-274
// Samples at 1M and 4M balls (0.5 s); extrapolates max balls fitting 13.33 ms (80% of 1/60 s).
double estimateBallsAtTarget(double smallBalls, double smallFrameSeconds, double largeBalls, double largeFrameSeconds,
                             double frameBudgetSeconds, double capacity) {
    // A stepped backend's frame grows almost in proportion to the count (2.6x to 3.3x for 4x the balls); one whose
    // balls fly freely only draws sampled bidders and stays within noise of flat. Fitting a line through two
    // nearly equal noisy times would extrapolate noise, so anything growing less than this is capacity-limited.
    constexpr double flatGrowth = 1.5;
    const double addedBalls = largeBalls - smallBalls;
    if (addedBalls <= 0.0 || largeFrameSeconds <= smallFrameSeconds * flatGrowth) {
        return largeFrameSeconds <= frameBudgetSeconds ? capacity : 0.0;
    }
    const double perBall = (largeFrameSeconds - smallFrameSeconds) / addedBalls;
    const double fixed = std::max(0.0, largeFrameSeconds - perBall * largeBalls);
    const double reachable = fixed < frameBudgetSeconds ? (frameBudgetSeconds - fixed) / perBall : 0.0;
    return std::min(reachable, capacity);
}

Compute-bound versus memory-limited backends in the benchmark

When frame time grows by < 1.5x between 1M and 4M balls, backends are classified as capacity-limited, not compute-bound.

Source: source/core/sim/BackendBenchmark.cpp, source/tests/BenchmarkTests.cpp

source/core/sim/BackendBenchmark.cpp lines 260-274
double estimateBallsAtTarget(double smallBalls, double smallFrameSeconds, double largeBalls, double largeFrameSeconds,
                             double frameBudgetSeconds, double capacity) {
    // A stepped backend's frame grows almost in proportion to the count (2.6x to 3.3x for 4x the balls); one whose
    // balls fly freely only draws sampled bidders and stays within noise of flat. Fitting a line through two
    // nearly equal noisy times would extrapolate noise, so anything growing less than this is capacity-limited.
    // If growth <= 1.5x, avoids fitting noisy flat times; returns full capacity if within budget, else 0.
    constexpr double flatGrowth = 1.5;
    const double addedBalls = largeBalls - smallBalls;
    if (addedBalls <= 0.0 || largeFrameSeconds <= smallFrameSeconds * flatGrowth) {
        return largeFrameSeconds <= frameBudgetSeconds ? capacity : 0.0;
    }
    const double perBall = (largeFrameSeconds - smallFrameSeconds) / addedBalls;
    const double fixed = std::max(0.0, largeFrameSeconds - perBall * largeBalls);
    const double reachable = fixed < frameBudgetSeconds ? (frameBudgetSeconds - fixed) / perBall : 0.0;
    return std::min(reachable, capacity);
}

Choosing the benchmark winner, and the fallback rules

The benchmark picks the backend sustaining the highest ball capacity at 60 FPS, breaking ties by step throughput.

Source: source/core/sim/BackendBenchmark.cpp, source/tests/BenchmarkTests.cpp

source/core/sim/BackendBenchmark.cpp lines 214-229
// Candidates ranked by estimatedBalls at 60 FPS to maximize sustained balls; excludes failed backends.
std::optional<size_t> bestCandidate(std::span<const CandidateResult> results) {
    std::optional<size_t> best;
    const auto better = [](const CandidateResult& challenger, const CandidateResult& holder) {
        // Ties broken by ballStepsPerSecond throughput.
        if (challenger.estimatedBalls != holder.estimatedBalls) {
            return challenger.estimatedBalls > holder.estimatedBalls;
        }
        return challenger.ballStepsPerSecond > holder.ballStepsPerSecond;
    };
    for (size_t index = 0; index < results.size(); ++index) {
        const CandidateResult& result = results[index];
        if (result.status == CandidateStatus::Done && (!best || better(result, results[*best]))) {
            best = index;
        }
    }
    return best;
}

Key facts

  • The plugin interface version is physicsPluginInterfaceVersion = 5, exported via ballsPhysicsInterfaceVersion().
  • The Unity C ABI version is BALLS_UNITY_ABI_VERSION = 7 in source/plugin/BallsUnityApi.h.
  • Every physics plugin shared library exports 10 required C entry points and up to 5 optional entry points.
  • Windows locks loaded DLL files, requiring makeShadowCopy to duplicate plugin binaries into %TEMP%\balls\physics-plugins.
  • Plugin module cache is deliberately leaked via *new std::map<...> to prevent static destruction crashes with GPU drivers at process exit.
  • BackendHandle is std::unique_ptr<PhysicsBackend, BackendRelease>, ensuring DLLs remain mapped until backend destruction completes.
  • GPU devices are deduplicated across CUDA and Vulkan by their 16-byte UUID (or PCI domain/bus/device tuple) and sorted by PCI location.
  • Startup arbitration hierarchy follows: command line, saved settings, Hybrid (if >= 2 devices), target benchmark winner, fastest CPU backend.
  • SessionRunner::Shared uses a 3-view buffer pool (viewPoolSize = 3) so rendering holds an immutable snapshot without stalling physics.
  • Commit 7e8012d9b fixed a bug where idle session views pinned follower boards, causing stale brick rendering for up to 16 seconds.
  • Commit 4705f5628 replaced full board copies with change log replay in BoardSnapshots, preventing 53-second frame stalls on 8192x8192 boards.
  • BackendBenchmark uses estimateBallsAtTarget with a 1.5x flat growth threshold to separate compute limits from memory capacity limits.
  • FrameTimings tracks a rolling 1-second window (windowLengthMs = 1000.0), separating active workMs from display waits.
  • PhysicsRate averages tick throughput over 0.5-second windows (windowLengthSeconds = 0.5) and counts ticks rather than frames.

5 · CPU physics

Collision model: ball-vs-wall and ball-vs-brick

Ball-vs-wall detection and specular reflection

Specular reflection mirrors out-of-bounds balls and forces velocity inward to keep them strictly inside the arena.

Source: source/core/sim/kernels/BallStepCore.h

source/core/sim/kernels/BallStepCore.h lines 216-233
const float radius = params.radius;
x += vx * params.deltaTime;
y += vy * params.deltaTime;

// Wall bounces increment consecutive bounce counter to trigger retargeting at retargetBounceThreshold.
float normalX = 0.0f;
float normalY = 0.0f;
bool bounced = false;
// Exact specular reflection mirrors coordinates back into valid territory.
if (x < radius) {
    x = 2.0f * radius - x;
    // fabsf guarantees inward velocity; multiplying by -1 can vibrate if rounding leaves ball outside.
    vx = fabsf(vx);
    normalX = 1.0f;
    bounced = true;
} else if (x > params.fieldWidth - radius) {
    x = 2.0f * (params.fieldWidth - radius) - x;
    vx = -fabsf(vx);
    normalX = -1.0f;
    bounced = true;
}

Finding candidate bricks across grid cells

Testing four quadrant candidate cells detects all potential brick overlaps since ball radius is <= 0.5 cells.

Source: source/core/sim/kernels/BallStepCore.h

source/core/sim/kernels/BallStepCore.h lines 257-276, condensed
    const float fractionX = x - static_cast<float>(column);
    const float fractionY = y - static_cast<float>(row);
    // Radius <= 0.5 cannot touch opposite quadrant; checking 4 candidates covers all overlaps vs 9 in a 3x3.
    const int neighborColumn = fractionX < radius ? -1 : (fractionX > 1.0f - radius ? 1 : 0);
    const int neighborRow = fractionY < radius ? -1 : (fractionY > 1.0f - radius ? 1 : 0);

    float bestDistanceSquared = radius * radius;
    // ... iterates over 4 candidate cells:
    for (int candidate = 0; candidate < 4; ++candidate) {
        const int cellColumn = column + ((candidate & 1) ? neighborColumn : 0);
        const int cellRow = row + ((candidate & 2) ? neighborRow : 0);
        // ... clamps to cell box and compares distanceSquared < bestDistanceSquared ...
    }

Collision response when a ball hits a brick

Matching colors destroy bricks immediately without bouncing, while mismatched colors reflect along contact normals.

Source: source/core/sim/kernels/BallStepCore.h

source/core/sim/kernels/BallStepCore.h lines 293-308
if (bestBrick >= 0) {
    // Matching color returns brick index immediately without bouncing, letting gameplay layer record hit.
    if (bestColor == color) {
        return bestBrick;
    }
    const float distance = sqrtf(bestDistanceSquared);
    if (distance > 1e-6f) {
        normalX = bestOffsetX / distance;
        normalY = bestOffsetY / distance;
        x += normalX * (radius - distance);
        y += normalY * (radius - distance);
    // If distance <= 1e-6f, pushOutOfCell snaps ball outside nearest face with cardinal normal.
    } else {
        pushOutOfCell(x, y, normalX, normalY, bestColumn, bestRow, radius);
    }
    reflectVelocity(vx, vy, normalX, normalY);
    bounced = true;
}

Ball-vs-ball collisions and the Scalar-only restriction

Ball-vs-ball collisions are restricted to ScalarBackend to avoid 42x overhead and preserve parallel stepping.

Source: source/backends/cpu/ScalarBackend.cpp, source/tests/SimulationTests.cpp, commit af2812113

source/backends/cpu/ScalarBackend.cpp lines 69-96
// Opt-in diagnostic (commit af2812113); omitted elsewhere: ~42x overhead (1.59 vs 0.038 ms for 2,000 balls).
void ScalarBackend::resolveBallCollisions(const StepParams& params) {
    touched.assign(store.count, uint8_t{0});
    bool collided = false;
    // Linked-list grid (2*radius cells); contacts leave bounce counts untouched to avoid spurious retargeting.
    for (int pass = 0; pass < maximumCollisionPasses; ++pass) {
        buildCollisionGrid(params);
        bool resolved = false;
        for (size_t i = 0; i < store.count; ++i) {
            if (active[i] == 0) {
                continue;
            }
            resolved = collideWithNeighbors(i, params) || resolved;
        }
        if (!resolved) {
            break;
        }
        collided = true;
    }
    if (!collided) {
        return;
    }
    // Every touched ball exchanged normal velocity components, which can drift from the exact speed
    // through rounding; restore it so the constant-speed invariant still holds after a collision.
    for (size_t i = 0; i < store.count; ++i) {
        if (touched[i] != 0) {
            rescaleSpeed(i, params.speed);
        }
    }
}

Data layout: compact 8-byte representation and SoA

The compact 8-byte ball layout

Packing ball position and state into two 32-bit integers minimizes memory bandwidth while keeping precision.

Source: source/backends/cpu/CompactBallStore.h, source/backends/cpu/CompactBallStore.cpp

source/backends/cpu/CompactBallStore.h lines 11-24
// CompactBallStore packs each ball into 8 bytes; speed is omitted because active balls share params.speed.
namespace compact {

inline constexpr float positionScale = 65535.0f;
inline constexpr uint32_t positionMask = 0xFFFFu;
// 13-bit direction (+/-4095) keeps trajectory error < 0.01 cells over 10 s while leaving 6 bits free.
inline constexpr int directionBits = 13;
inline constexpr uint32_t directionMask = (1u << directionBits) - 1u;
inline constexpr float directionScale = 4095.0f;
inline constexpr uint32_t colorShift = 26u;
inline constexpr uint32_t colorMask = 7u;
inline constexpr uint32_t bounceShift = 29u;
inline constexpr uint32_t bounceMask = 3u;
inline constexpr int savedBounceLimit = 3;

}

Structure of Arrays in CompactBallStore

Structure of Arrays enables 256/512-bit SIMD loads and cuts memory writes by updating state only on collisions.

Source: source/backends/cpu/CompactBallStore.h, source/backends/cpu/Avx2Backend.cpp, data/docs/codebase.html

source/backends/cpu/CompactBallStore.h lines 26-36
// Structure of Arrays allows 256-bit and 512-bit aligned SIMD loads without interleaving or shuffling.
struct CompactBallStore {
    // Steady flight reads 4B pos + 4B state and writes 4B pos, consuming only 12B DRAM bandwidth per ball.
    ReservedArray<uint32_t> position;
    // Masked vector stores write state only on wall/brick reflections, cutting write bandwidth in half.
    ReservedArray<uint32_t> state;
    // One render word per ball, kept only while there are fewer balls than visibility-grid slots; above that the
    // grid is the render output and this array is released, so a held ball costs 8 bytes.
    // Deallocated once ball count exceeds grid slots, keeping memory strictly at 8 bytes per held ball.
    SimdVector<uint32_t> packed;
    std::vector<uint32_t> grid;
    int gridWidth = 0;
    int gridHeight = 0;
    bool gridFilled = false;
    double renderSampleShare = 1.0;

The 8-byte layout compared with the original layout and the Scalar store

Replacing seven arrays with an 8-byte layout cut DRAM traffic and boosted AVX2 throughput to 1.25G ball-steps/s.

Source: source/backends/cpu/CpuBallStore.h, data/docs/hybrid-physics.md, commit 671e1cb82

struct CompactBallStore {
    // Reduces coasting DRAM traffic to 8 bytes read and 4 bytes written per ball.
    ReservedArray<uint32_t> position;
    ReservedArray<uint32_t> state;
    // One render word per ball, kept only while there are fewer balls than visibility-grid slots; above that the
    // grid is the render output and this array is released, so a held ball costs 8 bytes.
    SimdVector<uint32_t> packed;
    std::vector<uint32_t> grid;
    int gridWidth = 0;
    int gridHeight = 0;
    bool gridFilled = false;
    double renderSampleShare = 1.0;

Decoding balls back to floating-point positions and velocities

Decodes 8-byte compact balls into floating-point spawns when exporting to other backends, snapshots, or devices.

Source: source/backends/cpu/CompactBallStore.cpp

source/backends/cpu/CompactBallStore.cpp lines 182-190
BallSpawn CompactBallStore::decode(size_t index) const {
    const uint32_t bits = state[index];
    // Arithmetic shifts (bits << 19) >> 19 sign-extend 13-bit direction components.
    const float dx = static_cast<float>(signedComponent(bits & compact::directionMask));
    const float dy = static_cast<float>(signedComponent((bits >> compact::directionBits) & compact::directionMask));
    // Clamping length to >= 1.0f prevents division by zero if both quantized components are zero.
    const float length = std::max(std::hypot(dx, dy), 1.0f);
    return {static_cast<float>(position[index] & compact::positionMask) * fieldWidth / compact::positionScale,
            static_cast<float>(position[index] >> 16) * fieldHeight / compact::positionScale, dx / length * speed,
            dy / length * speed, static_cast<int32_t>(stateColor(bits))};
}

Spatial acceleration: padded board, near-brick caching, and row limits

The padded spatial grid in CpuBoard

A 1-cell sentinel border initialized to -1 lets spatial neighbor queries skip bounds checks completely.

Source: source/backends/cpu/CpuBoard.h, source/core/sim/kernels/BallStepCore.h

source/backends/cpu/CpuBoard.h lines 8-60
struct CpuBoard {
    // Sentinel 1-cell border holds -1, ensuring neighbor lookups never require bounds branching.
    SimdVector<int32_t> paddedCells;
    SimdVector<int32_t> brickColors;
    SimdVector<int32_t> targetCells;
    SimdVector<int32_t> nearBrickCells;
    std::vector<int32_t> nearCellsPerRow;
    int paddedWidth = 1;
    int nearRowsEnd = 0;

    // Incrementally updates changedCells and maintains nearCellsPerRow counters.
    void upload(const BoardUpload& board) {
        if (board.onlyChangedCells && paddedCells.size() == board.paddedCells.size()) {
            for (const int32_t index : board.changedCells) {
                const size_t cell = static_cast<size_t>(index);
                paddedCells[cell] = board.paddedCells[cell];
                nearCellsPerRow[cell / static_cast<size_t>(paddedWidth)] +=
                    (board.nearBrickCells[cell] != 0) - (nearBrickCells[cell] != 0);
                nearBrickCells[cell] = board.nearBrickCells[cell];
            }
            if (!board.targetCells.empty()) {
                targetCells.assign(board.targetCells.begin(), board.targetCells.end());
            }
            for (size_t i = 0; i + 1 < board.targetUpdates.size(); i += 2) {
                targetCells[static_cast<size_t>(board.targetUpdates[i])] = board.targetUpdates[i + 1];
            }
            findNearRowsEnd();
            return;
        }
        paddedWidth = board.columns + 2;
        nearCellsPerRow.assign(static_cast<size_t>(board.rows) + 2, 0);
        for (size_t cell = 0; cell < board.nearBrickCells.size(); ++cell) {
            nearCellsPerRow[cell / static_cast<size_t>(paddedWidth)] += board.nearBrickCells[cell] != 0;
        }
        findNearRowsEnd();
        paddedCells.assign(board.paddedCells.begin(), board.paddedCells.end());
        brickColors.assign(board.brickColors.begin(), board.brickColors.end());
        brickColors.push_back(-1);
        if (!board.targetCells.empty()) {
            targetCells.assign(board.targetCells.begin(), board.targetCells.end());
        }
        nearBrickCells.assign(board.nearBrickCells.begin(), board.nearBrickCells.end());
    }

    // Marks lowest row containing any near-brick cells to bound active collision checks.
    void findNearRowsEnd() {
        int paddedRow = static_cast<int>(nearCellsPerRow.size());
        while (paddedRow > 0 && nearCellsPerRow[static_cast<size_t>(paddedRow) - 1] == 0) {
            --paddedRow;
        }
        nearRowsEnd = paddedRow;
    }

    BoardView view() const { return {paddedCells.data(), brickColors.data(), targetCells.data(), nearBrickCells.data()}; }
    int sentinelBrick() const { return static_cast<int>(brickColors.size()) - 1; }
};

The nearBrickCells array

A spatial occupancy mask lets balls in open space skip all candidate brick checks and contact arithmetic.

Source: source/core/sim/kernels/BallStepCore.h, source/backends/cpu/CpuBoard.h

source/core/sim/kernels/BallStepCore.h lines 246-256
const int column = static_cast<int>(floorf(x));
const int row = static_cast<int>(floorf(y));
// Zero in nearBrickCells mask means open space, skipping all 4 neighbor checks and returning noBrickHit.
if (!board.isNearBrick(paddedCellIndex(column, row, params.paddedWidth))) {
    if (bounced) {
        bounces += 1;
        if (bounces >= params.retargetBounceThreshold) {
            retargetTowardNearestBrick(x, y, vx, vy, color, normalX, normalY, board, params);
        }
    }
    // Bypasses contact math; nearBrickCells updates sparsely via changedCells in CpuBoard::upload.
    return noBrickHit;
}

Whole-tick coasting (coastsWholeTick) in AVX2

Predicting full-tick endpoints lets 8 balls in open space below active bricks coast with fused multiply-adds.

Source: source/backends/cpu/Avx2Backend.cpp, commit d502e1495

source/backends/cpu/Avx2Backend.cpp lines 702-715
// Doubled AVX2 throughput (commit d502e1495): 1.15G to 2.27G on i5-12400F, 3.29G to 5.04G on i9-14900K.
bool coastsWholeTick(const LaneState& state, const LaneConstants& k) {
    const __m256 endX = _mm256_fmadd_ps(state.vx, k.tickTime, state.x);
    const __m256 endY = _mm256_fmadd_ps(state.vy, k.tickTime, state.y);
    const __m256 insideX = _mm256_and_ps(greaterThan(endX, k.coastLeft), lessThan(endX, k.coastRight));
    // coastTop reflects nearRowsEnd, ensuring balls remain in open space below all active bricks.
    const __m256 insideY = _mm256_and_ps(greaterThan(_mm256_min_ps(endY, state.y), k.coastTop), lessThan(endY, k.coastBottom));
    return _mm256_movemask_ps(_mm256_and_ps(insideX, insideY)) == 0xff;
}

// Coasting advances positions with pure FMAs, skipping substeps, boundary checks, and brick searches.
void coast(LaneState& state, const LaneConstants& k, int substeps) {
    for (int substep = 0; substep < substeps; ++substep) {
        state.x = _mm256_fmadd_ps(state.vx, k.deltaTime, state.x);
        state.y = _mm256_fmadd_ps(state.vy, k.deltaTime, state.y);
    }
}

AVX2 vectorisation: lane masking, gathers, and branch elimination

Branchless wall collisions in AVX2

Resolves 8-lane wall collisions branchlessly with blends and bitwise sign flips, preventing pipeline flushes.

Source: source/backends/cpu/Avx2Backend.cpp

source/backends/cpu/Avx2Backend.cpp lines 570-588
// Branchless evaluation: single-cycle blend instructions prevent pipeline flushes on lane divergence.
LaneStepResult stepLanes(LaneState& state, const LaneConstants& k, const BoardView& board) {
    __m256 x = _mm256_fmadd_ps(state.vx, k.deltaTime, state.x);
    __m256 y = _mm256_fmadd_ps(state.vy, k.deltaTime, state.y);
    __m256 vx = state.vx;
    __m256 vy = state.vy;

    const __m256 hitLeft = lessThan(x, k.radius);
    const __m256 hitRight = _mm256_andnot_ps(hitLeft, greaterThan(x, k.rightLimit));
    const __m256 hitTop = lessThan(y, k.radius);
    const __m256 hitBottom = _mm256_andnot_ps(hitTop, greaterThan(y, k.bottomLimit));
    // Bitwise AND with absMask (0x7FFFFFFF) clears sign bits, matching fabsf branchlessly.
    const __m256 absVx = _mm256_and_ps(vx, k.absMask);
    const __m256 absVy = _mm256_and_ps(vy, k.absMask);
    x = select(hitLeft, _mm256_sub_ps(k.twiceRadius, x), select(hitRight, _mm256_sub_ps(k.twiceRightLimit, x), x));
    y = select(hitTop, _mm256_sub_ps(k.twiceRadius, y), select(hitBottom, _mm256_sub_ps(k.twiceBottomLimit, y), y));
    vx = select(hitLeft, absVx, select(hitRight, _mm256_sub_ps(k.zero, absVx), vx));
    vy = select(hitTop, absVy, select(hitBottom, _mm256_sub_ps(k.zero, absVy), vy));
    __m256 normalX = select(hitLeft, k.one, select(hitRight, k.minusOne, k.zero));
    __m256 normalY = select(hitTop, k.one, select(hitBottom, k.minusOne, k.zero));
    __m256 bounced = _mm256_or_ps(_mm256_or_ps(hitLeft, hitRight), _mm256_or_ps(hitTop, hitBottom));

Brick candidate searches and gathers across SIMD lanes

SIMD gathers fetch brick IDs across 8 lanes simultaneously, guarded by near-brick tests to avoid cache misses.

Source: source/backends/cpu/Avx2Backend.cpp, source/backends/cpu/Avx2Backend.cpp

source/backends/cpu/Avx2Backend.cpp lines 217-236, condensed
// Gathers brick occupants across 8 lanes; near-brick mask tests avoid serialized cache gathers in open air.
inline void considerCell(ContactSearch& best, const int32_t* paddedCells, __m256 x, __m256 y, __m256i column,
                         __m256i row, const LaneConstants& k) {
    const __m256i index = _mm256_add_epi32(_mm256_mullo_epi32(row, k.paddedWidth), _mm256_add_epi32(column, k.paddedOffset));
    const __m256i brick = _mm256_i32gather_epi32(paddedCells, index, 4);
    const __m256 occupied = _mm256_castsi256_ps(_mm256_cmpgt_epi32(brick, k.minusOneInt));
    const __m256 cellLeft = _mm256_cvtepi32_ps(column);
    const __m256 cellTop = _mm256_cvtepi32_ps(row);
    const __m256 closestX = _mm256_min_ps(_mm256_max_ps(x, cellLeft), _mm256_add_ps(cellLeft, k.one));
    const __m256 closestY = _mm256_min_ps(_mm256_max_ps(y, cellTop), _mm256_add_ps(cellTop, k.one));
    const __m256 offsetX = _mm256_sub_ps(x, closestX);
    const __m256 offsetY = _mm256_sub_ps(y, closestY);
    const __m256 distanceSquared = _mm256_fmadd_ps(offsetX, offsetX, _mm256_mul_ps(offsetY, offsetY));
    const __m256 closer = _mm256_and_ps(occupied, lessThan(distanceSquared, best.distanceSquared));
    best.distanceSquared = select(closer, distanceSquared, best.distanceSquared);
    // ... blends best brick, column, row ...
}

Rare paths in AVX2: embedded contacts and retargeting

Diverts rare paths (< 0.1% of balls) to scalar routines to prevent complex branch stalls across SIMD lanes.

Source: source/backends/cpu/Avx2Backend.cpp, source/backends/cpu/Avx2Backend.cpp

// Rare paths (deep penetration or 3+ bounce retargeting) fall back to scalar to avoid stalling SIMD lanes.
void resolveEmbeddedLanes(LaneState& state, unsigned lanes, const LaneStepResult& result, const BoardView& board,
                          const StepParams& params) {
    // ... stores vector registers into alignas(32) arrays ...
    while (lanes != 0) {
        // std::countr_zero extracts next divergent lane to run scalar contact resolution or targetInSight.
        const int lane = std::countr_zero(lanes);
        lanes &= lanes - 1;
        resolveEmbeddedContact(x[lane], y[lane], vx[lane], vy[lane], color[lane], bounces[lane], columns[lane],
                               rows[lane], board, params);
    }
    // ... reloads modified scalar arrays back into __m256 registers ...
}

Minimising write traffic and cache pollution in AVX2

Masked stores write state only on bounces, and non-temporal streaming stores bypass caches for render words.

Source: source/backends/cpu/Avx2Backend.cpp

_mm256_store_si256(reinterpret_cast<__m256i*>(positions + i), encodeSteppedPositionLanes(state, startPosition, k));
const __m256i stateChanged = _mm256_xor_si256(_mm256_cmpeq_epi32(state.bounces, startBounces), k.minusOneInt);
// When testz confirms no state changed in the 8-ball group, DRAM state writes are skipped entirely.
if (!_mm256_testz_si256(stateChanged, stateChanged)) {
    // Masked stores write only dirty lanes when reflections alter ball state.
    _mm256_maskstore_epi32(reinterpret_cast<int*>(states + i), stateChanged, encodeStateLanes(state, k));
}

const __m256i packed = packForRender(state.x, state.y, state.color, k);
if (grid.slots) {
    const unsigned bidding = grid.bidders(i) & laneLimit;
    if (bidding != 0) {
        grid.place(packed, bidding);
    }
} else if (packedPositions) {
    // Non-temporal stores stream single-use render words directly to DRAM, avoiding L1/L2 cache pollution.
    _mm256_stream_si256(reinterpret_cast<__m256i*>(packedPositions + i), packed);
}

Zen4 AVX-512 backend and architecture portability

The Zen4 backend and AVX-512

Zen4 uses 512-bit AVX-512 registers and native 16-bit opmasks to simulate 16 balls per instruction.

Source: source/backends/cpu/Zen4Backend.h, source/backends/cpu/Zen4Backend.cpp, source/backends/cpu/CompactBallStore.cpp

source/backends/cpu/Zen4Backend.cpp lines 233-253
// Padded to 16 lanes; delivers ~1.1x AVX2 throughput on Ryzen 7 7700 where memory streaming is the ceiling.
// Lanes outside `lanes` load no cell and read as empty, so they never become closer.
inline void considerCell(ContactSearch& best, const int32_t* paddedCells, __m512 x, __m512 y, __m512i column,
                         __m512i row, __mmask16 lanes, const LaneConstants& k) {
    const __m512i index = _mm512_add_epi32(_mm512_mullo_epi32(row, k.paddedWidth), _mm512_add_epi32(column, k.paddedOffset));
    // Native 16-bit opmask registers replace vector blend masks (_mm512_mask_i32gather_epi32).
    const __m512i brick = _mm512_mask_i32gather_epi32(k.minusOneInt, lanes, index, paddedCells, 4);
    const __m512 occupied = _mm512_castsi512_ps(greaterThanMask(brick, k.minusOneInt));
    const __m512 cellLeft = _mm512_cvtepi32_ps(column);
    const __m512 cellTop = _mm512_cvtepi32_ps(row);
    const __m512 closestX = _mm512_min_ps(_mm512_max_ps(x, cellLeft), _mm512_add_ps(cellLeft, k.one));
    const __m512 closestY = _mm512_min_ps(_mm512_max_ps(y, cellTop), _mm512_add_ps(cellTop, k.one));
    const __m512 offsetX = _mm512_sub_ps(x, closestX);
    const __m512 offsetY = _mm512_sub_ps(y, closestY);
    const __m512 distanceSquared = _mm512_fmadd_ps(offsetX, offsetX, _mm512_mul_ps(offsetY, offsetY));
    const __m512 closer = _mm512_and_ps(occupied, lessThan(distanceSquared, best.distanceSquared));
    best.distanceSquared = select(closer, distanceSquared, best.distanceSquared);
    best.offsetX = select(closer, offsetX, best.offsetX);
    best.offsetY = select(closer, offsetY, best.offsetY);
    best.column = selectInt(closer, column, best.column);
    best.row = selectInt(closer, row, best.row);
    best.brick = selectInt(closer, brick, best.brick);
}

CPU feature detection and binary portability

Dynamic feature detection checks CPUID and OS XSAVE state before selecting the fastest supported backend.

Source: source/backends/cpu/CpuPlugin.cpp, source/backends/CMakeLists.txt, source/backends/cpu/Zen4Backend.cpp

source/backends/cpu/CpuPlugin.cpp lines 47-67
// Single shared library (balls_physics_cpu) runs portably across any x86-64 CPU.
BALLS_PLUGIN_EXPORT const char* ballsBackendUnavailableReason(const char* kindName) {
    const std::string_view kind(kindName);
    // ScalarBackend runs portably on any C++20 target (ARM/Android, WebAssembly fallback).
    if (kind == scalarName) {
        return noReason;
    }
    // Checks CPUID and verifies OS state saving via _xgetbv(0) mask 0x6 (XMM/YMM state).
    if (kind == avx2Name) {
#if defined(BALLS_HAS_AVX2_BACKEND)
        return Avx2Backend::isSupportedByCpu() ? noReason : missingAvx2;
#else
        return avx2NotBuilt;
#endif
    }
    // Checks AVX-512 and verifies OS state saving via _xgetbv(0) mask 0xE6u (ZMM state).
    if (kind == zen4Name) {
#if defined(BALLS_HAS_ZEN4_BACKEND)
        return Zen4Backend::isSupportedByCpu() ? noReason : missingZen4;
#else
        return zen4NotBuilt;
#endif
    }
    return unknownKind;
}

Compiler bugs and header conflicts in the Zen4 backend

Template arguments pass comparison predicates as instruction immediates, avoiding Clang rejections and MSVC crashes.

Source: commit 0dc002f3c, commit 3dc867e35, source/backends/cpu/Zen4Backend.cpp

source/backends/cpu/Zen4Backend.cpp lines 41-55
// Renamed macro 'near' (commit 0dc002f3c); commit e093f6555 adopted camelCase (compareMask, greaterThanMask).
// The predicate is an instruction immediate, so it has to be a constant: clang rejects a runtime argument once the
// call is not inlined, and MSVC 14.44 crashes on it.
// Predicate must be compile-time immediate: runtime args crash MSVC 14.44 and fail Clang (commit 3dc867e35).
template <int Predicate>
// Expands 16-bit comparison opmask into a 512-bit vector mask for lane blending.
inline __m512 compareMask(__m512 a, __m512 b) {
    const __mmask16 mask = _mm512_cmp_ps_mask(a, b, Predicate);
    return _mm512_castsi512_ps(_mm512_maskz_set1_epi32(mask, -1));
}
inline __m512i greaterThanMask(__m512i a, __m512i b) {
    const __mmask16 mask = _mm512_cmpgt_epi32_mask(a, b);
    return _mm512_maskz_set1_epi32(mask, -1);
}
inline __m512i equalMask(__m512i a, __m512i b) {
    const __mmask16 mask = _mm512_cmpeq_epi32_mask(a, b);
    return _mm512_maskz_set1_epi32(mask, -1);
}

Multithreading, thread pooling, and power management

WorkerPool: partitioning ball work across CPU threads

A 64-bit atomic cursor combining generation and chunk index lets worker threads claim chunks lock-free.

Source: source/core/common/WorkerPool.h, source/core/common/WorkerPool.cpp

source/core/common/WorkerPool.cpp lines 117-136
// Partitioned into chunks: Avx2Backend uses default 2,048 balls (32 KB); Zen4Backend uses 16,384.
// Chunks are claimed one at a time from the cursor, and only while it still names this job's generation.
// Dispatching thread participates in drainChunks, eliminating idle master-thread time.
void WorkerPool::drainChunks(const Job& job) {
    uint64_t claimed = cursor.load(std::memory_order_acquire);
    for (;;) {
        // 64-bit cursor packs generation in high 32 bits to stop late-waking workers claiming newer jobs.
        if ((claimed >> 32) != (job.generation & 0xFFFFFFFFu) || (claimed & 0xFFFFFFFFu) >= job.chunks) {
            return;
        }
        if (!cursor.compare_exchange_weak(claimed, claimed + 1, std::memory_order_acq_rel)) {
            continue;
        }
        const size_t chunk = static_cast<size_t>(claimed & 0xFFFFFFFFu);
        const size_t begin = chunk * job.chunkSize;
        (*job.task)(chunk, begin, std::min(begin + job.chunkSize, job.itemCount));
        if (chunksDone.fetch_add(1, std::memory_order_acq_rel) + 1 == job.chunks) {
            std::lock_guard lock(mutex);
            doneCondition.notify_all();
        }
        claimed = cursor.load(std::memory_order_acquire);
    }
}

// Lowering to 2,048 balls (32 KB, commit c62090d44) fits L1 cache; 11% faster at 1M balls on 2-core laptops.
// 2048 balls is 32 KB of ball data: one chunk fits a core's L1 and balances evenly across four threads, and it
// measured 11% faster than 16384 on a 2-core laptop and no slower on a 16-thread desktop.
constexpr long defaultStepChunkBalls = 2048;
constexpr long defaultRenderSamplesPerTask = 64;
// Reserves one worker thread for GPU coordination during hybrid execution.
constexpr long defaultHybridSpareThreads = 1;

Eliminating false sharing and cache-line bouncing

Skipping writes to occupied visibility slots stops cache line bouncing, dropping frame time from 36 ms to 4 ms.

Source: source/backends/cpu/Avx2Backend.cpp, source/backends/cpu/Avx2Backend.cpp, commit 24d0da320, data/docs/hybrid-physics.md

source/backends/cpu/Avx2Backend.cpp lines 363-378
// Grid cache bouncing halved in-game throughput from 1,200M to 610M; storeSlotRelaxed avoids rewriting slots.
// Only an empty slot is written: on a board much bigger than the launch fan, millions of balls stay over a few
// thousand pixels for minutes, and every thread rewriting the same slots bounced their cache lines between cores.
// A filled slot is only read, which cores can share. The grid is cleared every frame, so the first sampled ball of
// the frame keeps the slot, as random a pick as the last.
// Bypassing writes to occupied slots dropped frame times from 36 ms to 4 ms (commit 24d0da320).
void storeSlotRelaxed(uint32_t& slot, uint32_t value) {
#if defined(__cpp_lib_atomic_ref)
    std::atomic_ref<uint32_t> shared(slot);
    // Bypasses writes to occupied slots so filled cache lines stay shared across cores.
    if (shared.load(std::memory_order_relaxed) == emptyRenderSlot) {
        shared.store(value, std::memory_order_relaxed);
    }
#else
    if (__atomic_load_n(&slot, __ATOMIC_RELAXED) == emptyRenderSlot) {
        __atomic_store_n(&slot, value, __ATOMIC_RELAXED);
    }
#endif
}

Preventing Windows EcoQoS throttling on hybrid P/E-core CPUs

// Disabling EcoQoS boosted i9-14900K from 1.14G (E-core cap) to 3.28G ball-steps/s (commit de701ed33).
void requestFullSpeed() {
#if defined(_WIN32)
    THREAD_POWER_THROTTLING_STATE state{};
    state.Version = THREAD_POWER_THROTTLING_CURRENT_VERSION;
    state.ControlMask = THREAD_POWER_THROTTLING_EXECUTION_SPEED;
    // Clearing StateMask disables EcoQoS execution speed throttling.
    state.StateMask = 0;
    SetThreadInformation(GetCurrentThread(), ThreadPowerThrottling, &state, sizeof(state));
#endif
}

Ball lifecycle: parallel removals, preallocation, and paging

Removing balls efficiently when bricks break

Swap-and-pop removes balls in O(1) time per hit by moving tail survivors into holes, avoiding O(N) array shifts.

Source: source/backends/cpu/CompactBallStore.cpp

source/backends/cpu/CompactBallStore.cpp lines 136-169
// Swap-and-pop runs in O(1) per hit, independent of millions of surviving balls (vs O(N*K) for erase).
void CompactBallStore::swapRemove(std::span<const uint32_t> descendingIndices, const RangeRunner& runRanges) {
    constexpr size_t parallelRemovals = 16384;
    // Indices must be descending so swapped tail positions remain valid during sequential moves.
    if (!runRanges || descendingIndices.size() < parallelRemovals) {
        size_t last = count;
        for (uint32_t index : descendingIndices) {
            moveBall(static_cast<uint32_t>(--last), index);
        }
        resize(last);
        return;
    }
    // The removed indices at or above the new count need no filling; the rest (holes) take the tail's survivors
    // (sources), one to one, so every move is independent of the others.
    const size_t kept = count - descendingIndices.size();
    size_t removedInTail = 0;
    while (removedInTail < descendingIndices.size() && descendingIndices[removedInTail] >= kept) {
        ++removedInTail;
    }
    removalHoles.assign(descendingIndices.begin() + static_cast<std::ptrdiff_t>(removedInTail), descendingIndices.end());
    removalSources.clear();
    size_t nextRemoved = 0;
    for (size_t index = count; index-- > kept;) {
        if (nextRemoved < removedInTail && descendingIndices[nextRemoved] == index) {
            ++nextRemoved;
        } else {
            removalSources.push_back(static_cast<uint32_t>(index));
        }
    }
    runRanges(removalHoles.size(), [&](size_t begin, size_t end) {
        for (size_t i = begin; i < end; ++i) {
            moveBall(removalSources[i], removalHoles[i]);
        }
    });
    resize(kept);
}

Parallel ball removal at tens of thousands of deaths per frame

Pre-pairing hole indices with tail survivors lets WorkerPool execute thousands of removals in parallel.

Source: source/backends/cpu/CompactBallStore.cpp, commit ebf445077

source/backends/cpu/CompactBallStore.cpp lines 146-168
// Pre-pairing cut hit stalls from 1.6 ms to 0.62 ms with 60M balls (commit ebf445077).
// The removed indices at or above the new count need no filling; the rest (holes) take the tail's survivors
// (sources), one to one, so every move is independent of the others.
const size_t kept = count - descendingIndices.size();
size_t removedInTail = 0;
// Tail removals at or above kept need no filling since array is truncated to kept.
while (removedInTail < descendingIndices.size() && descendingIndices[removedInTail] >= kept) {
    ++removedInTail;
}
removalHoles.assign(descendingIndices.begin() + static_cast<std::ptrdiff_t>(removedInTail), descendingIndices.end());
removalSources.clear();
size_t nextRemoved = 0;
for (size_t index = count; index-- > kept;) {
    if (nextRemoved < removedInTail && descendingIndices[nextRemoved] == index) {
        ++nextRemoved;
    } else {
        removalSources.push_back(static_cast<uint32_t>(index));
    }
}
runRanges(removalHoles.size(), [&](size_t begin, size_t end) {
    for (size_t i = begin; i < end; ++i) {
        moveBall(removalSources[i], removalHoles[i]);
    }
});
resize(kept);

ReservedArray: no reallocation copies near the RAM ceiling

Reserves virtual address space upfront so physical pages commit in 64 MB granules without copying buffers.

Source: source/backends/cpu/ReservedArray.h, source/backends/cpu/ReservedArray.h, commit 671e1cb82

source/backends/cpu/ReservedArray.h lines 184-211
    // Fixed base pointer avoids reallocation buffer copies near RAM limits (commit 671e1cb82).
    bool reserveAddressSpace(size_t needed) {
        if (elements) {
            return needed <= reserved;
        }
        reserved = roundUp(std::max(needed, physicalMemoryBytes()), commitGranule);
#if defined(_WIN32)
        elements = static_cast<T*>(VirtualAlloc(nullptr, reserved, MEM_RESERVE, PAGE_NOACCESS));
#else
        void* address = mmap(nullptr, reserved, PROT_NONE, MAP_PRIVATE | MAP_ANONYMOUS | MAP_NORESERVE, -1, 0);
        elements = address == MAP_FAILED ? nullptr : static_cast<T*>(address);
#endif
        if (!elements) {
            reserved = 0;
        }
        return elements != nullptr && needed <= reserved;
    }

    // Commits physical pages in 64 MB granules on demand; decommitAll releases RAM when count hits zero.
    bool commit(size_t offset, size_t bytes) {
        if (bytes == 0) {
            return true;
        }
        char* start = reinterpret_cast<char*>(elements) + offset;
#if defined(_WIN32)
        return VirtualAlloc(start, bytes, MEM_COMMIT, PAGE_READWRITE) != nullptr;
#else
        return mprotect(start, bytes, PROT_READ | PROT_WRITE) == 0;
#endif
    }

Asynchronous pre-faulting

Pre-faults committed pages on a background thread to prevent Windows working set trim stalls up to 967 ms.

Source: source/backends/cpu/ReservedArray.h, commit 20d54ba9a

source/backends/cpu/ReservedArray.h lines 162-169
// Background pre-faulting eliminated 967 ms Windows working set trim stalls (commit 20d54ba9a).
prefaultBegin = committed;
committed += commitGranule;
volatile char* const first = reinterpret_cast<char*>(elements) + prefaultBegin;
// Background thread faults one byte per 4 KB page ahead, keeping frame times under 20 ms.
prefault = std::async(std::launch::async, [first] {
    for (size_t offset = 0; offset < commitGranule; offset += pageBytes) {
        first[offset] = 0;
    }
});

Numerical accuracy, unbiased dithering, and tunnelling prevention

Velocity drift in quantised balls, solved by dithering

Dithering with a hash of the previous position eliminates truncation bias, reducing velocity drift by 10x.

Source: source/core/sim/kernels/BallStepCore.h, data/docs/physics-accuracy.md, commit df27721f7

source/core/sim/kernels/BallStepCore.h lines 97-119
// Whether the straight line from (x, y) to the centre of the target cell crosses no other brick. The nearest
// same-color brick is found by distance alone, so it is often behind other bricks; aiming at it anyway sends the
// ball into the blocking brick, back off the wall behind it, and at the same target again, forever, and every
// ball near that column ends up on the same line. Cells are walked with a grid DDA (Amanatides and Woo).
// Stepped balls are stored back to 16-bit positions every frame, and a frame moves a ball a fixed ~255.4 units,
// so rounding to nearest dropped the same fraction every frame: a steady speed error of up to 0.2% per axis and
// 0.12 cells of drift in 10 s against an exact engine. Adding a pseudo-random fraction before truncating makes the
// rounding unbiased. The fraction comes from a hash of the ball's previous stored position, which every stepper
// has and which changes every frame; low 16 bits for x, high 16 bits for y.
// Dithering cut 10 s velocity drift from 0.12 to 0.011 cells without extra state (commit df27721f7).
BALLS_HOST_DEVICE unsigned int roundingDither(unsigned int previousPosition) {
    unsigned int hash = previousPosition * 0x9E3779B1u;
    hash ^= hash >> 15;
    hash *= 0x85EBCA77u;
    hash ^= hash >> 13;
    hash *= 0xC2B2AE3Du;
    hash ^= hash >> 16;
    return hash;
}

BALLS_HOST_DEVICE float ditherFraction(unsigned int dither16) {
    constexpr float sixteenBitUnits = 1.0f / 65536.0f;
    return static_cast<float>(dither16 & 0xFFFFu) * sixteenBitUnits;
}

Accuracy tests against the double-precision reference engine

// ReferenceBackend uses double precision and micro-steps <= 0.01 cells, sharing zero production code.
TEST_CASE("every optimized backend stays within accuracy bounds of the naive reference engine") {
    constexpr size_t boxBalls = 3000;
    constexpr int boxFrames = 300;
    // Bounds: mean error < 0.02 cells, max < 0.06 cells, speed < 1e-3, zero balls outside bounds.
    constexpr double meanPositionBound = 0.02;
    constexpr double maxPositionBound = 0.06;
    BrickLayout box;
    box.width = fieldColumns;
    box.height = 1;
    box.playableHeight = fieldRows;
    box.cellIds.assign(static_cast<size_t>(fieldColumns), emptyBrickId);
    struct Drawn {
        bool grid;
        bool drawing;
    };
    for (const Drawn drawn : {Drawn{true, true}, Drawn{false, true}, Drawn{true, false}}) {
        AccuracyScenario scenario{"empty box", box, boxBalls, false, boxFrames};
        scenario.visibilityGrid = drawn.grid;
        scenario.drawing = drawn.drawing;
        const AccuracyRun reference = runAccuracyScenario(createReferenceBackend(), scenario);
        for (const BackendKind kind : allBackendKinds) {
            if (!isBackendAvailable(kind)) {
                continue;
            }
            const AccuracyReport report = compareToReference(runAccuracyScenario(createBackend(kind), scenario), reference);
            EXPECT(report.comparedPositions);
            EXPECT(report.meanPositionError < meanPositionBound);
            EXPECT(report.maxPositionError < maxPositionBound);
            EXPECT(report.worstOutsideField == 0);
            EXPECT(report.worstInsideBricks == 0);
            EXPECT(report.worstSpeedError < 1e-3);
        }
