}

Preventing tunnelling through bricks and walls

Substepping (default 2) and pushOutOfCell keep displacement small and push balls outside brick faces.

Source: source/core/sim/kernels/BallStepCore.h, source/core/sim/kernels/BallStepCore.h

source/core/sim/kernels/BallStepCore.h lines 73-95
// Substeps keep move < 0.12 cells; raising physics to 240 Hz drops hit error vs reference from 18% to 2.4%.
BALLS_HOST_DEVICE void pushOutOfCell(float& x, float& y, float& normalX, float& normalY, int column, int row,
                                     float radius) {
    const float toLeft = x - static_cast<float>(column);
    const float toRight = static_cast<float>(column + 1) - x;
    const float toTop = y - static_cast<float>(row);
    const float toBottom = static_cast<float>(row + 1) - y;
    const float nearest = fminf(fminf(toLeft, toRight), fminf(toTop, toBottom));
    normalX = 0.0f;
    normalY = 0.0f;
    // Snaps ball radius distance outside nearest face and assigns cardinal normal.
    if (nearest == toBottom) {
        normalY = 1.0f;
        y = static_cast<float>(row + 1) + radius;
    } else if (nearest == toTop) {
        normalY = -1.0f;
        y = static_cast<float>(row) - radius;
    } else if (nearest == toLeft) {
        normalX = -1.0f;
        x = static_cast<float>(column) - radius;
    } else {
        normalX = 1.0f;
        x = static_cast<float>(column + 1) + radius;
    }
}

The retargeting loop bug and targetInSight

Grid DDA raycasting checks unobstructed line-of-sight to target bricks, preventing infinite bounce loops.

Source: source/core/sim/kernels/BallStepCore.h, source/tests/SimulationTests.cpp, commit d11443834

source/core/sim/kernels/BallStepCore.h lines 121-156, condensed
// Distance-only retargeting trapped balls; DDA ray rejects occluded targets (commit d11443834).
template <typename Board>
BALLS_HOST_DEVICE bool targetInSight(float x, float y, int targetColumn, int targetRow, const Board& board,
                                     int paddedWidth) {
    // ... Amanatides & Woo grid DDA raycast:
    for (int crossed = 0; crossed < cellsToCross; ++crossed) {
        if (nextX < nextY) {
            nextX += deltaX;
            column += stepColumn;
        } else {
            nextY += deltaY;
            row += stepRow;
        }
        if (column == targetColumn && row == targetRow) {
            return true;
        }
        int color = -1;
        // Intervening bricks block the ray, rejecting retargeting to maintain specular reflection.
        if (board.occupant(paddedCellIndex(column, row, paddedWidth), color) >= 0) {
            return false;
        }
    }
    return column == targetColumn && row == targetRow;
}

CPU-GPU consistency: shared step core and retargeting

Keeping step logic consistent across CPU and GPU backends

Centralizing step logic in BallStepCore.h ensures physics matches across CPU backends, CUDA, and shaders.

Source: source/core/sim/kernels/BallStepCore.h, data/docs/codebase.html

source/core/sim/kernels/BallStepCore.h lines 3-27
// Shared step logic; tests verify every backend matches scalar reference within 5% on 1,000 bricks.
#if defined(__CUDACC_RTC__)
#define BALLS_HOST_DEVICE __device__ __forceinline__
#else
#include <cmath>
#define BALLS_HOST_DEVICE inline
#endif

struct StepParams {
    float deltaTime;
    float radius;
    float speed;
    float fieldWidth;
    float fieldHeight;
    int paddedWidth;
    int paddedCellCount;
    int retargetBounceThreshold;
    float packScaleX;
    float packScaleY;
    int substeps;
    // Ticks since the simulation started, at the start of this step; wraps after about a year at 120 Hz.
    unsigned int tick;
    // Analytical triangle wave evaluation when all bricks are cleared and collisions are off.
    // Nonzero when nothing on the board can change a ball's path (no bricks left, no ball-vs-ball collisions):
    // every ball then flies a closed-form path between the walls and backends may stop stepping it.
    int freeFlight;
};

Closed-form free flight once every brick is cleared

Once all bricks are cleared, wall bounces unfold into a periodic triangle wave evaluated in O(1) time.

Source: source/core/sim/kernels/BallStepCore.h, data/docs/free-flight.md

source/core/sim/kernels/BallStepCore.h lines 343-355
// Period 2*span in 32-bit fixed point wraps modulo 2^32 without rounding drift, freezing numerical stepping.
BALLS_HOST_DEVICE unsigned int freeFlightPhase(float position, float velocity, float extent, float radius) {
    const float span = freeFlightSpan(extent, radius);
    const float along = clampFloat(position - radius, 0.0f, span);
    // Unfolds bouncing motion into a periodic domain of period 2 * span.
    const float unfolded = velocity < 0.0f ? 2.0f * span - along : along;
    const float fraction = unfolded / (2.0f * span);
    const float wrapped = fraction >= 1.0f ? 0.0f : fraction;
    return static_cast<unsigned int>(wrapped * freeFlightPhaseUnits);
}

BALLS_HOST_DEVICE unsigned int freeFlightAdvance(float velocity, float deltaTime, float extent, float radius) {
    const float perTick = fabsf(velocity) * deltaTime / (2.0f * freeFlightSpan(extent, radius));
    return static_cast<unsigned int>(perTick * freeFlightPhaseUnits + 0.5f);
}

Vectorised free-flight rendering in AVX2

Precomputing per-axis constants eliminates per-ball divisions in AVX2, cutting frame time from 40 ms to 13 ms.

Source: source/backends/cpu/Avx2Backend.cpp, commit c0542097b

source/backends/cpu/Avx2Backend.cpp lines 343-359
// Precomputed constants cut 40 ms frame time to 13-20 ms on i5-12400F with 60M balls (commit c0542097b).
// The sampled free-flight drawing, eight balls at a time: the same closed form as flyFreely, without its divisions
// (per-axis constants are worked out once a frame), followed by eight grid stores. The scalar form cost about 30 ns
// a ball, which bounded a big board at 60 FPS by how many balls a frame could sample.
struct FlightRenderLanes {
    FlightAxisLanes x;
    FlightAxisLanes y;
    __m256 speed;
    __m256 phaseAdvanceScale;
    __m256i ticks;

    explicit FlightRenderLanes(const CompactBallStore& store)
        : x(store.flightParams.fieldWidth, store.flightParams.radius, store.flightParams.packScaleX, store.gridWidth),
          y(store.flightParams.fieldHeight, store.flightParams.radius, store.flightParams.packScaleY, store.gridHeight),
          speed(_mm256_set1_ps(store.speed)),
          phaseAdvanceScale(_mm256_set1_ps(store.flightParams.deltaTime * freeFlightPhaseUnits)),
          // Elapsed ticks since free flight epoch timestamp.
          ticks(_mm256_set1_epi32(static_cast<int>(store.clockTick - store.freeFlightEpoch))) {}
};

Historical optimisations and measured performance gains

Impact of the compact 8-byte layout over the 7-array float layout

The 8-byte layout and sampled grid writes doubled AVX2 throughput and eliminated thread cache bouncing.

Source: data/docs/hybrid-physics.md, data/docs/codebase.html, commit 671e1cb82

data/docs/hybrid-physics.md lines 37-48
# 8-byte layout boosted AVX2 to 1,080M-1,250M steps/s (commit 671e1cb82); rooflines: 2.15G (i5), 5.1G (i9).
### What made the CPU side worth adding

The first hybrid was slower than CUDA alone. Three changes fixed it:

| Change | AVX2 throughput (2 substeps) |
|---|---|
| Original: seven float/int arrays, 28 bytes read and 24 written per ball | 650 M ball-steps/s |
| Compact 8-byte ball (CUDA's encoding), state written only when it changes, near-brick skip | 1080 M (bricks), 1250 M (empty board) |
# Sampling 8 bids per slot stopped cache bouncing, doubling in-game throughput from 610M to 1200M.
| Sampled visibility-grid bids (8 per slot) instead of every ball writing the shared grid | in-game 610 M -> 1200 M |

The last one matters only in the game: eleven threads storing every ball into the same ~45 KB grid made its
cache lines bounce between cores and halved CPU throughput.

Whole-tick coasting and row bounds doubling AVX2 throughput

Coasting in open rows doubled AVX2 throughput to 2.27G steps/s, letting Hybrid beat CUDA alone by 46%.

Source: commit d502e1495, source/backends/cpu/Avx2Backend.cpp

source/backends/cpu/Avx2Backend.cpp lines 702-715
// Coasting below nearRowsEnd doubled AVX2 (commit d502e1495); at 96M balls Hybrid rose to 13.8G steps/s.
bool coastsWholeTick(const LaneState& state, const LaneConstants& k) {
    const __m256 endX = _mm256_fmadd_ps(state.vx, k.tickTime, state.x);
    const __m256 endY = _mm256_fmadd_ps(state.vy, k.tickTime, state.y);
    const __m256 insideX = _mm256_and_ps(greaterThan(endX, k.coastLeft), lessThan(endX, k.coastRight));
    // Confirms balls stay below nearRowsEnd (coastTop) where no live bricks exist.
    const __m256 insideY = _mm256_and_ps(greaterThan(_mm256_min_ps(endY, state.y), k.coastTop), lessThan(endY, k.coastBottom));
    return _mm256_movemask_ps(_mm256_and_ps(insideX, insideY)) == 0xff;
}

void coast(LaneState& state, const LaneConstants& k, int substeps) {
    for (int substep = 0; substep < substeps; ++substep) {
        state.x = _mm256_fmadd_ps(state.vx, k.deltaTime, state.x);
        state.y = _mm256_fmadd_ps(state.vy, k.deltaTime, state.y);
    }
}

Parallel removals and background pre-faulting against latency spikes

Parallel swap-removal and background pre-faulting eliminated latency spikes at tens of thousands of deaths.

Source: commit ebf445077, commit 20d54ba9a, data/docs/edge-cases.md

source/backends/cpu/CompactBallStore.cpp lines 136-169
// Parallel removals cut hit handling to 0.54-0.62 ms (commit ebf445077); rejected autonomous CPU hits.
void CompactBallStore::swapRemove(std::span<const uint32_t> descendingIndices, const RangeRunner& runRanges) {
    constexpr size_t parallelRemovals = 16384;
    if (!runRanges || descendingIndices.size() < parallelRemovals) {
        size_t last = count;
        for (uint32_t index : descendingIndices) {
            moveBall(static_cast<uint32_t>(--last), index);
        }
        resize(last);
        return;
    }
    // Pre-faulting 64 MB chunks stopped 967 ms trim stalls; parallel moves avoid sorts (commit 20d54ba9a).
    // The removed indices at or above the new count need no filling; the rest (holes) take the tail's survivors
    // (sources), one to one, so every move is independent of the others.
    const size_t kept = count - descendingIndices.size();
    size_t removedInTail = 0;
    while (removedInTail < descendingIndices.size() && descendingIndices[removedInTail] >= kept) {
        ++removedInTail;
    }
    removalHoles.assign(descendingIndices.begin() + static_cast<std::ptrdiff_t>(removedInTail), descendingIndices.end());
    removalSources.clear();
    size_t nextRemoved = 0;
    for (size_t index = count; index-- > kept;) {
        if (nextRemoved < removedInTail && descendingIndices[nextRemoved] == index) {
            ++nextRemoved;
        } else {
            removalSources.push_back(static_cast<uint32_t>(index));
        }
    }
    runRanges(removalHoles.size(), [&](size_t begin, size_t end) {
        for (size_t i = begin; i < end; ++i) {
            moveBall(removalSources[i], removalHoles[i]);
        }
    });
    resize(kept);
}

Key facts

  • Each compact ball occupies exactly 8 bytes: 32 bits for position (X/Y scaled to 65,535) and 32 bits for state (two 13-bit signed direction components quantized to ±±4,095, 3-bit color, 2-bit bounce count).
  • Speed is omitted from per-ball storage because all balls share the global scalar params.speed.
  • CompactBallStore uses Structure of Arrays (SoA), storing position and state in separate ReservedArray buffers; state is only written when bounces change via masked vector stores.
  • The spatial board is padded with a 1-cell sentinel border (paddedWidth = columns + 2), eliminating boundary clamp branches during neighbor lookups.
  • nearBrickCells caches live brick proximity per cell; balls in open space skip contact searches and immediately return noBrickHit.
  • coastsWholeTick tests if all 8 SIMD lanes remain below nearRowsEnd and within walls, executing pure FMAs and doubling AVX2 throughput (1.15G to 2.27G ball-steps/s on i5-12400F).
  • AVX2 processes 8 balls per vector; rare paths (embedded contact resolution and line-of-sight retargeting) extract active lane masks with _mm256_movemask_ps and run scalar fallbacks.
  • Zen4 processes 16 balls per vector using AVX-512 (F, BW, DQ, VL, VBMI2, VPOPCNTDQ) and achieves ~1.1x AVX2 speedup on Ryzen 7 7700.
  • The CPU plugin is built into a single portable binary that tests CPUID and XGETBV (0x6 for AVX2, 0xE6u for Zen4/AVX-512) at runtime; ARM and WebAssembly targets fall back to ScalarBackend.
  • WorkerPool distributes work in dynamic chunks (2,048 balls in AVX2, fitting L1 cache; 16,384 balls in Zen4) using an atomic cursor encoding generation in high 32 bits and chunk index in low 32 bits.
  • storeSlotRelaxed uses relaxed atomic loads before writing visibility grid slots, eliminating cache-line bouncing and cutting frame time from 36 ms to 4 ms.
  • requestFullSpeed opts out of Windows EcoQoS power throttling via SetProcessInformation, boosting i9-14900K throughput from 1.14G to 3.28G ball-steps/s.
  • ReservedArray reserves address space for all physical RAM up front, committing 64 MB granules and pre-faulting pages on a background thread to prevent 967 ms OS working-set trimming stalls.
  • roundingDither runs a MurmurHash mixer on the previous 32-bit position word, adding unbiased dither fractions before quantization and cutting velocity drift from 0.12 to 0.011 cells.
  • targetInSight uses Amanatides & Woo grid DDA raycasts to check line-of-sight before retargeting, preventing balls from getting trapped in infinite reflection loops behind occluding bricks.

source/backends/cuda/CudaDriver.cpp lines 26-52
std::vector<const BallKernelImage*> imagesToTry(int computeMajor, int computeMinor) {
    // BALLS_CUDA_IMAGE forces a specific cubin or PTX image for testing (commit cbcfff6d0).
    if (const std::string forced = forcedImageName(); !forced.empty()) {
        std::vector<const BallKernelImage*> images;
        for (const BallKernelImage& image : ballKernelImages) {
            if (imageName(image) == forced) {
                images.push_back(&image);
            }
        }
        return images;
    }
    // Native SASS cubins avoid JIT stutter on older drivers; PTX provides forward compatibility.
    const int deviceVersion = computeMajor * 10 + computeMinor;
    const auto versionOf = [](const BallKernelImage& image) { return image.computeMajor * 10 + image.computeMinor; };
    std::vector<const BallKernelImage*> images;
    for (const BallKernelImage& image : ballKernelImages) {
        // Same-major cubins run directly; newer architectures JIT from PTX.
        const bool binaryRuns = image.computeMajor == computeMajor && image.computeMinor <= computeMinor;
        if (image.binary ? binaryRuns : versionOf(image) <= deviceVersion) {
            images.push_back(&image);
        }
    }
    // Prefers the highest matching native cubin, then falls back to compatible PTX.
    std::ranges::stable_sort(images, [&](const BallKernelImage* left, const BallKernelImage* right) {
        if (left->binary != right->binary) {
            return left->binary;
        }
        return versionOf(*left) > versionOf(*right);
    });
    return images;
}

CudaDriver and multiple NVIDIA GPUs

Per-GPU primary contexts and modules allow multiple NVIDIA cards to step concurrently without collision.

Source: source/backends/cuda/CudaDriver.h, source/backends/cuda/CudaDriver.cpp

source/backends/cuda/CudaDriver.cpp lines 153-174
// Isolates device state per GPU ordinal behind a static mutex; library init is shared globally.
CudaDriver& CudaDriver::forDevice(int ordinal) {
    static std::mutex mutex;
    static std::map<int, std::unique_ptr<CudaDriver>> drivers;
    std::lock_guard lock(mutex);
    std::unique_ptr<CudaDriver>& driver = drivers[ordinal];
    if (!driver) {
        // Retains a primary context per device; backends set it current before driver calls.
        driver.reset(new CudaDriver(ordinal));
    }
    return *driver;
}

CudaDriver::CudaDriver(int ordinal) : deviceOrdinal(ordinal) {
    const CudaLibrary& library = cudaLibrary();
    functions = library.functions;
    if (!library.errorMessage.empty()) {
        errorMessage = library.errorMessage;
    } else if (ordinal < 0 || ordinal >= library.devices) {
        errorMessage = "no CUDA device";
    } else {
        createContextAndModule();
    }
}

Architectures without warp-level match instructions

Pre-Volta architectures fall back to direct per-lane atomics without hardware warp matching.

Source: source/tools/ptxgen/PtxGen.cpp, source/backends/cuda/BallKernels.cu, data/docs/gpu-compute-apis.md

source/backends/cuda/BallKernels.cu lines 148-177
// A ball takes a hit by subtracting first and refunding when the brick had none left, which gives out exactly the
// brick's hits without a compare-and-swap loop. When a launch wave reaches a row of bricks, hundreds of thousands of
// balls hit the same few in one tick; their CAS retries serialized into 150 ms steps. Lanes of a warp hitting the same
// brick also share one atomic where the GPU can group them.
// Pre-Volta architectures lack __match_any_sync and compile with BALLS_RANK_WITH_ATOMICS.
#if defined(BALLS_RANK_WITH_ATOMICS)
__device__ __forceinline__ bool consumeBrickHit(int* brickHitsLeft, int brick) {
    // Direct atomicSub fallback added for compute_50 (commit cbcfff6d0); 9.67 ms vs 9.45 ms on GTX 1650.
    if (atomicSub(&brickHitsLeft[brick], 1) > 0) {
        return true;
    }
    atomicAdd(&brickHitsLeft[brick], 1);
    return false;
}
// Volta+ aggregates warp requests before touching memory.
#else
__device__ __forceinline__ bool consumeBrickHit(int* brickHitsLeft, int brick) {
    const unsigned int peers = __match_any_sync(__activemask(), brick);
    const int lane = static_cast<int>(threadIdx.x) & 31;
    const int leader = __ffs(peers) - 1;
    const int wanted = __popc(peers);
    int granted = 0;
    if (lane == leader) {
        const int previous = atomicSub(&brickHitsLeft[brick], wanted);
        granted = max(0, min(previous, wanted));
        if (granted < wanted) {
            atomicAdd(&brickHitsLeft[brick], wanted - granted);
        }
    }
    granted = __shfl_sync(peers, granted, leader);
    return __popc(peers & ((1u << lane) - 1u)) < granted;
}
#endif

CUDA simulation kernels and thread structure

Thread layout of stepBallsKernel

Processing two balls per thread keeps memory requests in flight to hide DRAM latency.

Source: source/backends/cuda/BallKernels.cu, data/docs/cuda-limits.md

source/backends/cuda/BallKernels.cu lines 363-385
// Commit 21090f9ad set two balls per thread; four gave no extra gain on CUDA.
extern "C" __global__ void stepBallsKernel(DeviceBalls balls, int count, const int* cellWords, const int* targetCells,
                                           StepParams params, int* brickHitsLeft, unsigned int* killedIndices,
                                           unsigned int killedCapacity, FrameCounters* counters, VisibilityGrid grid,
                                           unsigned int packedCapacity) {
    const int blockStart = static_cast<int>(blockIdx.x) * ballsPerStepThread * static_cast<int>(blockDim.x);
    unsigned int positions[ballsPerStepThread];
    unsigned int states[ballsPerStepThread];
    // Preloading two balls hides DRAM latency: on 60M balls, 10.19 ms to 10.0 ms (bricks), 7.07 ms to 6.86 ms (empty).
    for (int item = 0; item < ballsPerStepThread; ++item) {
        const int index = blockStart + item * static_cast<int>(blockDim.x) + static_cast<int>(threadIdx.x);
        positions[item] = index < count ? balls.position[index] : 0u;
        states[item] = index < count ? balls.state[index] : 0u;
    }
    const CellBoard board{cellWords, targetCells};
    for (int item = 0; item < ballsPerStepThread; ++item) {
        const int index = blockStart + item * static_cast<int>(blockDim.x) + static_cast<int>(threadIdx.x);
        if (index >= count) {
            return;
        }
        stepOneBall(static_cast<unsigned int>(index), positions[item], states[item], balls.position, balls.state,
                    false, balls.packed, board, params, brickHitsLeft, killedIndices, killedCapacity, counters, grid,
                    packedCapacity);
    }
}

Consuming brick hits without compare-and-swap retry storms

Warp-level matching and subtract-with-refund eliminate compare-and-swap contention on dense brick impacts.

Source: source/backends/cuda/BallKernels.cu, data/docs/codebase.html

source/backends/cuda/BallKernels.cu lines 161-176
// Commit 81a04aaa3 replaced CAS loops that serialized dense hits to over 150 ms.
__device__ __forceinline__ bool consumeBrickHit(int* brickHitsLeft, int brick) {
    // Elects a warp leader to issue a single atomicSub for all lanes matching the brick.
    const unsigned int peers = __match_any_sync(__activemask(), brick);
    const int lane = static_cast<int>(threadIdx.x) & 31;
    const int leader = __ffs(peers) - 1;
    const int wanted = __popc(peers);
    int granted = 0;
    // Immediately refunds excess hits via atomicAdd if fewer remained than requested.
    if (lane == leader) {
        const int previous = atomicSub(&brickHitsLeft[brick], wanted);
        granted = max(0, min(previous, wanted));
        if (granted < wanted) {
            atomicAdd(&brickHitsLeft[brick], wanted - granted);
        }
    }
    // Broadcasts granted count across the warp and assigns hits via prefix popcount.
    granted = __shfl_sync(peers, granted, leader);
    return __popc(peers & ((1u << lane) - 1u)) < granted;
}

Instruction-issue bound brick levels and spatial sorting

Sorting balls by cell on the GPU concentrates brick collisions into fewer warps, raising SIMD lane utilization.

Source: source/backends/cuda/BallKernels.cu, data/docs/cuda-limits.md

data/docs/cuda-limits.md lines 52-62
# Commit 8e47f6c4c: GPU cell sort and fused step-and-sort kernel.
## Spatial sorting

# Unsorted balls drag SIMD lane utilization on contact code down to ~19%.
Only 19% of ball-substeps are in a near-brick cell and 2.5% touch a brick. Yet with balls in arbitrary order almost
every 32-lane warp contains one, so the near path ran at about 19% lane utilization. That was wasted issue, not a
hardware limit. The backend now sorts balls by board cell on the GPU:

# Cell counting uses warp-aggregated shared atomics before single-block prefix scan.
- a block-aggregated counting pass (`countCellsKernel`, warp-aggregated shared atomics through `__match_any_sync`)
  and a single-block scan give each cell its output range;
# Fused step-and-sort avoids a 16-byte scatter pass, cutting tick time from 16.8 ms to 10.2 ms on 60M balls.
- on a sort tick, `stepAndSortBallsKernel` steps 4096 balls per block and writes each one straight to its
  cell-sorted slot in a second buffer, so sorting costs only the 4-byte counting pass instead of a separate
  16-byte scatter pass.

SortSchedule: choosing the sort interval dynamically

SortSchedule measures GPU time across candidate sort intervals at runtime to lock in the cheapest setting.

Source: source/core/sim/SortSchedule.h, data/docs/cuda-limits.md

source/core/sim/SortSchedule.h lines 7-32
class SortSchedule {
public:
    // Dynamically tests {8, 16, 32} tick sort intervals, locking in the lowest-cost candidate for 1,200 ticks.
    static constexpr std::array<int, 3> intervalCandidates{8, 16, 32};
    static constexpr int warmupTicks = 16;
    static constexpr int measuredTicks = 32;
    static constexpr int ticksBetweenTrials = 1200;
    static constexpr size_t minimumBalls = size_t{1} << 20;
    static constexpr size_t bulkAddDenominator = 32;

    // Requires at least 1M balls to amortize the counting pass overhead.
    bool sortThisTick(size_t balls) {
        if (balls < minimumBalls || intervalTicks <= 0 || ++ticksSinceSort < intervalTicks) {
            return false;
        }
        ticksSinceSort = 0;
        return true;
    }

    // Balls appended in bulk (a composer moving them in from another device) arrive unsorted and change the set, so
    // a trial measuring meanwhile would charge the arrival to its candidate: that candidate is measured again.
    // Spawns in play are far below the threshold.
    // Bulk arrivals reset trials so migration costs are not misattributed (commit 6977f5712).
    void ballsAdded(size_t added, size_t balls) {
        if (ticksUntilTrial == 0 && added * bulkAddDenominator >= balls) {
            trialTick = 0;
            trialMsPerBall = 0.0;
        }
    }

Step-kernel techniques tested and rejected

Benchmarking proposed kernel optimizations on real hardware revealed synchronization and memory overheads.

Source: data/docs/cuda-limits.md

data/docs/cuda-limits.md lines 132-147
# Reverted optimizations measured on GTX 1650 that degraded performance or gave no gain.
## What did not help

Each of these was measured on the brick level and reverted:

| change | result |
|---|---|
| neighbor-occupancy masks to skip the contact loop | 13.7 ms (no gain) |
| streaming / read-only cache hints | 13.6 ms (no gain) |
| edge-distance fast path for contact candidates | -48 static instructions, no measurable gain |
# Block compaction cost 16.9 ms (barrier overhead); block-level sort ran at 11.8 ms (scattered writes).
| block-level compaction of near-brick balls through shared memory | 16.9 ms (per-substep barriers cost more than the saved lanes) |
| sorting inside 256-thread step blocks | 11.8 ms (sorted writes scatter: 0.2 balls per cell per block) |
# Scatter pass ran at 17.4 ms vs 14.6 ms fused; shared board cells ran at 10.67 ms vs 9.70 ms (empty: 6.4 ms).
| separate scatter pass instead of the fused sort | every tick 17.4 ms, every 4 ticks 10.7 ms (fused: 14.6 / 10.0) |
| board cell words staged in shared memory | 10.67 vs 9.70 ms (slower; empty board 9.5 vs 6.4 ms) |
# Persistent grid-stride ran at 9.46 ms vs 8.33 ms due to loop overhead and register pressure.
| persistent grid-stride step kernel with prefetch | 9.46 vs 8.33 ms on normal ticks |
| plain shared atomics instead of warp-aggregated ranking | count 4.0 vs 2.2 ms, sorting step 14.4 vs 13.1 ms |
# Fine 3x3 edge-band sorting decayed within one tick as balls move 0.23 cells/tick.
| sort key (cell, 3x3 edge band) instead of cell | 9.68 vs 10.36 ms when fresh (-6.5%), but balls cross a 0.2-cell band about every tick (0.23 cells/tick), so the order decays before a sort pays for itself |

GPU ball removal and in-place compaction

Compacting killed balls on the GPU without the host

Two GPU kernels compact killed balls in place without host synchronization, scaling with killed count.

Source: source/backends/cuda/BallKernels.cu, source/backends/cuda/CudaBackend.cpp

source/backends/cuda/BallKernels.cu lines 195-221
// Compacts killed balls in two kernels without host sync; work scales with killedCount, not total balls.
extern "C" __global__ void collectHolesKernel(const unsigned int* killedIndices, unsigned int killedCount,
                                              unsigned int survivorCount, unsigned int* holes,
                                              FrameCounters* counters) {
    const unsigned int index = blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x;
    if (index >= killedCount) {
        return;
    }
    const unsigned int killed = killedIndices[index];
    if (killed < survivorCount) {
        holes[atomicAdd(&counters->holeCount, 1u)] = killed;
    }
}

// Copies surviving balls from above survivorCount into available hole slots.
extern "C" __global__ void fillHolesKernel(DeviceBalls balls, unsigned int survivorCount, unsigned int oldCount,
                                           const unsigned int* holes, FrameCounters* counters,
                                           unsigned int packedCapacity) {
    const unsigned int source = survivorCount + blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x;
    if (source >= oldCount || stateColor(balls.state[source]) == deadColor) {
        return;
    }
    const unsigned int target = holes[atomicAdd(&counters->holeCursor, 1u)];
    balls.position[target] = balls.position[source];
    balls.state[target] = balls.state[source];
    if (source < packedCapacity) {
        balls.packed[target] = balls.packed[source];
    }
}

Why removeBalls is empty in CudaBackend

CUDA resolves hits and compacts dead balls on the GPU during the step, making host compaction unnecessary.

Source: source/backends/cuda/CudaBackend.cpp, source/backends/cuda/CudaBackend.h

source/backends/cuda/CudaBackend.cpp lines 1019-1032
// CUDA resolves hits and compacts dead balls on-device before returning, unlike CPU backends.
DeviceHitResults CudaBackend::deviceHitResults() const {
    DeviceHitResults results;
    if (lastCounters.killedCount == 0) {
        return results;
    }
    // Returns hit brick IDs and kill counts for scorekeeping without inspecting individual balls.
    results.hitBricks = hostHitBricks;
    for (int color = 0; color < ballColorCount; ++color) {
        results.killsByColor[color] = lastCounters.killsByColor[color];
    }
    results.killed = lastCounters.killedCount;
    return results;
}

// Dead balls were already compacted on the GPU during the step, so removeBalls is a no-op.
void CudaBackend::removeBalls(std::span<const uint32_t>) {}

Guarding the per-step kill buffer against overflow

Excess brick hits are refunded if the per-step kill buffer fills, preventing GPU out-of-memory crashes.

Source: source/backends/cuda/CudaBackend.cpp, source/backends/cuda/BallKernels.cu

source/backends/cuda/BallKernels.cu lines 350-360
// Kill buffer is clamped between 1,024 and 4,194,304 to prevent exhausting device memory.
if (brick != noBrickHit && consumeBrickHit(brickHitsLeft, static_cast<int>(brick))) {
    // Commit 3519d09ee capped the per-step kill buffer to prevent GPU crashes on massive hits.
    const unsigned int slot = atomicAdd(&counters->killedCount, 1u);
    if (slot < killedCapacity) {
        outState[target] = deadColor << colorShift;
        killedIndices[slot] = target;
        killedIndices[killedCapacity + slot] = static_cast<unsigned int>(brick);
        atomicAdd(&counters->killsByColor[color], 1u);
    // Refunds hit to the brick if kill buffer overflows; ball stays alive to hit next tick.
    } else {
        atomicAdd(&brickHitsLeft[brick], 1);
    }
}

if (!succeeded(api.memcpyDeviceToHost(&lastCounters, counters.pointer, sizeof(FrameCounters)), "download counters")) {
    return;

    float elapsedMs = 0.0f;
    api.eventElapsedTime(&elapsedMs, stepStarted, stepFinished);
    lastStepKernelMs = elapsedMs;
    sortSchedule.record(elapsedMs, count);

    return;
}

Memory management and host-device transfer overlapping

GrowableDeviceArray: growing GPU memory in place

Virtual memory management expands GPU ball arrays in place without reallocating buffers or moving data.

Source: source/backends/cuda/GrowableDeviceArray.cpp, source/backends/cuda/CudaBackend.cpp

source/backends/cuda/GrowableDeviceArray.cpp lines 53-76
// Virtual memory growth avoids 448 ms cudaMalloc copy stalls near memory limits (commit 0369ca091).
const size_t added = roundUp(bytes - mapped, granularity);
if (mapped + added > reserved) {
    return false;
}
const CUmemAllocationProp properties = deviceAllocation(owner->cudaDevice());
CUmemGenericAllocationHandle handle = 0;
// Allocates physical memory in 64 MB granules as ball count grows.
if (api.memCreate(&handle, added, &properties, 0) != CUDA_SUCCESS) {
    return false;
}
// Maps physical handles into reserved virtual range; pointers stay fixed so balls never move.
if (api.memMap(base + mapped, added, 0, handle, 0) != CUDA_SUCCESS) {
    api.memRelease(handle);
    return false;
}
CUmemAccessDesc access{};
access.location = properties.location;
access.flags = CU_MEM_ACCESS_FLAGS_PROT_READWRITE;
if (api.memSetAccess(base + mapped, added, &access, 1) != CUDA_SUCCESS) {
    api.memUnmap(base + mapped, added);
    api.memRelease(handle);
    return false;
}
mappings.push_back({handle, mapped, added});
mapped += added;
return true;

Uploading launches to the GPU without stalling

Parallel CPU encoding into pinned memory and asynchronous GPU uploads keep ball spawn latency under 12 ms.

Source: source/backends/cuda/CudaBackend.cpp, source/backends/cuda/BallKernels.cu

source/backends/cuda/CudaBackend.cpp lines 491-515
// Pinned memory for three words a ball, or null where the driver can't copy from it asynchronously. Waits only for
// the previous upload to have been read, which the GPU does long before the next frame's launches.
// Multicore encoding into pinned memory holds 4.2 ms spawn time up to 367M balls; no frame exceeds 12 ms.
uint32_t* CudaBackend::uploadStaging(size_t balls) {
    const auto& api = driver.api();
    if (!api.memcpyHostToDeviceAsync || !stagingRead || balls == 0) {
        return nullptr;
    }
    // Overlaps CPU spawn encoding with GPU execution; next frame waits only on stagingRead event.
    api.eventSynchronize(stagingRead);
    if (stagingBalls < balls) {
        constexpr size_t minimumStagingBalls = size_t{1} << 16;
        const size_t wanted = std::max({balls, stagingBalls * 2, minimumStagingBalls});
        if (staging) {
            api.memFreeHost(staging);
        }
        staging = nullptr;
        stagingBalls = 0;
        void* memory = nullptr;
        if (api.memAllocHost(&memory, wanted * 3 * sizeof(uint32_t)) != CUDA_SUCCESS) {
            return nullptr;
        }
        staging = static_cast<uint32_t*>(memory);
        stagingBalls = wanted;
    }
    return staging;
}

Partial board cell uploads to the GPU

Sparse cell updates upload only modified brick words to the GPU, preventing PCIe bandwidth saturation.

Source: source/backends/cuda/CudaBackend.cpp, source/backends/cuda/BallKernels.cu

source/backends/cuda/CudaBackend.cpp lines 678-701
// Writes (index, value) pairs into a device array with the cell-word kernel; false when there was nothing to write.
// Uploads sparse modified cells instead of full boards, conserving PCIe bandwidth (commit 6ea6f768f).
bool CudaBackend::scatterUpdates(std::span<const int32_t> pairs, CUdeviceptr target, DeviceArray& staging) {
    if (pairs.empty() || target == 0) {
        return false;
    }
    // Uploads sparse (index, word) pairs into staging and scatters them via kernel.
    upload(staging, pairs.data(), pairs.size_bytes());
    CUdeviceptr updatesPointer = staging.pointer;
    int updateCount = static_cast<int>(pairs.size() / 2);
    void* arguments[] = {&updatesPointer, &updateCount, &target};
    return launch(applyCellWordsFunction, static_cast<size_t>(updateCount), arguments, "scatter board updates");
}

void CudaBackend::applyChangedCellWords(const BoardUpload& board) {
    if (board.changedCells.empty()) {
        return;
    }
    const std::vector<int32_t> updates = buildCellWordUpdates(board);
    upload(cellWordUpdates, updates.data(), updates.size() * sizeof(int32_t));
    CUdeviceptr updatesPointer = cellWordUpdates.pointer;
    CUdeviceptr wordsPointer = cellWords.pointer;
    int updateCount = static_cast<int>(board.changedCells.size());
    void* arguments[] = {&updatesPointer, &updateCount, &wordsPointer};
    launch(applyCellWordsFunction, board.changedCells.size(), arguments, "applyCellWordsKernel");
}

Free flight without step computation or transfers

Free flight evaluates closed-form wall bounces analytically during rendering, skipping collision steps.

Source: source/backends/cuda/BallKernels.cu, source/backends/cuda/CudaBackend.cpp

source/backends/cuda/BallKernels.cu lines 428-458
// While every ball flies freely the stored state is each ball at the epoch tick. Rendering a frame only needs the
// balls that bid for a visibility slot, so whole 256-ball chunks are sampled with a stride: the reads stay
// contiguous and the cost follows the number of slots, not the number of balls.
// Freezes stepping and evaluates closed-form bounce trajectories when no bricks remain (commit e125d92eb).
extern "C" __global__ void freeFlightRenderKernel(DeviceBalls balls, int count, StepParams params,
                                                  unsigned int ticksSinceEpoch, VisibilityGrid grid,
                                                  unsigned int packedCapacity, unsigned int chunkStride,
                                                  unsigned int chunkOffset) {
    const unsigned long long chunk =
        static_cast<unsigned long long>(chunkOffset) + static_cast<unsigned long long>(blockIdx.x) * chunkStride;
    const unsigned long long wide = chunk * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x;
    if (wide >= static_cast<unsigned long long>(count)) {
        return;
    }
    const unsigned int index = static_cast<unsigned int>(wide);
    const unsigned int state = balls.state[index];
    const unsigned int color = stateColor(state);
    float x = 0.0f;
    float y = 0.0f;
    float vx = 0.0f;
    float vy = 0.0f;
    decodePosition(balls.position[index], x, y, params);
    decodeVelocity(state, vx, vy, params);
    const FreeFlightPosition flown = flyFreely(x, y, vx, vy, ticksSinceEpoch, params);
    const unsigned int packed = packBallForRender(flown.x, flown.y, static_cast<int>(color), params.packScaleX,
                                                  params.packScaleY);
    // Strided chunk sampling scales render cost with visibility slots instead of ball count.
    if (grid.slots) {
        bidForSlot(packed, index, grid);
    } else if (index < packedCapacity) {
        balls.packed[index] = packed;
    }
}

Rendering ball positions: interop vs visibility grid readback

OpenGL interop instead of CPU readback for ball rendering

Sharing vertex buffers between CUDA and OpenGL eliminates CPU readback for ball rendering.

Source: source/backends/cuda/CudaBackend.cpp, source/backends/cuda/CudaBackend.h

source/backends/cuda/CudaBackend.cpp lines 1053-1082
// Registers OpenGL vertex buffer with write-discard so the driver maps it without preserving contents.
bool CudaBackend::shareRenderBuffer(unsigned int openGlBuffer, size_t capacityBytes) {
    if (isFaulted() || !driver.api().graphicsGlRegisterBuffer) {
        return false;
    }
    driver.makeCurrent();
    releaseRenderBuffer();
    if (driver.api().graphicsGlRegisterBuffer(&renderResource, openGlBuffer, writeDiscardFlag) != CUDA_SUCCESS) {
        renderResource = nullptr;
        return false;
    }
    renderCapacityBytes = capacityBytes;
    return true;
}

// Maps OpenGL buffer into CUDA to copy or unpack render data directly on GPU without host roundtrips.
bool CudaBackend::copyIntoRenderBuffer(CUdeviceptr source, size_t elements) {
    if (!renderResource || elements * 4 > renderCapacityBytes) {
        return false;
    }
    const auto& api = driver.api();
    if (api.graphicsMapResources(1, &renderResource, nullptr) != CUDA_SUCCESS) {
        return false;
    }
    CUdeviceptr mapped = 0;
    size_t mappedBytes = 0;
    bool copied = api.graphicsResourceGetMappedPointer(&mapped, &mappedBytes, renderResource) == CUDA_SUCCESS &&
                  mappedBytes >= elements * 4 &&
                  succeeded(api.memcpyDeviceToDevice(mapped, source, elements * 4), "copy to render buffer");
    api.graphicsUnmapResources(1, &renderResource, nullptr);
    return copied;
}

The 64-bit atomic visibility grid and PCIe bandwidth

Rasterizing balls into a fixed-resolution GPU visibility grid keeps PCIe rendering readback constant.

Source: source/backends/cuda/BallKernels.cu, source/backends/cuda/CudaBackend.cpp, data/docs/codebase.html

source/backends/cuda/BallKernels.cu lines 128-146
__device__ __forceinline__ int slotForPacked(unsigned int packed, const VisibilityGrid& grid) {
    // GPU visibility grid avoids reading back 400 MB over PCIe for 100M balls without graphics interop.
    const int column =
        min(static_cast<int>(static_cast<float>(packed & renderCoordinateMax) * grid.scaleX), grid.width - 1);
    const int row = min(
        static_cast<int>(static_cast<float>((packed >> renderCoordinateBits) & renderCoordinateMax) * grid.scaleY),
        grid.height - 1);
    return row * grid.width + column;
}

// Non-GL front ends download only fixed grid cells (16 KB) via scratch arrays (commit 272acaf08).
__device__ __forceinline__ void bidForSlot(unsigned int packed, unsigned int index, const VisibilityGrid& grid) {
    const unsigned int priority = slotPriority(index, grid.frameSeed);
    if ((priority >> 1) & grid.bidderMask) {
        return;
    }
    const int slot = slotForPacked(packed, grid);
    if (priority > static_cast<unsigned int>(grid.slots[slot] >> 32)) {
        atomicMax(&grid.slots[slot], (static_cast<unsigned long long>(priority) << 32) | packed);
    }
}

64-bit atomic visibility bidding for each slot

A 64-bit atomicMax bids for visibility slots, storing priority and packed pixel data in one transaction.

Source: source/backends/cuda/BallKernels.cu, data/docs/codebase.html

source/backends/cuda/BallKernels.cu lines 137-146
// 64-bit slot holds hash priority in high 32 bits and packed render word in low 32 bits.
__device__ __forceinline__ void bidForSlot(unsigned int packed, unsigned int index, const VisibilityGrid& grid) {
    const unsigned int priority = slotPriority(index, grid.frameSeed);
    // Tests bidderMask to prune low-priority candidates before issuing atomic operations.
    if ((priority >> 1) & grid.bidderMask) {
        return;
    }
    const int slot = slotForPacked(packed, grid);
    // atomicMax selects highest-priority ball and its render word in a single transaction.
    if (priority > static_cast<unsigned int>(grid.slots[slot] >> 32)) {
        atomicMax(&grid.slots[slot], (static_cast<unsigned long long>(priority) << 32) | packed);
    }
}

Merging visibility grids in hybrid CPU+GPU simulation

Hybrid CPU and GPU visibility grids are merged on the host using throughput-weighted probabilistic slot replacement.

Source: source/core/sim/HybridBackend.cpp, data/docs/hybrid-physics.md

source/core/sim/HybridBackend.cpp lines 174-205
// Sampled bidding on the ~45 KB grid stopped cache bouncing across 11 threads (610 M to 1200 M steps/s).
void HybridBackend::overlay(const BallSnapshot& balls, uint32_t replaceBelow) {
    if (!balls.packed || balls.count == 0) {
        return;
    }
    // Hash threshold replaces GPU slots probabilistically based on CPU throughput share.
    const auto place = [&](size_t slot, uint32_t value) {
        if (merged[slot] == emptyRenderSlot || slotHash(static_cast<uint32_t>(slot)) < replaceBelow) {
            merged[slot] = value;
        }
    };
    if (balls.visibilityGrid) {
        if (balls.count != merged.size()) {
            return;
        }
        for (size_t slot = 0; slot < balls.count; ++slot) {
            if (balls.packed[slot] != emptyRenderSlot) {
                place(slot, balls.packed[slot]);
            }
        }
        return;
    }
    const float coordinateToColumn = static_cast<float>(stepGridWidth) / (renderCoordinateMax + 1);
    const float coordinateToRow = static_cast<float>(stepGridHeight) / (renderCoordinateMax + 1);
    for (size_t i = 0; i < balls.count; ++i) {
        const uint32_t value = balls.packed[i];
        const int column = std::min(static_cast<int>(static_cast<float>(value & renderCoordinateMax) * coordinateToColumn),
                                    stepGridWidth - 1);
        const int row = std::min(
            static_cast<int>(static_cast<float>((value >> renderCoordinateBits) & renderCoordinateMax) * coordinateToRow),
            stepGridHeight - 1);
        place(static_cast<size_t>(row) * static_cast<size_t>(stepGridWidth) + static_cast<size_t>(column), value);
    }
}

Vulkan compute and ROCm HSA backends

The Vulkan compute backend's copy of the CUDA step pipeline

The Vulkan compute backend ports the CUDA step pipeline to HLSL using group-shared atomics for portability.

Source: data/docs/gpu-compute-apis.md, source/backends/vulkan/VulkanBackend.cpp

source/tools/gpucompute/BallStep.hlsl lines 445-483
// Ports CUDA fused step-and-sort to HLSL compiled to SPIR-V, preserving the 8-byte ball layout.
void computeMain(uint3 group : SV_GroupID, uint3 thread : SV_GroupThreadID) {
    const uint cellCount = (uint)(sortColumns * sortRows);
    for (uint cell = thread.x; cell < cellCount; cell += SORT_THREADS) {
        blockCells[cell] = 0u;
    }
    GroupMemoryBarrierWithGroupSync();
    const uint groupIndex = group.y * groupsPerRow + group.x;
    const uint blockStart = groupIndex * SORT_BALLS_PER_GROUP;
    for (uint item = 0; item < SORT_ITEMS; ++item) {
        const uint slot = item * SORT_THREADS + thread.x;
        const uint index = blockStart + slot;
        uint packedSlot = 0u;
        if (index < ballCount) {
            const uint ballCell = sortCellOf(positions[index]);
            uint rank = 0u;
            // Intra-cell ball ranking uses group-shared InterlockedAdd for cross-vendor portability.
            InterlockedAdd(blockCells[ballCell], 1u, rank);
            packedSlot = (ballCell << RANK_BITS) | rank;
        }
        cellAndRank[slot] = packedSlot;
    }
    GroupMemoryBarrierWithGroupSync();
    for (uint claimed = thread.x; claimed < cellCount; claimed += SORT_THREADS) {
        if (blockCells[claimed] != 0u) {
            uint base = 0u;
            InterlockedAdd(cellCursors[claimed], blockCells[claimed], base);
            blockCells[claimed] = base;
        }
    }
    GroupMemoryBarrierWithGroupSync();
    // On 60M balls, Vulkan runs 9.57 ms vs CUDA's 9.44 ms (bricks) and 6.59 ms vs 6.18 ms (empty).
    [loop] for (uint ball = 0; ball < SORT_ITEMS; ++ball) {
        const uint slot = ball * SORT_THREADS + thread.x;
        const uint index = blockStart + slot;
        if (index < ballCount) {
            const uint packedSlot = cellAndRank[slot];
            const uint target = blockCells[packedSlot >> RANK_BITS] + (packedSlot & RANK_MASK);
            stepOneBall(target, positions[index], states[index]);
        }
    }
}

VulkanComputeDevice with GPUs from different vendors

Instance-level dispatchers let NVIDIA and AMD GPUs run Vulkan compute shaders side-by-side in one process.

Source: source/backends/vulkan/VulkanComputeDevice.h, source/backends/vulkan/VulkanComputeDevice.cpp

source/backends/vulkan/VulkanComputeDevice.h lines 14-40
// The plugin's one VkInstance. NVRHI's static Vulkan build calls through Vulkan-Hpp's global default dispatcher,
// which is initialised here at instance level only and never re-initialised for a device: device functions then
// resolve to the loader's trampolines, which dispatch on the VkDevice handle, so devices from different drivers
// (an NVIDIA and an AMD GPU) can be used side by side and from several threads.
// Initializes Vulkan-Hpp dispatcher at instance level only, avoiding per-device reinitialization.
class VulkanInstance final {
public:
    static std::shared_ptr<VulkanInstance> create(std::string& error);
    ~VulkanInstance();

    VulkanInstance(const VulkanInstance&) = delete;
    VulkanInstance& operator=(const VulkanInstance&) = delete;

    VkInstance handle() const { return instance; }
    uint32_t apiVersion() const { return version; }
    PFN_vkGetInstanceProcAddr getInstanceProcAddr() const { return getProcAddr; }
// Trampolines dispatch on VkDevice, allowing NVIDIA and AMD GPUs to run side-by-side without conflicts.
    const vk::detail::DispatchLoaderDynamic& dispatcher() const { return instanceDispatcher; }

private:
    VulkanInstance() = default;
    bool initialize(std::string& error);

    std::unique_ptr<DynamicLibrary> loader;
    PFN_vkGetInstanceProcAddr getProcAddr = nullptr;
    VkInstance instance = VK_NULL_HANDLE;
    uint32_t version = VK_API_VERSION_1_0;
    vk::detail::DispatchLoaderDynamic instanceDispatcher;
};

The ROCm backend on the HSA runtime instead of HIP

Direct HSA runtime calls and AQL packet queues eliminate HIP launch overhead on AMD GPUs under Linux.

Source: source/backends/rocm/RocmDriver.h, source/backends/rocm/RocmBackend.cpp, data/docs/hybrid-physics.md

source/backends/rocm/RocmBackend.cpp lines 479-508
                                  bool barrier, hsa_signal_t signal) {
    // Dynamically loads HSA runtime without HIP; allocates VRAM directly from agent memory pools.
    void* kernargs = getKernargSlot(argsSize);
    std::memcpy(kernargs, args, argsSize);

    const auto& api = driver.api();
    // Enqueues low-level AQL packets directly, avoiding graphics queue submission overhead.
    uint64_t index = api.queueAddWriteIndexRelaxed(queue, 1);
    const uint32_t mask = queue->size - 1;
    auto* packet = static_cast<hsa_kernel_dispatch_packet_t*>(queue->base_address) + (index & mask);

    std::memset(packet, 0, sizeof(*packet));
    packet->setup = 1 << HSA_KERNEL_DISPATCH_PACKET_SETUP_DIMENSIONS;
    packet->workgroup_size_x = workgroupSize;
    packet->workgroup_size_y = 1;
    packet->workgroup_size_z = 1;
    packet->grid_size_x = gridSize;
    packet->grid_size_y = 1;
    packet->grid_size_z = 1;
    packet->kernel_object = kernel.kernelObject;
    packet->kernarg_address = kernargs;
    packet->group_segment_size = kernel.groupSize;
    packet->private_segment_size = kernel.privateSize;
    packet->completion_signal = signal;

    uint16_t header = (HSA_PACKET_TYPE_KERNEL_DISPATCH << HSA_PACKET_HEADER_TYPE) |
                      (barrier ? (1 << HSA_PACKET_HEADER_BARRIER) : 0) |
                      (HSA_FENCE_SCOPE_SYSTEM << HSA_PACKET_HEADER_SCACQUIRE_FENCE_SCOPE) |
                      (HSA_FENCE_SCOPE_SYSTEM << HSA_PACKET_HEADER_SCRELEASE_FENCE_SCOPE);
    // Publishes AQL packet header via atomic store directly to the hardware queue.
    __atomic_store_n(reinterpret_cast<uint16_t*>(packet), header | (packet->setup << 16), __ATOMIC_RELEASE);
    return index;
}

Hybrid CPU+GPU physics and device composition

DeviceComposer: splitting work across heterogeneous devices

DeviceComposer steps heterogeneous backends in parallel across a unified ball array with dynamic rebalancing.

Source: source/core/sim/DeviceComposer.cpp, data/docs/hybrid-physics.md

source/core/sim/DeviceComposer.cpp lines 441-466
// A part that is itself a composer (VulkanMulti) may move balls between its devices inside its step; that step
// says nothing about its speed, and timing it would shift balls away from it, so it goes unmeasured.
// Coordinates heterogeneous backends over unified ball indices; all devices step their share (commit d51d0515e).
const auto stepPart = [&params](Part& part) {
    const auto* nested = dynamic_cast<const DeviceComposer*>(part.backend.get());
    const size_t movesBefore = nested ? nested->movingStepCount() : 0;
    const Clock::time_point start = Clock::now();
    part.backend->step(params, part.hits);
    part.lastMs = nested && nested->movingStepCount() != movesBefore ? 0.0 : millisecondsSince(start);
};
// Rebalance migrates balls between parts in 8-byte records toward proportional throughput shares.
std::vector<std::future<void>> helpers;
helpers.reserve(parts.size());
for (size_t index = 0; index < parts.size(); ++index) {
    Part& part = parts[index];
    part.hits.clear();
    part.steppedBalls = part.backend->ballCount();
    part.lastMs = 0.0;
    // Steps secondary devices concurrently on background threads while the lead steps on the caller.
    if (part.steppedBalls > 0 && index > 0) {
        helpers.push_back(std::async(std::launch::async, stepPart, std::ref(part)));
    }
}
if (parts.front().steppedBalls > 0) {
    stepPart(parts.front());
}
for (std::future<void>& helper : helpers) {
    helper.get();
}

Proportional brick budgets across several GPUs

DeviceComposer partitions brick hits into proportional quotas so multiple GPUs resolve collisions locally.

Source: source/core/sim/DeviceComposer.cpp, data/docs/codebase.html

source/core/sim/DeviceComposer.cpp lines 317-372
// Splits a brick's remaining hits between the device-resolving parts in proportion to their shares of the balls,
// so their budgets add up to exactly what the brick has. The units left after rounding go to a part drawn by hash,
// so on average every part gets its proportional chance at the last hit of a brick. Every brick is drawn again each
// tick: a brick's last hit drawn to a part whose balls never reach it would otherwise stay there and keep the brick
// alive. Only parts whose budget changed are sent it: the others' device copies already match.
// Divides brick hit points into per-device quotas weighted by ball share (commit f982dbc57).
void DeviceComposer::splitBrickBudgets() {
    std::vector<double> weights(deviceParts);
    double totalWeight = 0.0;
    // Uploads brick hit budgets only to devices whose quotas changed, enabling local resolution.
    for (size_t index = 0; index < deviceParts; ++index) {
        weights[index] = parts[index].backend->ballCount() > 0 ? parts[index].share : 0.0;
        totalWeight += weights[index];
    }
    if (totalWeight <= 0.0) {
        std::ranges::fill(weights, 1.0);
        totalWeight = static_cast<double>(deviceParts);
    }
    std::vector<bool> changed(deviceParts, false);
    for (size_t index = 0; index < deviceParts; ++index) {
        if (parts[index].budget.size() != brickHits.size()) {
            parts[index].budget.assign(brickHits.size(), 0);
            changed[index] = true;
        }
    }
    ++budgetEpoch;
    std::vector<int32_t> split(deviceParts);
    for (size_t brick = 0; brick < brickHits.size(); ++brick) {
        const int32_t hits = std::max(brickHits[brick], 0);
        int32_t assigned = 0;
        for (size_t index = 0; index < deviceParts; ++index) {
            split[index] = static_cast<int32_t>(static_cast<double>(hits) * weights[index] / totalWeight);
            assigned += split[index];
        }
        // Distributes fractional hits deterministically using mixHash on brick ID and epoch.
        for (int32_t unit = 0; assigned < hits; ++unit, ++assigned) {
            const uint32_t draw = mixHash(static_cast<uint32_t>(brick) ^ mixHash(budgetEpoch + static_cast<uint32_t>(unit) * 0x51ed27u));
            double pick = static_cast<double>(draw) / slotSelectionRange * totalWeight;
            size_t chosen = deviceParts - 1;
            for (size_t index = 0; index < deviceParts; ++index) {
                if (pick < weights[index]) {
                    chosen = index;
                    break;
                }
                pick -= weights[index];
            }
            ++split[chosen];
        }
        for (size_t index = 0; index < deviceParts; ++index) {
            changed[index] = changed[index] || parts[index].budget[brick] != split[index];
            parts[index].budget[brick] = split[index];
        }
    }
    for (size_t index = 0; index < deviceParts; ++index) {
        if (changed[index]) {
            parts[index].backend->uploadBrickHits(parts[index].budget);
        }
    }
}

Hiding the fixed CUDA+AVX2 pair while Hybrid is the default

The legacy CUDA+AVX2 pair is hidden from UI menus in favor of generalized multi-device composition.

Source: source/core/sim/BackendWorker.cpp, source/core/sim/BackendSelection.cpp, data/docs/physics-backends.md

// Filters out legacy CudaAvx2 so UI exposes generalized multi-device composition instead.
std::vector<BackendKind> selectableBackends() {
    bool otherBackendAvailable = false;
    for (const BackendKind kind : allBackendKinds) {
        if (kind != BackendKind::Scalar && backendStatus(kind).available) {
            otherBackendAvailable = true;
            break;
        }
    }
    std::vector<BackendKind> kinds;
    for (const BackendKind kind : allBackendKinds) {
        if (kind != BackendKind::CudaAvx2 && (kind != BackendKind::Scalar || !otherBackendAvailable)) {
            kinds.push_back(kind);
        }
    }
    return kinds;
}

// Maps legacy CudaAvx2 to Hybrid for backwards compatibility with existing configs and CLI flags.
BackendKind offeredBackend(BackendKind kind) { return kind == BackendKind::CudaAvx2 ? BackendKind::Hybrid : kind; }

bool isBackendSelectable(BackendKind kind) {
    const std::vector<BackendKind> kinds = selectableBackends();
    return std::find(kinds.begin(), kinds.end(), kind) != kinds.end();
}

HybridBackend: rebalancing ball counts without frame drops

Capping ball migrations per tick keeps dynamic CPU-GPU rebalancing within the 16.6 ms frame budget.

Source: source/core/sim/HybridBackend.cpp, data/docs/hybrid-physics.md

source/core/sim/HybridBackend.cpp lines 93-114
// CPU share is smoothed dynamically between 8% and 50%; CPU receives zero balls below 262,144 balls.
void HybridBackend::rebalance() {
    const size_t total = ballCount();
    const size_t current = cpu->ballCount();
    const size_t target = targetCpuCount(total);
    const size_t difference = current > target ? current - target : target - current;
    // Rebalances only if ball count deviates past tolerance (2% or 131,072 balls).
    const size_t tolerance =
        std::max(minimumRebalanceBalls, static_cast<size_t>(static_cast<double>(target) * rebalanceTolerance));
    if (difference <= tolerance && !(target == 0 && current > 0)) {
        return;
    }
    // Moving 4M balls took ~30 ms; capping moves at 1M keeps frame transfers within the 16.6 ms budget.
    const size_t moved = std::min(difference, maximumBallsMovedPerStep);
    PhysicsBackend& source = current < target ? *gpu : *cpu;
    PhysicsBackend& destination = current < target ? *cpu : *gpu;
    if (source.takeCompactTail(moved, movedBalls)) {
        if (!destination.addCompact(movedBalls)) {
            source.addCompact(movedBalls);
        }
        return;
    }
    const std::vector<BallSpawn> balls = source.takeTail(moved);
    destination.addBalls(balls);
}

CPU optimisations that let AVX2 assist the GPU

Compact 8-byte layouts and sampled grid bidding doubled AVX2 throughput so CPU simulation speeds up hybrid runs.

Source: data/docs/hybrid-physics.md, source/backends/cpu/Avx2Backend.cpp

data/docs/hybrid-physics.md lines 37-48
# Three optimizations made CPU assistance faster than CUDA alone (commit 4651f393e).
### What made the CPU side worth adding

The first hybrid was slower than CUDA alone. Three changes fixed it:

| Change | AVX2 throughput (2 substeps) |
|---|---|
# Compact 8-byte layout boosted AVX2 throughput from 650 M to 1080 M (bricks) and 1250 M (empty).
| Original: seven float/int arrays, 28 bytes read and 24 written per ball | 650 M ball-steps/s |
| Compact 8-byte ball (CUDA's encoding), state written only when it changes, near-brick skip | 1080 M (bricks), 1250 M (empty board) |
# Sampled bidding stopped ~45 KB grid cache bouncing across 11 threads, boosting rate from 610 M to 1200 M.
| Sampled visibility-grid bids (8 per slot) instead of every ball writing the shared grid | in-game 610 M -> 1200 M |

The last one matters only in the game: eleven threads storing every ball into the same ~45 KB grid made its
cache lines bounce between cores and halved CPU throughput.

GPU hardware limits, profiling, and failure recovery

Hardware bottlenecks of the CUDA step kernel on the GTX 1650

The CUDA step kernel is memory-bandwidth bound on empty boards and throttled by board power limits under collision load.

Source: data/docs/cuda-limits.md

data/docs/cuda-limits.md lines 112-124
# Empty board streams 153.8 GB/s (93.7% of 165.9 GB/s D2D copy limit), making it memory-bandwidth bound.
| `nvidia-smi` every 20 ms during a sustained brick-level run | **SW Power Cap active in 100% of busy samples**, 81.6 W average against the 82 W limit (peak 85.5 W), SM clock 1733 MHz average (1635 min) instead of 1920 MHz |
| empty board, same sampling | 43 W, 1920 MHz, no cap |
| compute-heavy control: empty board + 256 injected instructions | 11.24 ms plain vs 9.32 ms isolated (Nsight: 91% issue active) |

# Thermal throttling under power caps slowed the same build from 10.0 ms to 14.8 ms across 400 ticks.
In isolation, the brick-level step is bound by DRAM bandwidth, like the empty board. Under sustained load, every
compute-heavy CUDA kernel on this card runs into the **GTX 1650's 75-82 W board power limit**. The driver then
lowers the SM clock until the power fits, so the same instruction stream takes 30-40% longer. How much depends on the
chip's temperature (leakage power): the same build measured 10.0 ms and 14.8 ms for 400 brick-level ticks at
different points of a long session. The memory-bound empty board draws half the budget and never caps.

# Spatial sorting cut warp instructions from 850M to 403M, reducing power throttling.
Both limits are physical: DRAM bandwidth when power is available, board power under sustained compute. On a power-
capped card, fewer instructions per ball mean less energy per ball. That is why the cell sort, which cut a tick from
850M to 403M warp-instructions, also helps under the cap.

Proving the instruction-issue bottleneck without CUPTI admin access

Instruction injection benchmarks measure execution slack without CUPTI admin rights to prove issue saturation.

Source: data/docs/cuda-limits.md

data/docs/cuda-limits.md lines 28-45
# Instruction injection measures issue headroom without requiring Windows admin CUPTI access.
GPU performance counters need administrator rights on Windows (CUPTI stops reporting once the CUDA context is
created), so the issue limit is measured with an **instruction-injection test** (`scripts/bench/issue_slack.py`)
instead. It adds K independent, non-foldable ALU instructions per thread (alternating IMAD and PRMT, so both math
pipes stay at one issue per clock) and compares the extra kernel time with what K full issue slots cost:
# Issue capacity is 107.5 G warp-instructions/s (14 SMs x 4 schedulers x 1.92 GHz).
`K x warps / 107.5 G/s` = 0.0174 ms per instruction for 1.875M warps.

- If the schedulers have idle cycles (the kernel waits on memory or latency), the injected instructions fill them
  and cost less than a full slot.
- If every issue cycle is already used, each injected instruction costs a full slot.

# Injected instructions cost 100% on brick levels vs 59-74% on empty boards, confirming issue saturation.
| board | K = 32 | K = 64 | K = 128 |
|---|---|---|---|
| brick level | 144% | 92% | 93% |
| empty board | 59% | 72% | 74% |

The brick level has no spare issue cycles: every added instruction costs a full issue slot from the first one, so
the SMs' instruction issue rate is the limit. The empty board, which is memory-bound, shows the expected slack,
which confirms the test can tell the two cases apart.

Detecting and recovering from device loss and driver crashes

GPU driver errors and device losses trigger automatic runtime migration of active balls to CPU simulation.

Source: source/backends/cuda/CudaBackend.cpp, source/core/game/GameSession.cpp, source/core/sim/Simulation.cpp

source/core/game/GameSession.cpp lines 447-454
// Driver errors set fault state to no-op GPU dispatches; OOM is treated as a cap (commit 0b631930a).
void GameSession::recoverFromBackendFault() {
    const std::string_view fault = physicsSimulation.backend().faultMessage();
    if (fault.empty()) {
        return;
    }
    backendFault = std::string(fault);
    // Migrates balls from faulted GPU backend to fastest available CPU backend without dropping state.
    switchBackendNow(fastestAvailableCpuBackend());
}

Testing multi-device hit resolution and budget splitting without GPUs

FakeDeviceBackend simulates GPU hit resolution and memory compaction for headless CI tests without real GPUs.

Source: source/tests/ComposerTests.cpp, source/core/sim/PhysicsAccuracy.cpp

source/tests/ComposerTests.cpp lines 31-49
// FakeDeviceBackend wraps ScalarBackend with device-side budget tracking for headless CI testing.
void step(const StepParams& params, std::vector<BallHit>&) override {
    innerHits.clear();
    inner->step(params, innerHits);
    hitBricks.clear();
    dead.clear();
    tally = {};
    const std::vector<BallSpawn> balls = innerHits.empty() ? std::vector<BallSpawn>{} : inner->exportBalls();
    for (const BallHit& hit : innerHits) {
        int32_t& left = hitsLeft[static_cast<size_t>(hit.brick)];
        if (left > 0 && std::ranges::find(dead, hit.ball) == dead.end()) {
            --left;
            hitBricks.push_back(static_cast<uint32_t>(hit.brick));
            dead.push_back(hit.ball);
            ++tally[static_cast<size_t>(balls[hit.ball].color)];
        }
    }
    std::ranges::sort(dead, std::greater<>{});
    // Simulates device-side compaction and verifies multi-device hit budget conservation.
    inner->removeBalls(dead);
}

Key facts

  • balls_ptxgen compiles CUDA kernels with NVRTC at build time into native SASS cubins (sm_50 through sm_89) and PTX (compute_75, compute_50).
  • At runtime, the engine uses the CUDA Driver API (nvcuda.dll / libcuda.so.1) and needs no CUDA Toolkit or runtime DLLs.
  • stepBallsKernel steps 2 balls per thread (ballsPerStepThread = 2) to keep twice as many memory requests in flight.
  • consumeBrickHit deduplicates brick hits across warp lanes using __match_any_sync, issuing one leader atomicSub and refunding excess.
  • Spatial cell sorting (countCellsKernel, scanCellsKernel, stepAndSortBallsKernel) cut brick-level step time from 16.8 ms to 10.2 ms (-39%) on 60M balls.
  • SortSchedule trials intervals of 8, 16, and 32 ticks, re-evaluating every 1200 ticks and picking unsorted execution on empty boards.
  • Ball compaction is executed on-GPU via collectHolesKernel and fillHolesKernel, making removeBalls a no-op on CUDA.
  • Per-step kill buffer is clamped to 4,194,304 (1u << 22); overflow refunds hits via atomicAdd(&brickHitsLeft[brick], 1).
  • GrowableDeviceArray uses cuMemAddressReserve, cuMemCreate, and cuMemMap to expand GPU buffers in 64 MB granules without copying balls.
  • The 64-bit atomic visibility grid (atomicMax with hash priority in high bits, packed coordinates in low bits) bounds readback to 16 KB regardless of ball count.
  • ROCm runs directly on the HSA runtime (libhsa-runtime64.so.1) with AQL queue dispatches and OpenCL C kernels, beating Vulkan by 13-14% on RX 6700 XT.
  • DeviceComposer partitions brick hit quotas across GPUs via splitBrickBudgets (which exist only when two or more device-resolving parts run), resolving remainder fractions with mixHash; the composer no longer parks picked parts.
  • On GPU fault or device lost, GameSession::recoverFromBackendFault hot-swaps live simulation to the fastest CPU backend (Zen4 or AVX2).
  • GTX 1650 empty-board throughput reaches 153.8 GB/s (93.7% of measured copy bandwidth), while sustained brick load hits the 75-82 W board power limit.

void App::handleEvent(const SDL_Event& event) {
    ImGui_ImplSDL3_ProcessEvent(&event);
    // GameplayScreen::handleLauncherClick translates board-space mouse clicks to launcher indices.
    input::handleEvent(event);
    if (event.type == SDL_EVENT_QUIT || event.type == SDL_EVENT_WINDOW_CLOSE_REQUESTED) {
        running = false;
    } else if (event.type == SDL_EVENT_DROP_BEGIN) {
        droppedPaths.clear();
    } else if (event.type == SDL_EVENT_DROP_FILE && event.drop.data) {
        droppedPaths.push_back(pathFromUtf8(event.drop.data));
    } else if (event.type == SDL_EVENT_DROP_COMPLETE && !droppedPaths.empty()) {
        deliverImport(std::move(droppedPaths));
        droppedPaths.clear();
    }
}
